Conventions used throughout:

- `Pn-Cm` = commit `m` of Phase `n`. Every commit must pass **the gate** (§1.1). Each `Pn-Cm` is one git commit; the phase-level commit message in §3 is the message of the phase's exit/tag commit.
- `file:line` citations refer to the flat sketch at `b53cfe4` until the `src/` move (P2-C8); after the move the same line numbers apply to the moved files (a pure move changes no lines).
- "audit §N" = section of `PEBBLEBOL_IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT.md`; "spec §N" or "§N" = the product spec.

## 0. Decisions

### 0.1 Decisions taken by this plan (owner may override; each is one commit to reverse)

| # | Decision | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | **Base approach: always-buildable strangler-fig.** Every commit compiles at 0 project warnings and keeps `make -C tests check` green; removals happen one obsolete subsystem per commit in the flat layout before the `src/` move so the audit's `file:line` surgery lists stay valid; `ui.cpp` is migrated one screen per commit with fall-through to the old switches. | §51 "keep the repository buildable", §68 r19; audit §17 line lists are only valid in the flat layout. |
| T2 | **Reuse over rewrite (§68 r20).** File names and `nt_`/`sim_`/`rd_`/`gt_`/`net_`/`store_` prefixes are kept; files are `git mv`'d into `src/…`; `render.cpp` stays one TU (it is both §5 `hardware/Display` and `ui/Renderer`) and lives in `src/ui/`; `sim.cpp` stays single-instance because spec §27 says "Active Pebble only" (stored Pebbles use a stateless `box_recover()`); the 16 B `Genome` (nt_types.h:309-330) is embedded in `PebbleInstance` so `genome_breed()` (genome.cpp:420-511, `genome.h:85`) is reused whole; the milli-point integrator keeps `int32 care[5]` + `int16 care_rem[5]` exactly as `accum_stat()` needs them (sim.cpp:179-197, `STAT_MILLI_MAX 100000` config.h:232). | audit §16 reuse table; judges' verdict that u8 care stats break `accum_stat`. |
| T3 | **ENERGY is kept as a fifth, mostly internal care stat.** The sleep machine reads `p.stat[ST_ENERGY]`/`pct_milli(ST_ENERGY)` (sim.cpp:764-765) and the regen rule keys off it (sim.cpp:920-935). The four §27 stats are the displayed ones. | audit §16 "Sleep machine, health regen rule" = reuse. |
| T4 | **Content = generated `constexpr` tables from committed JSON** (`tools/content/*.json` → `src/data/*_table.h`), never parsed at runtime; the same generator emits the creator `/api/schema` JSON constants so device and page cannot disagree. `PROGMEM` is a no-op on ESP32; `constexpr` already lands in `.flash.rodata` (sprites.h:9-10 pattern). | §32 "follow the existing project and ESP32 memory constraints" (spec example shape is JSON). |
| T5 | **Persistence = versioned blobs, one NVS key pair per blob** (`<key>0`/`<key>1`, header `seq`), loader picks the highest valid `seq`, writer overwrites the inactive copy and verifies bytes written (storage.cpp:568-574 pattern); plus a **second NVS partition `nvs2`** (custom `partitions.csv`, honoured by core 3.1.1 `platform.txt:113`) holding a daily/level-up checkpoint of Box+active, because `initArduino()` erases the whole default `nvs` partition on `ESP_ERR_NVS_NO_FREE_PAGES`/`NEW_VERSION_FOUND` before `setup()` (core `esp32-hal-misc.c:287-300`). | §48 "at least one previous valid save", §31 "must survive firmware restart"; audit §5 core hazard. |
| T6 | **Radio: OFF is the resting state; exactly one stack resident** (`RadioMode`, nt_types.h:139-144). Wi-Fi is requested only by SCAN (NETWORK), LINK and CREATOR states and released on exit. SNTP is deleted; the clock is calibrated from the phone (`POST /api/time`) or the on-device time screen. | §40, §68 r5-r6; audit risks 2 and 11. |
| T7 | **Local protocol proven on host first**: codec + session + lockstep battle protocol run over an in-process loopback with fault injection (drop/dup/reorder) in Phase 4; the device transport (D2) arrives in Phase 7 behind the §59 `Transport` seam. Battle over link is **lockstep with a per-round state hash**; a mismatch ends the battle with `DESYNC` and no rewards — neither side trusts the other (§68 r12). | audit §7.3; §15, §59. |
| T8 | **Input**: the double-tap gesture is retired (tap latency 305 ms → ~25 ms, input.cpp:283-286); button sampling moves to a 5 ms `esp_timer` sampler feeding a small ring so presses during blocking peripheral init are not lost (input is loop-polled today, input.cpp:42). §7 grammar: A = TAP_L (confirm/next), B = TAP_R (back/cancel); `HOLD_R = back` (ui.cpp:3389-3395) retired. | §7, §45 "use hardware timers", §46 "responsive even while peripheral operations are initialized"; audit risk 10. |
| T9 | **Unknown clock charges zero absence** (drops the 6 h `ABSENCE_LARGA_S` floor, sim.cpp:1920-1930, config.h:428) and encounter cooldowns fall back to a per-boot RAM cooldown while `CAL_UNSET`. | §27 "never require attention", §21 "avoid resetting when battery dies". |
| T10 | **`rd_fatal()` (noreturn, render.cpp:1034-1074) is replaced by the `ERROR` state in Phase 2**, not Phase 10. | §47 "must never get stuck permanently"; audit risk 15. |
| T11 | **Sketch folder renamed `sketch_aug30b/` → `Pebblebol/`** and `.ino` → `Pebblebol.ino` in P2-C1 (folder must equal `.ino` basename, README.md:131-134). Owner may keep the old name (decision D3 below); nothing else depends on it. | audit risk 17. |
| T12 | **UI strings stay Spanish in `strings_es.h`**; identifiers, comments and docs become English (Spanish config.h:15-76 block translated in P2-C8; Spanish README/CHANGELOG/ESTUDIO_VISUAL archived under `docs/legacy/` in P10-C5). | Language rule in the task context; spec allows. |
| T13 | **Species/attack/item roster sprites are 24x24, 2 frames, XBM (72 B/frame)**; 60 species = 8,640 B, fits the atlas after the 38 Nottamagochi sets are removed (`SPRITE_DATA_BYTES` assert sprites.h:1259-1260; audit §19.3). Custom creator sprites use the same format. | audit §19.3; §37 one pipeline. |

Decisions live in `docs/decisions.md` (created P1-C1); each closed decision is one commit that also updates that file. **D1 is deferred by the owner** (keep the committed pin values) and **D2 is closed in favour of ESP-NOW** — see that file for the consequences each one carries into later phases.

## 1. Target architecture

### 1.1 The gate every commit must pass

`tools/check.sh` (created P2-C1, run before every commit; CI from P1-C1 for tests, P2-C1 for the firmware build):

1. `arduino-cli compile --fqbn esp32:esp32:esp32c3:PartitionScheme=huge_app,CDCOnBoot=cdc --warnings all /home/user/Pebblebol/Pebblebol` exits 0 **and** the log contains zero `warning:` lines pointing into the sketch (core/library warnings ignored, as the baseline does). The FQBN **keeps** `PartitionScheme=huge_app`. P2-C9d measured why: arduino-cli does copy the sketch's `partitions.csv` into the build and esptool flashes that table, so `nvs2` is genuinely in force, but `upload.maximum_size` comes from the board menu rather than the CSV. Dropping the option lowered the size ceiling to 1,310,720 B and failed the 1.87 MB build. Two gates pin the real behaviour instead: the app maximum must read 3,145,728 B, and the table about to be flashed must contain `nvs2`. Recorded in `docs/decisions.md` D6.
2. `make -C tests check` exits 0 (`g++ -std=c++17 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`, no external framework).
3. The "Sketch uses" / "Global variables use" lines are parsed and compared with `GATE_FLASH_MAX` / `GATE_GLOBALS_MAX` (config.h:642-643 — referenced nowhere today, audit §14); the gate makes them real.
4. Grep gates (added as the corresponding commits land): `grep -rE "s_screen\s*=" src/` == 0 outside `app/state_machine.cpp` (P2-C11 exit, **tree-wide since P2-C11d**: the exception this gate used to carry - `dev/godmode.cpp`'s unrelated `static uint8_t s_screen` for its console sub-screen - was removed by renaming that variable `s_console_page`); `grep -c "nullptr," src/ui/screen_table.cpp` == 0 (P2-C11d: every §6 state has a row with all five hooks); `grep -c "WiFi.begin" src/` == 0 (P5-C1); `grep -rn "esp_random" src/` == 0 on non-comment lines outside `app/app.cpp` (P2-C2/P3-C4 — **rewritten by the P3-C5 follow-up, which also added it to `check.sh`, where it had never been**. As first written — `grep -c "genome_rand\|esp_random" src/game src/minigames` == 0 — it could never have passed: `genome_rand` is *defined and used* in `src/game/genome.cpp`, 6 hits. The invariant meant is `core/rng.h:12-14`, "on the device `esp_random()` is called EXACTLY ONCE, in app.cpp … nothing else in the tree may call it", and that one does hold: every other hit in `src/` is a comment); `grep -c "rd_u8g2()" src/app src/minigames src/game` == 0 (P10-C4).

`tools/build_matrix.sh` (P2-C1, run at every phase tag): baseline, `FEATURE_BLE=0`, `FEATURE_WEB=0`, `GOD_MODE_ENABLED=0`, all-off, release. Warning-free in every variant from P2-C5 on (today all-off has 12 unused-function warnings).

### 1.2 Directory tree

arduino-cli compiles `<sketch>/src/**` recursively; every `#include` inside `src/` is relative to the including file (`"../core/config.h"`), so neither toolchain needs an extra `-I`. **Probe this first** (P2-C8 skeleton compile) before moving anything.

  .github/workflows/ci.yml          make -C tests check (P1-C1); + arduino-cli compile with cached core (P2-C1)

  tools/

The 16 existing modules map onto this tree without rewriting (audit §1.2 table); `render.cpp` and `sim.cpp` are deliberately **not** split (T2).

### 1.3 Layer rules

1. **Pure layers** — `src/core/{crc16,rng}.cpp`, `src/game/**`, `src/persistence/{save_manager,migration}.cpp`, `src/networking/{protocol,session,battle_link,trade_link,breed_link}.cpp`, `src/minigames/games/*_logic.cpp` — include only `<stdint.h>`, `<string.h>`, `core/config.h`, `core/nt_types.h`, `core/platform.h`. They never include `Arduino.h`. The host Makefile compiles them directly with `-Werror` and no Arduino include path (§68 r8, r10).
2. **Single owners** (already true today, audit exec summary item 4; preserved by every move): one radio owner `net.cpp`, one U8G2 owner `render.cpp`, one NVS owner `hardware/kv_nvs.cpp`, one libc-time owner `gametime.cpp`, one `esp_random()` caller `app.cpp` (seeds `rng_seed_all`).
3. **Rendering reads views, not models**: `petfx`/`actfx`/screens read `PetView` (pet_view.h), never `sim.h`/`genome.h` (audit risk 9: petfx.cpp:26-28, :580, :764-770, :894; actfx.cpp:588).
4. **State machine is the only navigation**: `ui_goto()`/`sm_goto()` is the sole funnel; `ScreenDef{enter,update,render,input,leave}` per §6 state; the three bypasses (ui.cpp:2460, :2729, :3463) are removed; grep gate in §1.1.
5. **Persisted or transmitted layouts are never edited in place**: bump `SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION`/`PROTOCOL_VERSION`, add a migration or a reject path, add a test (§31, §60).
6. **Radio is screen-owned**: `net_request(RADIO_WIFI)` only from NETWORK (scan), LINK, CREATOR `enter()`; `net_request(RADIO_OFF)` in their `leave()` (§40, §68 r6). No `WiFi.begin()` anywhere except the creator softAP path (§68 r5).
7. **No blocking in `loop()`**: no `delay()` > 1 ms, no synchronous radio waits; long work (catch-up, scans, server) is phased and pumped. A single `loop()` iteration must stay < 1 s (Task WDT is 5 s, `CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_TIMEOUT_S`, audit §2).
8. **Drawing goes through `gfx.h`/`rd_*`** so screens, minigames and the battle renderer render into the host framebuffer fake for §63 snapshot tests (P2-C11 onward).

### 1.4 Module contracts (header + key functions)

C-style free functions (existing `sim_*/store_*/rd_*` convention) so moves are mechanical and host tests need no C++ runtime features. Pure unless marked (device).

|---|---|---|---|

`PROTO_FRAME_MAX` 164 and `static_assert(PROTO_FRAME_MAX <= 250)` against the ESP-NOW datagram. **Errors: 13 named codes (`PE_SHORT`..`PE_TEAM_PAD`), not five.** **Types: exactly the twelve §15 messages.** `DISCOVERY_BEACON`, `TRADE_*`, `BREED_*`, `TIME_SYNC` and `PING` are **NOT** in the set - §15's own list is the twelve that shipped, so they are P7's protocols and not a §15 shortfall; this row promised them and that is why it read as one. **`Transport` is `{ctx, send, recv, mtu}`** - datagram and poll-driven, no `begin`/`end`/`beacon`/`service`/`PeerAddr`: addressing belongs to the transport, not to the seam. **`session_step`/`session_abort` do not exist**; the FSM is `session_poll` / `session_cancel` / `session_close` over nine states, with a **9 x 1000 ms** ladder (not 3 s x 3) and a sequence window that bounds the FIRST sequence number as well as the jumps after it. **`transport_loopback()` is TEST-ONLY** - nothing on the device declares a `LoopbackLink`, and the "on-device self-test" this row promised was never built. `transport_espnow()` and `transport_ble()` are P7-C1 behind the same seam (D2 chose ESP-NOW). | `TEAM_SUBMIT` = 3 x **48 B** wire instance, and **the nickname is deliberately NOT transmitted** - a peer has no business learning what you call your Pebble, and the 16 bytes it would cost are the difference between 48 and 64 | P4-C5 |

### 1.5 Data formats

#### 1.5.1 `PebbleInstance` — 128 B, packed, little-endian, CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE over bytes 0..125

Same size as today's `PetSave` (nt_types.h:420-479) so the NVS arithmetic is known; 12 reserved bytes avoid a schema bump for small additions (§60).

```text
off size field                notes
  0   2  magic                0x4250 'PB'
  2   1  layout_ver           PEBBLE_LAYOUT_VER = 1 (SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION lives in BoxHeader)
  3   1  species_id           0 = empty slot; 1..199 built-in roster; 200..209 custom (cs0..cs9)
  4   4  id                   0 = empty; hash32(device_id, next_id_counter); unique in the Box (§9 "no duplication")
  8   4  creation_seed        individual variation source (§10); nothing derived from it is stored
 12   4  birth_epoch          0 when the clock was CAL_UNSET at creation
 16   4  last_updated_epoch   §10 lastUpdated; drives box_recover() and care catch-up
 20   4  age_s                accumulated by care ticks, clock-jump proof (PetSave.age_s semantics)
 24   2  xp                   XP inside the current level (u16; XP_TABLE max < 65535, static_assert)
 26   1  level                1..30 (§11)
 27   1  evo_state            bits1:0 stage-in-family 0..2; bit7 EVOLVE_PENDING
 28  20  care[5]              int32 milli-points 0..100000: HUNGER, HAPPINESS, HEALTH, CLEANLINESS, ENERGY (T3)
 48  10  care_rem[5]          int16 exact remainders (|r| < 3600) — the existing integrator (sim.cpp:179-197)
 58   2  hp_cur               current battle HP; maxHp derived
 60   1  status               bit0 SICK bit1 ASLEEP bit2 CORRUPTED (§55) bit3 FAINTED bit4 RESERVED (was LIGHT_ON, retired P3-C2b — never reused)
 61   1  flags                bit0 CUSTOM bit1 TRADED bit2 BRED bit3 GOD_TAINTED bit4 RARE bit5 HAS_CUSTOM_SPRITE
 62   4  moves[4]             AttackId, 0 = empty; validator requires exactly 4 legal (§13, §35)
 66   1  origin               STARTER / WILD / BRED / TRADED / CREATOR
 67   1  trait_id             0 = none (§56, optional)
 68   2  battles_won
 70   2  battles_lost
 72   2  minigames_won
 74   1  evolutions
 75   1  trades
 76   4  lifetime_active_s    seconds spent as the active Pebble (activity input, §25)
 80  16  genome               existing 16 B Genome (nt_types.h:309-330): lineage_id, generation, parent_tag, g0..g2
 96  13  nickname[13]         NAME_MAX_LEN 12 + NUL (config.h:644)
109   1  custom_sprite        0xFF none; else cs slot index
110   4  seq                  pair sequence number (T5); loader picks the highest valid seq of <key>0/<key>1
114  12  reserved[12]         must be 0
126   2  crc16                crc16_ccitt over bytes 0..125
```

`static_assert(sizeof(PebbleInstance) == 128)` + `offsetof` guards on `species_id`, `care`, `care_rem`, `moves`, `genome`, `nickname`, `seq`, `crc16`. Derived (never stored, §10): `hp_max = 10 + 2*base_hp + level`; `atk/def/spd = base + level/3 + genome variation (0..2)`; `type` = `SpeciesDef.type` (custom species carry their own type in `cs*`). All integer (no FPU, audit §2).

#### 1.5.2 Content tables (`src/data/*_table.h`, generated, `inline constexpr`, flash-resident)

```cpp
struct SpeciesDef {                 // 24 B, 60+ rows ≈ 1.5 KB flash
  uint8_t  id;                      // 1..199, contiguous == index+1 (static_assert)
  uint8_t  family;                  // 1..20+
  uint8_t  stage;                   // 0 base, 1 mid, 2 final (§19)
  uint8_t  type;                    // PebbleType: TYPE_SIGNAL / TYPE_CORRUPT / TYPE_SYSTEM (§12)
  uint8_t  base_hp, base_atk, base_def, base_spd;   // 1..10 (§11)
  uint8_t  moves[4];                // learnset (validator: moves must be in this list or the family pool)
  uint8_t  evo_rule;                // index into EVOLUTION_RULES[], 0xFF = none (§18)
  uint8_t  rarity;                  // 0 common, 1 uncommon, 2 rare, 3 special (§22)
  uint8_t  spawn_weight;            // relative weight inside its rarity band (§20)
  uint8_t  compat_group;            // breeding (§17)
  uint8_t  category_mask;           // NetCategory bits it can spawn under (§20)
  uint8_t  sprite_id;               // index into SPRITE_SETS (sprites_pebbles.h), 24x24 x 2 frames (T13)
  uint16_t name_idx;                // index into SPECIES_NAMES[] (proper nouns, App. A)
  uint16_t flavor_idx;              // StrId of the Spanish flavor line (§53)
  uint8_t  reserved[2];
};
static_assert(sizeof(SpeciesDef) == 24, "SpeciesDef layout drifted");

struct AttackDef {                  // 16 B, ~30 rows
  uint8_t  id, type, category;      // category: DAMAGE / DEFENSIVE / OFF_BUFF / SPEED / PROTECT / RISK (§13)
  uint8_t  power, accuracy;         // 0..100
  int8_t   priority;
  uint8_t  effect, effect_value, effect_duration, cooldown, anim_id, budget_cost;   // budget_cost feeds §36
  uint16_t name_idx;                // §13 "name"
  uint8_t  reserved[2];
};
static_assert(sizeof(AttackDef) == 16);

struct ItemDef  { uint8_t id, klass, value, rarity; uint16_t name_idx; uint8_t reserved[2]; };   // 8 B (§24: XP candy, capture, care, battle modifier)
struct EvolutionRule { uint8_t species, target, level, cond; uint16_t cond_value; };  // 6 B (§18); cond: EVOC_NONE, EVOC_HAPPINESS_GE, EVOC_CORRUPTED, EVOC_ITEM, EVOC_ACTIVITY_GE  [SHIPPED SHAPE, P3-C3 — was 8 B with a uint8 cond_value and a BATTLES_WON_GE kind; cond_value is 16-bit so a generator must NOT truncate it, and battles-won was dropped because no rule in the content pack uses it]
struct EncounterRow  { uint8_t category, outcome, weight, rarity_min, rarity_max; uint8_t reserved[3]; }; // 8 B (§22)
inline constexpr int8_t TYPE_CHART[3][3] = { /* SIGNAL>CORRUPT>SYSTEM>SIGNAL */ };
```

Generator-emitted compile-time guards: id contiguity; every `moves[i] < ATTACK_COUNT`; every `EvolutionRule.target` is `stage+1` of the same family; every `sprite_id < SPRITE_SET_COUNT`; `sum(spawn_weight) > 0` per category; `XP_TABLE[LEVEL_MAX] < 65535`. `species_get()` also resolves `cs*` records so battle/validator code never branches on "custom".

**P4-C1 SHIPPED ALL SIX AND CORRECTED TWO OF THEM.** (a) **`moves[i] < ATTACK_COUNT` IS OFF BY ONE AS WRITTEN** and would reject the last attack in the table: attack ids are 1-based (`ATTACKS_TABLE[id - 1]`), so the true bound is `1 <= id <= ATTACK_COUNT`. `attacks_table.h::species_learnsets_resolve()` uses the correct form and says why; `test_content.cpp` asserts `attack_get(ATTACK_COUNT) != nullptr`, which is exactly the row the plan's literal form would have banned. (b) **`XP_TABLE[LEVEL_MAX] < 65535` cannot fail** — index 30 is 0 by design — so `balance.h` keeps the pair P3-C2 wrote (`xp_table_monotonic()` and `xp_table_total() < 65535`) and the plan's literal form was not "restored". **"Stronger pair" was half true and the P4-C1 review measured which half**: `xp_table_monotonic()` does the work (flat at L15, backward at L20 and a non-zero `XP_TABLE[30]` all fire it), while `xp_table_total() < 65535` is not failable by any plausible curve — the shipped one totals 8,845 and even the REJECTED 4.12× curve totals 36,453 — and the sum is not the quantity the u16 bound is about, since `PebbleInstance.xp` holds xp toward the NEXT level. It stays as a floor, and `balance.h` now says all of that where the assert is rather than calling it strong. (c) `sprite_id < SPRITE_SET_COUNT` is asserted in `game/species.cpp`, the one TU that sees both tables, because putting it in `species_table.h` pulls the 86 KB atlas into every translation unit that only wants a `base_hp`; the bound the roster size comes from is `SPR_BABY_BLOB + sprite_id`, not the field. **P4-C4a narrowed the rest of that half-sentence**, which read "the real bound is": the sum is a FORWARD bound on P10's one-body-per-species atlas and was never the resolution anything drew through — against the 38-set atlas it sends species 25..36 onto GHOST, TOMB and the pose sets. It stays (it is what caps the roster at 36); a second `static_assert` beside it now checks the resolution the firmware runs. (d) `sum(spawn_weight)` is a `uint16_t` table, not a byte: a single category's common band already sums past 255 on this roster. (e) `species_get()` still returns `nullptr` for 200..209 — the `cs*` half is P8's. Guards shipped BEYOND this list: both `sizeof` asserts, family bounds, non-zero base stats, non-zero `compat_group` and `category_mask`, `SPECIES_BASE_OF_FAMILY` resolving to every family's stage-0 row, four DISTINCT own-type-or-NEUTRAL moves with at least one damaging one, an antisymmetric `TYPE_CHART`, every item class populated, every family owing exactly two evolution rules, no non-final stage without a way out, a conditional rule with no `cond_value`, encounter weights summing to 100, NOTHING at 15 %, every WILD row resolving to a non-empty species pool, every item drop naming a real item, and every built-in row inside the stat/attack/power budgets the creator is held to.

#### 1.5.3 Save blobs (`SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION 2`; the legacy Nottamagochi blobs are version 1)

All blobs: `magic u16, version u8, …, seq u32, …, crc16 u16` over `size-2`; `static_assert`ed; stored as **pairs** `<key>0`/`<key>1` unless marked single. NVS keys ≤ 15 chars. Namespace `"pbbl"` (D3).

| Key(s) | Struct | Bytes | Written | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pb00`/`pb01` … `pb90`/`pb91` | `PebbleInstance` | 128 | active slot: every 300 s unforced or forced after actions (1 s floor, deferred never dropped — storage.cpp:549-559); stored slots: on Box mutation only | one pair per slot; the active Pebble is an **index**, not a copy (§9) |
| `box0`/`box1` | `BoxHeader` | 32 | on Box mutation | `magic 'BX' u16, schema_version u8, active_slot u8 (0xFF none), slot_mask u16, content_version u16, next_id_counter u32, saved_epoch u32, seq u32, protocol_version u8 (§31), flags u8, captures u16, battles u16, reserved[4], crc16` |
| `cfg0`/`cfg1` | `ConfigV2` | 256 | settings change | `device_id u32` (§43, generated once), `device_name[13]`, `time_cal_state u8`, `time_cal_epoch u32`, `last_known_epoch u32` (§26), `creator_pin u16`, `pin_fail_count u8`, `pin_lock_until u32`, `creator_idle_s u16` (D7), `ap_pass[17]`, `brightness u8`, `flags u16` (mute, sh1106 override, statusbar mode), `tz[40]`, `seq u32`, `reserved[…]`, `crc16`. **No Wi-Fi credentials, no Telegram, no lat/lon** (§68 r5). |
| `inv0`/`inv1` | `Inventory` | 32 | item change | 12 x `{u8 item_id, u8 count}` + header + `xp_ledger[4] u8` + `ledger_epoch u32` (anti-farm, generalised `GainSave`) + crc (§24 "no enormous inventory") |
| `cd0`/`cd1` | `CooldownTable` | 272 | after each encounter roll | header 14 B (`magic, ver, n, seq, reserved[6]`) + 32 x `{u32 net_hash, u32 until_epoch}` + crc (§21) |
| `cs0`..`cs9` (single) | `CustomSpeciesRec` | 192 | creator upload | `magic, ver, slot, name[13], type, base[4], moves[4], compat_group, budget_used u16, sprite[2][72] (24x24 XBM), reserved[17], crc`; CRC failure → instance shows a placeholder sprite and `HAS_CUSTOM_SPRITE` is cleared, never destroyed |
| `tr` (single) | `PendingTrade` | 64 | only during a trade COMMIT | two-phase journal `{phase u8, out_id u32, in PebbleInstance-wire 48 B, peer_id u32, crc}` resolved at boot (§16) |
| `gl` (single) | `GainSave` | 20 | rides every forced save (storage.cpp:607-714 wear filter) | kept as-is (anti-farm ledger, storage.h:86-95) |
| `t`, `ok` (single) | u64, u32 | 8, 4 | 60 s / boot | kept as-is (storage.cpp:715-778, :330-356) |
| **`nvs2` partition** `ck_box`, `ck_pb0..9`, `ck_cfg` (single) | same structs | 32 + 10x128 + 256 | daily, on level-up/evolution/capture/trade, on factory reset (T5) | survives `initArduino()`'s wholesale erase of `nvs`; loader uses it when `nvs` has no `box*` and no `ok` (`LOAD_RECOVERED_CKPT`, shown to the user) |
| removed v1 keys | `save`, `cfg`, `anc`, `egg` in namespace `"notta"` | — | — | read once by the migration, then erased |

NVS budget (20 KB partition ≈ 504 usable 32 B entries, audit §5): 128 B blob = 6 entries, 256 B = 10, 32 B = 3, 272 B = 11, 192 B = 8, 64 B = 4. Live worst case: `pb` pairs 10x2x6 = 120 + `box` pair 6 + `cfg` pair 20 + `inv` pair 6 + `cd` pair 22 + `cs` 80 + `tr` 4 + `gl` 2 + `t`/`ok` 2 = **262 entries (52 %)**. Idle wear ≈ active slot 288/day x 6 + `t` 1,440 ≈ 3,200 entries/day (today 4,032, audit §5). `nvs2` (64 KB) holds ≈ 1,900 entries; the checkpoint set is 6x10 + 3 + 10 = 73. Live RAM for the whole game state: 10x128 + 32 + 256 + 32 + 272 + 64 = 1,936 B (custom species loaded one at a time).

Commit order (§31): construct → `validate_pebble` → serialise → CRC → `kv_put` the inactive copy → verify bytes written → the new copy is authoritative by `seq`. Multi-blob transactions (trade, capture into a full Box) write `tr` first. NVS itself commits one key atomically, so the pair protects against firmware bugs and bit rot, the `nvs2` checkpoint against the core's wholesale erase.

#### 1.5.4 Load pipeline (§48)

`read → checksum → schema validation (magic/version/size) → migration (v1 → v2 via legacy_v1.h) → runtime validation (validate_pebble on every slot; header/slot disagreement: slot magic is truth) → result`. `LOAD_CORRUPT` → `SCR_ERROR` "SAVE ERROR / A: Recover (checkpoint from nvs2) / B: Factory reset (two confirms)"; `LOAD_FOREIGN_NEWER` → `SCR_ERROR` "Save from newer firmware — A: Power off / B: Factory reset (two confirms)"; never auto-wipe (replaces sketch_aug30b.ino:279-283).

### 1.6 RNG service (§30)

- One xorshift32 core (genome.cpp:28-45, seed 0 remapped) behind `core/rng.h`; named streams `RNG_CARE, RNG_ENCOUNTER, RNG_BATTLE, RNG_BREEDING, RNG_MINIGAME, RNG_LOOT, RNG_MISC`, each seeded independently by `rng_seed_all(boot_seed)` (`boot_seed = esp_random()` once in `app_setup()`; tests pass constants).
- Per-session streams (`struct Rng`) for a battle (seed = `hash(idA, idB, nonceA, nonceB)` agreed in SESSION_ACCEPT, so both peers run identical resolution — lockstep) and for each minigame run (seed from the manager) so scripted-input tests are reproducible.
- Grep gate: no `esp_random()`/`random()`/`rand()` outside `app.cpp`; no `genome_rand()` outside `genome.cpp`.

### 1.7 Time model (§26)

- Source of truth: RTC-timer-backed `time()` (`CONFIG_ESP_TIME_FUNCS_USE_RTC_TIMER=y`, survives soft reset and deep sleep, drifts without a 32 kHz crystal; audit §2). Uptime (`esp_timer_get_time()`, gametime.cpp:129, :297-298) is used only for `millis()`-class intervals.
- Calibration state `TimeCal { CAL_UNSET, CAL_ESTIMATED, CAL_USER, CAL_PHONE }` persisted in `ConfigV2.time_cal_state` with `time_cal_epoch`; `last_known_epoch` persisted at the existing 60 s `t` cadence (storage.cpp:715-778).
- Setters: `POST /api/time` (CAL_PHONE) and the on-device time screen (CAL_USER). Rollback guard: a set that moves the clock backwards by more than 5 min is refused unless `src == CAL_USER` and confirmed; `epoch < NT_EPOCH_SANE_MIN` refused.
- Absence on boot = `now − last_seen` only when `gt_cal_state() != CAL_UNSET`, `BootKind ∉ {FIRST_RUN, CRASH, SOFT_RESET}`, `now > last_seen`, `now − last_seen ≤ ABSENCE_MAX_S` (400 d, config.h:418); otherwise **zero** (T9). `BOOT_DEEPSLEEP` (`ESP_RST_DEEPSLEEP`, folded into SOFT_RESET today at storage.cpp:207) charges elapsed time as normal absence.
- Every long-term system (care decay/recovery, box recovery, cooldowns, sleep window, growth, daily buckets) uses epoch deltas clamped to `[0, ABSENCE_MAX_S]`, never tick counts; all u32 accumulators saturate; `test_overflow.cpp` covers xp u16, `age_s`/`lifetime_active_s` u32, epoch rollback, cooldown `until` wrap, millis wrap.
- Catch-up budget: `sim_catch_up_ex` runs 1,800 s chunks up to 2,000 steps (sim.cpp:1899-1982) with ~100 soft 64-bit divisions per 60 s sub-step (audit §2). P2-C10 measures the worst case (400 d) on device via DIAG; if it exceeds 1 s it is made resumable and pumped from the LOAD_SAVE state ("Poniéndose al día…") so no `loop()` iteration approaches the 5 s Task WDT.

---

## 3. Phases (exactly spec §52, 1..10)

Every commit lists tasks (files), acceptance (the gate is implied; extras named), size. Phase commit message = message of the phase exit commit; each `Pn-Cm` is its own commit titled `Pn-Cm: <summary>`; a commit that changes sim numbers is titled `retune: …` and regenerates the golden with the reason in the body.

### Phase 1 — Repository archaeology — size S — goal: audit + plan committed, repo hygiene

- [x] **P1-C0** Write `PEBBLEBOL_IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (spec §69 items 1-20; 669 lines; build verified 2,105,548 B / 72,748 B / 0 warnings). Done 2026-09-02.
- [x] **P1-C0** Write `PEBBLEBOL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (this file: decisions, architecture, surgery list, §52 phases, §50 test map, §67 done, risks). Done 2026-09-02.
- [x] **P1-C1** `git add docs/PEBBLEBOL_PRODUCT_SYSTEM_SPEC.md PEBBLEBOL_IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT.md PEBBLEBOL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (both untracked today, `git status`).
- [x] **P1-C1** Widen `.gitignore` from `*.bak` to `build/`, `tests/bin/`, `*.o`, `*.bin`, `*.elf`, `.arduino-cli/`, `.vscode/`.
- [x] **P1-C1** Create `docs/decisions.md` with D1-D7 (evidence copied from §0.2, default, "outcome" column empty; D1 marked "owner must inspect the physical board").
- [x] **P1-C1** Fix the doc drifts that mislead a builder now: FQBN README.md:131-134 (`nologo_esp32c3_super_mini`) → the verified `esp32:esp32:esp32c3:PartitionScheme=huge_app,CDCOnBoot=cdc`; stale sizes README.md:136, ESTUDIO_VISUAL.md:66/:455 → 2,105,548 / 72,748 with the date.
- [x] **P1-C1** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` running `make -C tests check` on push (no-op until P2-C2 adds tests; the firmware compile job is added in P2-C1 with a cached core). CI is not optional: §51 "keep the repository buildable" must be enforced by something other than a local script.
- [x] **P1-C1** Remove the stray tracked `00-Investigacion-inicial.docx` (6,536 B, pre-firmware note) or move it to `docs/legacy/`.
- Acceptance: `git status` clean; `arduino-cli compile` output unchanged (no source touched); CI green (empty test run).
- Commit message: `phase-1: repository archaeology — audit, plan, decisions log, CI skeleton, doc drift fixes`

### Phase 2 — Core engine — size L — goal: game state, save/load, clock, input, state machine, Pebble model, Box; buildable and host-tested with no cloud code

> **Known transient state entering P2-C6** (created by P2-C5, closed by Phase 8): with `/api/cfg` deleted and `index_html.h` a static placeholder, there is **no runtime Wi-Fi provisioning path**. Credentials reach `net_set_credentials()` only from the compile-time `CFG_WIFI_*` defaults through `g_cfg`. The captive-portal redirect still works, it just has no form to serve. This is expected, not a regression: the creator API restores provisioning in P8-C3.

DIAG/god command `stall <ms>` (busy-waits in `loop()`) to prove presses during a 300 ms stall are still recognised.

**P3-C2 XP and levels** — M
> **Trap from P3-C1's retune.** A meal is refused above `ACT_MEAL_REFUSE_PCT` (90 % satiety), and at the new `-4,200` mph hunger rate satiety stays above that for about **2.4 hours** after eating. So `FEED_MEAL` is only available roughly once every 2.4 h, and "+2 XP per care action with an hourly cap" will rarely be able to spend that hourly budget on feeding. Either source XP from the other care actions too (clean, play, pet), or size the cap to what the cooldowns actually permit — do not assume one feed per hour.
> Also: `CARE_DECAY_MPH[CARE_HEALTH]` is NOT a decay rate like its four neighbours. It is consumed sign-flipped inside `health_step()` as a damage rate, so a future "loop over all five and accumulate" refactor would apply health twice with the wrong sign. Give it its own named constant when you next touch `balance.h`. — **DONE in P3-C2**: the slot is now `0` and the bleed is `CARE_HEALTH_BLEED_MPH (+2000)`, same number, so the array is a pure decay table for the four stats that decay.

- [x] `game/xp.{h,cpp}`; `XP_TABLE[31]` in `balance.h` (`static_assert` sum < 65535); sources: care action +2 (hourly cap), minigame `permille*8/1000`, carried time +1/10 min awake (daily cap 48), later battle +25 / capture +10 / items; ledger snapshot/restore semantics from `GainSave`; `SIM_EV_LEVEL_UP` event → toast + `rd_flash`; HOME shows level + XP bar (§8); on level-up `hp_cur` rescales with `hp_max`.
- [x] `test_xp.cpp` (curve monotonic, multi-level carry-over, cap 30, caps, ledger under-reports never over-reports).
- Acceptance: gate; §67 "XP and leveling work".

**P3-C2b Delete the light; sleep follows the daylight; insistence wakes the pet** — S (owner-requested, closes D13)
> Not in the original plan. The owner's judgement (2026-09-03) was that the light switch "doesn't add anything", and D13 had measured what it costs: `sim_new_pet()` set `PF_LIGHT_ON` and `sleep_machine()` auto-slept only at night with the light OFF, so a player who never found the toggle owned a Pebble that **never slept** — energy pinned at 0 after 16.7 h and health bled to the 10 % floor by ~64 h, the worst outcome in the game, reached by doing nothing.

- [x] `data/balance.h` §5: a twelve-entry sunrise/sunset table (minutes from local midnight, ~40° N / peninsular Spain, LOCAL OFFICIAL time — the TZ string has already applied DST, so nothing may apply it twice), anchored at the middle of each month and interpolated by day of year, plus `SLEEP_AFTER_DUSK_MIN 90`, `WAKE_NUDGES 3`, `WAKE_NUDGE_WINDOW_S 10`, `SLEEP_RELAPSE_S 300`. **The header says outright that this is an approximation**: there is no latitude anywhere in the firmware (it went with the weather module; §44's Wi-Fi scan is a sensor, not a geolocator), so a player at another latitude sees a drift. Integer arithmetic only.
- [x] `game/daylight.{h,cpp}` (pure): `daylight_sunrise_min/sunset_min/bedtime_min/is_night`. `SLEEP_HOUR_START/END` deleted from `config.h`; bedtime is sunset + 90 min and morning is sunrise, both moving with the date.
- [x] `game/sim.cpp`: bedtime and the relapse are ONE rule — it is dark and nobody has touched the device for `SLEEP_RELAPSE_S`. Three refused gestures inside `WAKE_NUDGE_WINDOW_S` wake the pet and the third one then lands; the counter decays, so idle taps hours apart never accumulate, and **waking costs no stat** (§27 forbids punishment; the energy that drains awake is cost enough). The old "wake as soon as energy is full" rule is now day-only: energy refills in 5 h and a winter night is 13 h long, so at night it woke the pet and the window put it straight back — a SLEEP/WAKE pair every substep until dawn.
- [x] Delete the light everywhere: `PF_LIGHT_ON` (bit 0x0004 retired), `ACT_LIGHT_TOGGLE`, `MULT_LIGHT_ON_SLEEP`, `STR_MENU_LIGHT/STR_ACT_LIGHT/STR_RX_LIGHT_ON/STR_RX_LIGHT_OFF/STR_HLP_LIGHT`, the CARE row, the SETTINGS row, its `actfx` flash. `PBS_LIGHT_ON` becomes `PBS_RESERVED_LIGHT`: bit 0x10 is **reserved, never reused, never written**, no other bit moves (the 128 B layout is pinned by offsetof asserts) and the v1 migration now drops the bit instead of carrying it.
- [x] `test_daylight.cpp` (samples reproduced at their anchors, continuity across every day boundary incl. the December→January wrap, seasons the right way round, the night window agrees with the table) and `test_care.cpp` §§12-15 (asleep at 03:00 in January and July, awake at 10:00 in both, bedtime earlier in January than in July, three nudges inside the window wake it and three over an hour do not, waking costs no stat, the relapse after five quiet minutes, and the D13 regression: a full day of neglect is spent asleep and recharging, crossing the boundary exactly once each way). `sim_v1.txt` (now `care_v2.txt`) re-recorded — the hashed `PF_*` word lost bit 0x0004; verified by hashing the six stats WITHOUT the flags word on both sides of the change, which is byte-identical.
- Acceptance: gate; D13 closed in `docs/decisions.md`.

**P3-C3 Evolution** — M
- [x] `game/evolution.{h,cpp}` (pure) on `EvolutionRule` (`data/evolution_table.h`, all five §18 `EvoCond` kinds); `EVOLVE_PENDING` set by `xp_add`, consumed by the `EVOLUTION` state reusing the ceremony P2-C11c already parameterised (`CEREMONY_EVOLVE` starts at `HATCH_T_FLASH` and throws sparks); A confirms through a `CFM_EVOLVE` modal (§18) and a decline leaves the bit set (§27: no punishment); `evolution_apply` **and both its flushes** run inside `app_evolve_active()` **before** `ceremony_start()` arms the first frame — the ordering argument at the top of `ui/ceremony.h` — with `save_checkpoint_all()` in the same call. **The context carries a VALIDITY MASK**: a condition whose input this phase cannot supply REFUSES, never passes. `hp_cur` is rescaled by `xp_hp_rescale()`, the level-up path's own rescale, factored out so there is exactly one.
- [x] The roster grew from one placeholder row to the three REAL rows of family 1 (decision **D14**): Paketo/Fragmar/Rafagón, ids 1..3, with their six `strings_es.h` entries. It stops at family 1 because the contiguity guard makes the roster all-or-nothing in family blocks and the first family with a real condition is family 4. §18's fourth item, SOUND, arrives with `hardware/audio.{h,cpp}` in P6 — the ceremony's phase edges are where those calls slot in.
- [x] `test_evolution.cpp` (rule lookup, the level gate below/at/above, every condition kind satisfied / unsatisfied / **input-not-supplied refuses**, apply recomputes stats, final stage never evolves, a failing condition neither evolves nor clears the pending bit, the counter saturates, the whole family walk, and every compile-time guard re-checked at runtime). `test_screens` snapshots the confirmation. **P3-C5 CORRECTED THE REST OF THIS SENTENCE**, which used to read "`test_pet_view` proves the body the view describes really changes": it does not, because the function it drives is not the one the firmware runs. See the open bullet below. **P3-C5 follow-up: that correction had landed HERE ONLY.** e2004e7's diffstat does not contain `tests/test_pet_view.cpp` (it touches `tests/Makefile` alone), so the struck sentence survived verbatim in the two places a reader meets the test — the §P3-C3 banner and case name in `tests/test_pet_view.cpp` ("This is the only place the claim can be checked … what the ceremony reveals is a PetView, and a PetView is exactly what this file builds"), and the comment above its link line in `tests/Makefile` ("the view is where the body changed is provable"). Both now scope the case to the MODEL half on a fill path the firmware does not run, and point here; the case is renamed `an_evolution_changes_the_body_the_view_would_describe`.
- [x] **§18's VISUAL TRANSFORMATION. Unticked by P3-C5; CLOSED BY P4-C4a, and the paragraph below is kept verbatim as the record of what was wrong.** What P4-C4a changed: `ui/pet_art.h` resolves a Pebble's atlas key as `species_get(species_id)->sprite_id`, falling back to the genome nibble only when there is no row (id 0, the creator's 200..209, anything past the roster); `sprite_form_of()` takes that key instead of a `Genome` and `sprite_set_id()` has no `species` parameter left, so **no path from a genome to a body exists that does not pass a species row first**. `pet_view_attach()` re-derives `form` the moment `species_id` arrives - which is the ordering bug the old text did not name, since `pet_view_fill_sim()` runs one call EARLIER and cannot see it. The name follows the body: `ui_pet_name()` is nickname -> species -> dynasty, and the BOX list stopped calling ten different creatures SETA. `pet_view_fill()` is DELETED (see the P4-C1 obligation-6 bullet for why). Measured: 24 of 24 evolution rules change the drawn set id at BABY, ADULT and SENIOR and none at CHILD or TEEN. **P4-C4's FOLLOW-UP NARROWED THAT SENTENCE, WHICH WAS TRUE AND WAS BEING READ AS MORE THAN IT SAID.** Those are three stages, not three moments: `sim.cpp`'s ladder is `>= 20` SENIOR / `>= 15` ADULT / `>= 10` TEEN / `>= 5` CHILD, and **12 of the 24 shipped rules have a minimum level of 8, 10 or 12 — every family's FIRST evolution, the starter's Paketo → Fragmar among them — so they fire at CHILD or TEEN, where only the NAME and `hp_max` move**; the body follows when the pet reaches ADULT. The other 12 (levels 18, 20, 24) change the body at the moment they fire. That is a deliberate policy — the atlas authors exactly two CHILD and two TEEN designs and both pairs already carry care quality — and it is now a CHECKED number rather than a remembered one: `every_rule_measured_at_the_level_it_actually_fires_at` binds a Pebble at each rule's own level, asks the SIMULATION which stage that is, and asserts 12/12 with the name moving for all 24; the no-row fallback is bit-identical to the old arithmetic over gene 0..15 x 6 stages x 4 poses; three goldens moved and one was added. THE ORIGINAL TEXT FOLLOWS UNCHANGED. `pet_view_fill()` — the only path that feeds `evo_state`'s stage bits to `sprite_form_of()`, and the one `tests/test_pet_view.cpp` drives — **has no caller in `Pebblebol/src`**. The firmware fills the active pet's view through `pet_view_fill_sim()` + `pet_view_attach()` (`ui/ui.cpp`), whose `form` comes from `fill_genome(out, p.genome, p.minor_form, p.stage)`; all three inputs are `SimView` fields that an evolution does not move, as `app_evolve_active()` says itself. `sprite_lookup_pose()` keys on `gene_species(genome)`, life stage and `form`, never on `species_id`; `SpeciesDef.sprite_id` is read by nothing and `STR_SPC_NAME_1..3` / `STR_SPC_FLAV_1..3` are displayed nowhere. So a player who confirms an evolution today watches **2.7 s** of ceremony and gets back the same body, the same name and a different `hp_max`. (**P3-C5 follow-up corrected 4.5 s to 2.7 s.** The EVOLVE arm of `ceremony_begin()` back-dates `s_t0` by `HATCH_T_FLASH` = 1,800 ms, because there is no shell to rock or crack, and `CP_NAME` ends the show at `HATCH_TOTAL_MS` = 4,480 ms: an evolution runs 4,480 − 1,800 = 2,680 ms. 4.48 s is the HATCH ceremony. `ui/ceremony.cpp:69-73` and `:140-143`.) **Carried to P4-C1**, where the roster grows and the view has to learn `species_id`; the comment at `species_table.h:88-92` is wrong for the path the firmware runs and goes with it.
- Acceptance: gate; §67 "Evolution works".

**P3-C4 Minigame framework + input latency fix** — L
- [x] `minigames/minigame.h` (§1.4, six hooks incl. `finish()`), `manager.{h,cpp}` (pool via `RNG_MINIGAME`, sequence play of N=3 with a 1.2 s "¿SIGUIENTE?" card, A = next, B = stop; `finish()` called on completion or abort; cooldown from the care ledger), `MgCtx` fixed-size (no heap). **The manager is PURE** — the hooks split into `MgLogic` (the five pure ones) and `MinigameDef` (those plus `draw`, assembled in the device-only `minigames/registry.cpp`), so the "exactly one report per game" property is a host test rather than a hope.
- [x] Port REFLEJOS → `ping` and MEMORIA → `sequence` (shortened to 3 levels of 3/4/5) into `games/*_logic.cpp` + `*_draw.cpp`; delete SALTO and `MinigameState`. Renamed in the UI too (`PING`, `SECUENCIA`) so the set of six reads as one set; SALTO is in no §29 list and went entirely, taking the PLAY screen from 4 rows to 3 and `px_hline()` with it.
- [x] New logic files for the §29 set: `packet_flood`, `firewall`, `buffer`, `delete`; each 5-15 s, fixed 25 ms step (ui.cpp:1731-1739 pattern), per-run `Rng`, drawing only through `gfx_*`. **P3-C4b.** Runs are 11,675 / 12,000 / 10,000 / 11,600 ms and every one of them is a **fixed UPPER BOUND** rather than a worst case — no press can *lengthen* a run in any of the four, which is the hole both shipped games had. **P3-C5 follow-up narrowed this from "a CONSTANT":** a bound is what the four have and what the test asserts (`CHECK(sort_steps <= idle_steps); // playing can only shorten it`), but only FIREWALL (12,000 ms) and BUFFER (10,000 ms) are genuinely constant. Measured over five seeds and four tapes, reading `MgCtx::t_ms` at `done()`: PACKET FLOOD collapses 11,675 → 9,875 ms when played, because `pf_press()` calls `pf_resolve()` and advances `c.round` early; DELETE collapses 11,600 → 175 ms with B mashed, because the run ends at `s.charges == 0` (`delete_logic.cpp:211`). The test case that claimed otherwise in its NAME — `packet_flood_ends_at_the_same_instant_however_it_is_played` — is renamed to the bound it actually checks. The two collapse risks the step was scoped around are now compiled assertions rather than prose: FIREWALL is discrete (`c.score == fw_blocked(c) * 125` asserted after every press and every step, and a tape that sits in the correct lane for four fifths of every fall and steps out just before impact scores exactly 0), BUFFER is continuous (`c.score == buf_inside_steps(c) * 1000 / 340`, same per-step check, and a press is an impulse rather than a move); DELETE requires choosing WHICH block (a tape that walks to the nearest block of either kind must score strictly less than one that reads its type, and purging a healthy block is checked directly to pay nothing), and PACKET_FLOOD is a sort (its tape routes by matching the packet's digit against the two mouth digits and nothing else). Three small hardenings came out of review, all in the same shape — an arithmetic claim that lived in a comment now lives in the compiler: PING gained the run-budget `static_assert` the other five already had (its header said 5 * (1999 + 900) = 14,495 ms, but a round ends at the first step STRICTLY past arm + window and elapsed time is always a whole number of steps, so the bound is 5 * 2900 = 14,500 — the thinnest margin of the six); DELETE's choice proof now DERIVES its "3 indices apart" from `DEL_LIFE_MS`/`DEL_SPAWN_MS` instead of spelling the 3, because a hardcoded 3 keeps passing when the fuse is shortened, which is the one edit that kills the property; and `packet_flood_draw.cpp` stopped pulling `<stdio.h>` into a device translation unit to `snprintf` a single decimal digit. **(P3-C5 follow-up, scope note: `ping_draw.cpp:15` still does `#include <stdio.h>` to `snprintf` a run time at `:35-36`. This sentence is scoped to `packet_flood`, so it is not false — but P3-C5 pulled `ping_draw.o` into the host build for correction (a) and nobody noticed the same leftover next door. Open, not fixed here: removing it changes the flash figures in the table below.)** Three framework defects were fixed on the way, each of which would have bitten these four: `MgCtx::state` sat at offset 14 and `mg_state<T>()`'s `alignof` assert cannot see an offset, so every 32-bit game field was being read at 2 mod 4 (`alignas(uint32_t)` plus an `offsetof` assert; `sizeof(MgCtx)` stays 48); `draw_str_list()` sized its row array `CARE_ROWS` (5) and clamped, so at `PLAY_ROWS = 7` BORRAR and "Volver" would never have drawn while the cursor still rang through them; and the in-run pause was on `GST_TAP_R`, the same physical press `game_service()` feeds to `mgr_press()`, so one B press both played the game and opened a modal over it — moved to `GST_HOLD_R`, which emits no tap on release, with the strip now saying `JUGAR` / `JUGAR/PAUSA` during a run instead of advertising B as PAUSA in six games that use it to play. **The second half of that one landed in review**: moving the gesture stopped the collision but not the run — `sm_service()` calls the GAME update hook whatever is on top of the screen, so the confirm went up while the game kept stepping, kept scoring and kept eating the raw press edges the player was answering the dialog with. `game_service()` now returns early on `dialog_modal() != MODAL_NONE`, after moving its own timestamp forward so the paused time is dropped rather than delivered as one huge `dt`, and drains both press edges so the presses spent on the dialog are not replayed into the game.
- [x] Input: delete `GST_DBL_L/R` and `DOUBLE_TAP_WINDOW_MS` so a TAP is emitted at release (+25 ms debounce); add `input_pressed_edge(btn)`; HOME `DBL_R` pet → `TAP_R`; `test_input.cpp` asserts TAP ≤ 30 ms after release. **Done ahead of the rest of P3-C4** (the subagent API was returning 529s, so this self-contained half was built directly). The double tap paid for shortcuts on eight call sites, not the one the plan names: six list "jump to first/last" (dropped — every one of those lists already wraps), HOME's caress (`TAP_R` already did it), STATUS's genome-hex reveal (**moved to `HOLD_R`**, which `input_router.h` calls "B secondary" and which STATUS had free), the menu's repeat-last-action (**deleted with its only caller** — in no spec section, and its empty path toasted "bad argument"), and the god console's gene DECREMENT (dropped; `TAP_R` increments and wraps, so every value is still reachable). `s_last_action` went too: with repeat-last-action gone it was written in three places and read in none.
- [x] `test_minigames.cpp` (each game: same seed + scripted input tape → identical score; finishes ≤ 15,000 ms; score ∈ 0..1000; manager sums correctly; `finish()` called exactly once); `test_screens` snapshots for each game's intro/run/result. **P3-C4b: 49 cases, 132,445 checks.** **Two P3-C5 corrections.** (a) "snapshots for each game" was true of FOUR of six: `ping_draw.cpp` and `sequence_draw.cpp` were compiled only by the firmware build and never rendered on the host, because `MG_FRAME_OBJS` omitted them. P3-C5 added both objects and four goldens — `mg_ping_wait` / `mg_ping_lit` (dark, then half the screen solid) and `mg_sequence_show` / `mg_sequence_answer` (a symbol lit, then both pads outlined and the turn handed over) — so all six draw halves now render at 128x64 with `fb_oob() == 0`, and the claim is true as written. (b) "DELETE's choice proof now DERIVES its '3 indices apart' from `DEL_LIFE_MS`/`DEL_SPAWN_MS`" describes something that is not in the tree: `delete_forces_a_choice_because_two_corrupted_blocks_share_the_board` names neither macro. It measures the maximum simultaneous corrupt count over 32 seeds and asserts `>= 2`, which proves the property DIRECTLY rather than deriving the schedule that implies it — stronger than the description, but not the description. All six games are in `ALL[]` (with a `static_assert` against `MG_ID_COUNT`, so a seventh cannot be added without joining them) and the contract tapes are now GENERIC — they used to be PING's, applied to every game, which meant SEQUENCE was driven by `ping_is_lit()` reading `SeqState`'s bytes as a `PingState`. TEN MUTATIONS were run against the four new games and TWO SURVIVED the first draft, which is what the second draft is for: a purge that counted any block rather than only a corrupted one (every score-based delete case still passed — the tapes that walk already pick the right targets and the ones that do not barely reach anything, so the rule is now checked directly), and a 400 ms overlap window in place of firewall's single sampled impact. Two more were caught only by comparing the LADDER of score values a run climbs rather than its final: a speed bonus on a run that already reaches 1000 is invisible after `mg_score()` clamps. Snapshots: seven at P3-C4b (eleven since P3-C5's correction (a)), at the real 128x64 with `fb_oob() == 0` — `mg_packet_flood_run`, `mg_packet_flood_reroute`, `mg_firewall_run`, `mg_firewall_leaked`, `mg_buffer_run`, `mg_delete_run`, `mg_delete_purged`. They are the RUN frame only; intro and result are `ui.cpp`'s chrome and are not in a pure translation unit. FOUR MORE CASES CAME OUT OF ADVERSARIAL REVIEW, two of them against assertions that could not fail. `buffer_never_moves_the_score_by_an_event_sized_amount` drove an IDLE run: an untouched cursor is absorbed at the left wall and the zone provably never reaches a wall, so the score was 0 at every step, the largest step was 0 by construction and `CHECK(worst <= 3)` was `CHECK(0 <= 3)` — it is driven by the tracking and parking tapes now, with a `CHECK(worst >= 2)` under it so it cannot silently become a tautology again (measured: 3 shipped, 15 under a mutant paying 5 steps for ENTERING the zone, 117 at 40). Nothing asserted that the safe zone MOVES, which is half of spec 29.4 and the whole of what separates BUFFER from FIREWALL: freezing it (`s.zc + 0`) left all 45 cases green and was caught only by a pixel golden, which a legitimate `make -C tests screens` re-record would have erased. Two cases now state it — the zone must travel further than its own opening width (32 px at the least over these seeds, 0 frozen), and a CAMPER that latches the zone's opening position and holds it must score strictly less than a tracker on every seed (mean 183 against 1000; 1000 flat if the zone stops). The ceiling case drove only the idle tape and called it the worst case, which is true of five games and false of SEQUENCE — the one game a player can lengthen, since every correct answer buys another `SEQ_ANSWER_MS`: idle ends at 2,100 ms, and answering on the last legal step every time runs 14,100. It now drives every generic tape plus a per-`MgId` longest tape, with a case asserting the stretch tape really does stretch. And `every_game_wears_the_name_and_the_hint_of_its_own_id` closes the third link in the row-order chain: the PLAY rows are folded against `MgId` at compile time and their labels are held by a golden, but the `name_idx`/`hint_idx` pair a run draws is chosen inside each `*_logic.cpp`, where giving FIREWALL buffer's name passed the whole suite and the firmware build. `test_screens` links both halves of each game (`*_logic.o` AND `*_draw.o`, which draw only through the `gfx.h` seam); `test_minigames` links no draw object and must not.
- [x] Acceptance: gate; §67 "At least 5 minigames", "playable with two buttons", "transition cleanly" (6 games: ping, sequence, packet_flood, firewall, buffer, delete). **Met at P3-C4b**: six games, gate green, matrix green on all 7 variants (flash 1,893,040 of 2,400,000).

**P3-C5 Phase-3 exit** — S
- [x] Strengthen `care_one_hour_of_catch_up_is_the_same_however_it_is_chunked`: as written it **cannot fail**, because `sim_tick()` already splits any dt into 60 s substeps, so 3600, 60x60 and 6x600 are the identical substep sequence. Add the 1 s chunking (3600 calls of `sim_tick(1)`), which is the case that actually probes below the grid — the P3-C1 verifier ran it by hand and it does match byte for byte. **It does, and adding it FOUND A REAL DEFECT one hour away.** The hour itself matches on all 128 bytes at every chunk size, because 10:00 → 11:00 on a hatchling contains no rate CHANGE; the test now says that, and says why the other three cases are a tautology. Past that hour the 1 s path parts company at t = 72 min, and the cause was not rounding: **`poop_step()` scaled its advance by `MULT_SLEEP` (x0.35) and truncated it every sub-step with no carry.** `(1 * 350) / 1000` is 0 — and 1 s is the step the live device runs (`app.cpp` → `sim_step_seconds()` → 1 outside god mode) — so **a sleeping pebble never advanced its poop timer at all and could not poop overnight, ever**, while the offline catch-up over the same night (60 s sub-steps, an exact 21) produced two. Same night, same pebble, two different models. `poop_step()` carries its remainder now, the way `accum()` always has; measured over 8 h from 23:00, poops before the fix were 2 at every step ≥ 10 s, 1 at 5 s and **0 at 1 s**, and are 2 at every step from 1 s to 3600 s after it. `stage_step()`'s `g.acc_stage = 0` became `-=` for the same reason (harmless at dt ∈ {1, 60}, the same leak for anything else). **Nothing at dt = 60 moved**, because `60 * 350 / 1000` is exact, so `care_v2.txt` was NOT re-recorded and no other test changed. Two new cases carry the finding: `care_the_poop_clock_advances_at_every_step_size` (nine step sizes, and it asserts the span really is a night so it cannot pass for the wrong reason) and `care_a_finer_step_moves_a_rate_change_by_less_than_one_substep`, which states the part that is a legitimate bound rather than a bug — a rate CHANGE lands on the sub-step grid, so a finer step charges it up to one sub-step early, at most one sub-step of the largest rate change in the model (25 milli). Measured: 9 milli over 2 awake hours, 25 over 4, 11 over an 8 h night; a displayed percent is 1,000. `SIM_SUBSTEP_S` moved to `sim.h` so a test can state the bound. Mutation-checked: reverting the carry fails 6 checks across the two new cases. **P3-C5 follow-up corrects three claims in this line.** (1) 25 milli is not "the largest rate change in the model": the sleep/wake energy flip is 26,000 milli/h, one sub-step of it is 433 milli, and `care_run_chunked()` could not see it because it freezes the wall clock — a moving-clock case now measures 43 milli across bedtime and 126 across sunrise. (2) The strengthened chunk case still did not test `sim_tick()`'s chopper at all: 10:00 → 11:00 has no rate change, so deleting the chopper (`if (seconds > 0) sub_step(seconds);`) kept the whole gate green — three chunks above the grid over spans that DO contain a rate change now catch it (gaps 25 / 11 / 126 → 1,858 / 3,594 / 3,655), and the 60x60 and 6x600 cases guard nothing, not even the "`SIM_SUBSTEP_S` divides an hour" they were documented as guarding. (3) "Mutation-checked" covered the POOP carry only; reverting `stage_step()`'s carry left the entire suite green, and `care_the_stage_check_keeps_its_cadence_at_any_chunk_size` plus a `static_assert` on `SIM_SUBSTEP_S <= STAGE_CHECK_PERIOD_S` cover it now.
- [x] Re-check the soak criterion now that **D13 is closed** (P3-C2b). "No stat pinned at 0 for more than 6 simulated hours of neglect" used to be unreachable, because a pebble whose light nobody turned off never slept and energy pinned at 0 for ever. Since P3-C2b it sleeps every night and wakes recharged (`care_a_full_night_of_neglect_is_spent_asleep_and_recharging` pins that), so ENERGY can now satisfy the criterion — but HUNGER and HAPPINESS still cannot: at 4,200 and 3,000 milli/h they empty in about a day of total neglect and stay at 0, which is the design (health is what the floor protects). So restate the criterion as "no stat that the player is not deliberately ignoring", or scope it to energy and health, and run it. **RESTATED, ASSERTED AND MEASURED** as `care_a_fortnight_of_neglect_only_pins_the_stats_the_player_owns` (`test_care.cpp` §16): *fourteen simulated days of total neglect — a hatched pebble, a trustworthy clock, and not one action for a fortnight. ENERGY, the one core stat the simulation restores by itself, is never at 0 for more than 6 continuous simulated hours and is back above 90 % at every sunrise. HUNGER, HAPPINESS and CLEANLINESS are at 0 for most of the fortnight and are meant to be — they are the ones only the player can refill. HEALTH never reaches 0 at all and never falls below `HEALTH_FLOOR_PCT`.* **The plan's own restatement omitted CLEANLINESS, which is the worst of the five** (first to bottom out, at 24.7 h, because every poop adds `CARE_HYGIENE_POOP_MPH` on top of the base) — "hunger and happiness" and stop would have been factually wrong. Measured longest continuous run at 0, on the `test_care` fixture over 336 h: hunger 305.2 h, happiness 160.2 h, **energy 2.1 h**, cleanliness 311.4 h, health **never**; 14 wake-ups, all above 90 % (99 % every time). Robustness outside the suite, 480 fortnights (40 genesis genomes x 12 month anchors): energy's longest run at 0 is at most **3.87 h** and exceeds 6 h in 0 of 480; health reaches 0 in 0 of 480. Forcing the metabolism gene to 15 (x1.50), which genesis cannot roll — it clamps to 4..12 — over all 366 start days: **5.40 h**, still under 6. Happiness does not "stay empty" as the criterion drafts assumed: `events_step()` pays `EVENT_VISITA_HAPPINESS` at 72 h and `EVENT_BIRTHDAY_HAPPY` every 168 h, which is why its run is ~160 h and not ~300, and the test pins that too. **Scoped to a valid clock on purpose**: without one `is_night()` refuses to answer, the pebble never sleeps, and energy pins at 0 for **322.78 h of the same 336** — D13's known cost, recorded with it in `docs/decisions.md`.
- [x] Matrix + tag `v0.3.0-pet`; `test_sim_golden` renamed `test_care_golden`; bench: god-mode x3600 soak (godmode.cpp:538-597 CSV) shows no stat pinned at 0 for > 6 simulated hours of neglect. **The bench moved into the suite instead of the god console.** The criterion above is a host test that runs in about a millisecond, so it is a GATE rather than a thing somebody has to remember to drive on a device that has never been flashed; the god-mode CSV stays as the on-hardware confirmation and is listed with the other first-flash measurements. **(P3-C5 follow-up: it was NOT listed — `docs/decisions.md` "Measurements to record when hardware exists (P2-C0)" held the heap soak, boot free heap, `sendBuffer()` frame time, I2C probe and the CDC port name and no god-mode soak, and the P2-C0 box below does not list it either. The bullet exists now, so the sentence is true.)** `test_sim_golden.cpp` → `test_care_golden.cpp` and `golden/sim_v1.txt` → `golden/care_v2.txt` (`git mv`, `tests/Makefile` and both `retune:` targets updated): the `v1` meant the legacy v1 SIMULATION the file was first recorded from, which two retunes later is no longer what it pins. Only the transcript's header line changed; every hashed minute is byte-identical, which the diff and the passing test prove together. Matrix green on all 7 variants at 0 project warnings, flash 1,893,072 of 2,400,000 (**+28 B** for the poop carry — the exit said +32 B and no reconstruction of the pre-fix `poop_step()` reproduces it; 1,893,072 → 1,893,044 with the carry arithmetic, the `g.poop_rem` field and its three resets all removed, 1,893,058 with the arithmetic alone, globals 70,348 in every case), globals 70,348 of 90,000.
- [x] **P3-C5 follow-up (second): every NUMBER and every CLAIM in the exit documents, which is the pair of checks that never ran.** Five adversarial lenses were meant to check this exit; three ran and two died on API errors, and the two that died were these. Four blocking corrections, each backed by a command re-run against this tree. (1) **The evolution ceremony is 2.7 s, not the 4.5 s three documents said** — 4.48 s is the HATCH show; the EVOLVE arm back-dates `s_t0` by `HATCH_T_FLASH`. (2) **The three constants "the 6 h number" was said to depend on were all wrong, and the dependence is not monotonic** — first break at `CARE_ENERGY_ASLEEP_MPH` ≈ 11,000 (13,300 breaks nothing at all; what it protects is the 90 %-at-sunrise half), `SLEEP_AFTER_DUSK_MIN` ≈ 240 min, `CARE_DECAY_MPH[CARE_ENERGY]` ≈ −8,000; below ≈ 5,000 `SIM_WAKE_DAY_ENERGY_PCT` keeps the pebble asleep into the day and the run at 0 shortens again. Full sweep and the asleep-in-daylight counts in `docs/decisions.md`. The same section now records how weak the live assertion is: all three edits leave `care_a_fortnight_of_neglect_only_pins_the_stats_the_player_owns` GREEN, and the gate reddens through an unrelated grid-bound equality. (3) **"Every minigame run is a CONSTANT length" is a bound, not a constant, for four of six** — corrected in this file and in `CHANGELOG.md`, and `packet_flood_ends_at_the_same_instant_however_it_is_played` renamed, since its own body asserted only `sort_steps <= idle_steps`. (4) **The sentence the exit says it struck from `test_pet_view` was struck here only** — the test file and `tests/Makefile` still carried it; both corrected, case renamed. Also: **+28 B not +32 B** for the poop carry; three §67 boxes ticked and the section's rule written down; the `esp_random` gate added to `tools/check.sh`, where the plan had listed it for two phases without it ever existing; the god-mode ×3600 soak added to the first-flash measurement list two documents claimed it was already in; the CHANGELOG's `79 %` antecedent and its `[0.3.0-pet]` link note corrected; `rng.cpp`'s unexplained appearance in the exit diffstat explained. **One challenge REFUTED rather than fixed**: "Measured 9 milli over 2 awake hours (1 poop)" reproduces exactly from this tree — `care_grid_gap(10, h*3600)` for h = 1…8 gives 0 / 9 / 13 / 25 / 31 / 31 / 31 / 31 milli at 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 4 / 4 / 4 poops. The 2 h span is not a live assertion, but the sentence claims a measurement and the measurement is right.
- Commit message: `phase-3: virtual pet — hours-scale care, XP/levels, data-driven evolution, minigame framework with 6 two-button games, 25 ms tap latency`

### Phase 4 — Battle engine — size L — goal: types, attacks, battle state machine, 3-Pebble teams, switching, local battle protocol (host-proven)

**P4-C1 Content pipeline v0 + minimal roster** — M

Proven: editing one comment character in `species_table.h` → `--check` exit 1; moving one `spawn_weight` in `species.json` → `--check` exit 1 naming two files.
  - **THE ROSTER IS 36 SPECIES (12 families x 3), NOT THE 12 THIS LINE ASKED FOR, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED.** Contiguity (`id == index + 1`) is a `static_assert`, so the only legal subset of the 60-species pack is a PREFIX, i.e. a whole number of families. Three numbers decided which. (1) **Flash does not bind at any size**: the phase-3 baseline was 1,893,072 B against `GATE_FLASH_MAX` 2,400,000; this step costs **+3,022 B** (1,896,094, globals unchanged at 70,348) and the remaining 60-species families would add roughly another 1.5 KB against **503,906 B** of headroom. **GLOBALS, NOT FLASH, IS THE BUDGET PHASES 5-10 SHOULD PLAN AGAINST, and this step bought none of it back**: the tables are all `constexpr`/`.rodata`, so P4-C1 spent **exactly 0 B of globals on all 7 variants** — which is why the roster size was free, but also why the true measurement is that flash sits at 79.0 % of its cap with 503,906 B free while globals sits at 78.2 % of `GATE_GLOBALS_MAX` 90,000 with only **19,652 B** free. Saying only "flash does not bind" is true and would have been read as "nothing binds". (2) **The sprite atlas binds, hard.** `SPR_BABY_BLOB + sprite_id` with the pack's invariant `sprite_id == id - 1` gives `SPR_BABY_BLOB (2) + (N-1) < SPRITE_SET_COUNT (38)`, i.e. **N ≤ 36**. Shipping all 60 would mean deleting a live guard against real art; 36 is the largest roster today's atlas can address, and `game/species.cpp` now asserts it at compile time. **P4-C4a NARROWED THE FIRST HALF OF THIS SENTENCE, WHICH SAID `SpeciesDef.sprite_id` "resolves as" that sum**: nothing ever evaluated it to draw anything, and against the 38-set atlas it would have sent species 25..36 onto GHOST, TOMB and the ten pose sets. It is a FORWARD bound on the P10 atlas (2 eggs + one 24x24 body per species), and it is exactly as load-bearing for the roster size as this bullet says - what it is not is the resolution. The firmware folds the key into the authored pools (`% 8` at BABY, `% 6` at ADULT/SENIOR) and a second `static_assert` next to this one checks THAT — the guard plan §1.5.2 lists and `species_table.h` cannot carry without pulling the 86 KB atlas into every TU. (3) **12 species would have re-created the defect obligation 2 exists to close.** Families 1..5 of the pack are all SIGNAL: a 12-species roster carries ONE type, no shipped creature could ever hold a CORRUPT or SYSTEM attack (a learnset is "own type or NEUTRAL"), `TYPE_CHART` would evaluate to 0 for every pair of shipped species, and **20 of the 34 attacks would sit on no learnset at all**. It also does not compile: at 4 families the HIDDEN category has **no COMMON species**, so its rarity-0 WILD row (12 % of every HIDDEN scan) has an empty pool and `species_spawn_sums_are_usable()` rejects the table. 36 spans all three types (SIGNAL 1-5, CORRUPT 6-10, SYSTEM 11-12), leaves exactly 2 attacks for families still to come, and gives the eight legacy v1 families eight DISTINCT base-stage destinations, which 4 families cannot.

  - **`CONTENT_VERSION` moved out of `core/version.h` into the generated `data/content_version.h`** (0x5B4A). It is FNV-1a 32 over the six JSON files with every `_`-prefixed key stripped, plus the roster size, folded to a non-zero u16. The exclusion is the point and is proven both ways: moving a real `spawn_weight` takes it 0x5B4A → 0x8FF3, while editing a `_silhouette` design note leaves it at 0x5B4A — otherwise fixing a typo in a comment would mark every save on every device as made against foreign content.

A whole-game pacing change is its own commit with its own re-recorded golden. Recorded in `balance.h` §4.
