- Acceptance: gate; generator output committed; tables compile with all guards. **MET.** `tools/check.sh` → `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1896094 globals=70348 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 23/23` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` → `MATRIX OK`, all 7 variants at 0 project warnings.
- **EVERY GUARD WAS MADE TO FIRE.** A guard nobody has watched fail is a comment, so both halves were mutation-tested. **24 of 24 compile-time mutations are rejected by a named `static_assert`** — id out of place, a NEUTRAL species, a family with no stage-0 row, a `spawn_weight` of 0, a roster that is not whole families, a `sprite_id` past the atlas, accuracy 0, an asymmetric `TYPE_CHART`, an unresolvable learnset id, a repeated move, an off-type move, an item class with no row, a cross-family evolution, a conditional rule with no value, two rules sharing a source, `evo_rule` disagreeing with the table, a non-final stage with no way out, a family owing fewer than two rules, encounter weights not summing to 100, NOTHING below 15 %, a WILD row with no species pool, an item drop naming no item, a built-in breaking its own stage budget, and the legacy family map reverting to ids 1..8. **13 of 13 runtime mutations are caught by a named test** — species 1's `spawn_weight` moved, species 2's learnset reordered, attack 26 taken off its only learnset, SPECIAL's share of a scan cut, `EVOC_CORRUPTED` skipping its input check, `HAPPINESS_GE` becoming a strict `>`, the weighted picker losing its wrap, the ATK gene not reaching the derived stat, `level/3` becoming `level/4`, the genome fold losing its cap, `hp_max` leaving the XP module, the legacy map reverting, and a migrated pet losing its learnset. One test was found un-failable during that pass and tightened: `the_special_encounter_outcome_still_has_no_payload_table` summed SPECIAL's weight across all six categories, which survived cutting one category from 4 to 1; it checks per category now. **A SECOND ONE WAS FOUND BY THE P4-C1 REVIEW AND IS RECORDED IN THE FOLLOW-UP BULLET BELOW**: the `EVOC_CORRUPTED` input-check mutation in that list of 13 was caught by `test_evolution.cpp`, NOT by the obligation-4 case in `test_content.cpp` that was written for it. Both catch it now.
- **P4-C1 REVIEW FOLLOW-UP (five adversarial lenses).** One blocking finding and eight advisory ones were acted on; every fix is a change to code or to a sentence, and each was measured rather than argued.
  - **BLOCKING — obligation 4's un-failable half.** `apply_refuses_when_the_condition_input_was_never_supplied` never exercised the `have`-bit gate: both its contexts came from `evo_context_clear()`, so `ctx.corrupted` was 0 and the VALUE check refused first. Deleting the guard left `test_content` at 30/30. Fixed by adding the one form that separates the bit from the value — a TRUE value with the bit WITHHELD — on BOTH shipped conditional rules, and the plan sentence that claimed the deletion "fails two of them" is corrected above with each mutation named against the case it fails.
  - **The sixth gate could be disarmed by deleting its own input.** `tools/check.sh` wrapped the content gate in `if [ -d "$ROOT/tools/content" ]` with no `else`, so `rm -rf tools/content` printed `GATE OK` and exited 0 — the exact failure the file's own comment forbids. `tools/content` is a committed deliverable, not a not-yet-built feature, so the outer test is now a hard `fail`, proven: `GATE FAIL: tools/content is missing …`, exit 1.
  - **Three bad-input paths produced a traceback instead of a sentence.** `gen_content.py` wrote a `"` or a `\` from a JSON name raw into the `ES[]` array (invalid C, with the error pointing at `strings_es.h`), wrote an out-of-range integer verbatim into a `uint8_t` field (`-Wnarrowing` plus three unnamed `static_assert` errors), and raised `IndexError` on a rarity of 7. It now escapes and refuses strings by name (`c_str_literal()` — control characters and anything past Latin-1 die with the reason), bound-checks EVERY emitted integer against the struct field it lands in (`Content._check_field_widths()`), and names a bad rarity. Measured: `target = 999` → `evolution.json row 0 (species 1): target = 999 is outside the 0..255 the generated field holds`; rarity 7 → the same shape; an em dash → the `u8g2_font_5x8_tf` sentence. `verify.py` had the same disease in two places (a `KeyError` from a non-contiguous id, a `UnicodeEncodeError` from the flash-budget byte count) and now prints named `[FAIL]` lines and its `RESULT:` in both.
  - **`creator_schema.h`'s arithmetic did not close** — "rows for two of them" and then a list of eight, out of thirteen, with type validity and move legality unmentioned in a header whose job is to enumerate what has no row. It is 2 + 1 + 2 + 8 now, and the two derivable ones say what they are derivable from. Two constexpr guards also INDEXED tables they assumed a guard above them had already checked — `builtin_rows_respect_the_creator_budgets()` (`ATTACKS_TABLE`, `CREATOR_STAT_POINTS_BY_STAGE`, `CREATOR_RARITY_BUDGET_BONUS`) and `species_learnsets_are_legal()` (`ATTACKS_TABLE`) — so deleting the named guard upstream surfaced as `array subscript … outside the bounds` and `non-constant condition for static assertion`, errors with no sentence in them. Both bound their indices first now, and it is measured: with `species_learnsets_resolve()`'s `static_assert` deleted and one move id set to 99, the compiler used to print an unnamed bounds error and now prints two named sentences and nothing else. With the guard intact it fires first, as it always did.
  - **A fourth hole in the completeness pass**, the same shape as the three recorded: item 9 *Llave Raíz* is `ITEM_KLASS_CARE` with `value` 0, i.e. a care item that restores nothing by `items_table.h`'s own unit contract. Its real role is the `EVOC_ITEM` key (`evolution.json`: species 53 → 54, cond `ITEM`, `cond_value` 9) and spec §24 names no evolution-item class. The generator now DERIVES that paragraph from the JSON so it cannot go stale, and `the_care_item_that_restores_nothing_is_an_evolution_key_with_no_class` pins both halves — proven failable by giving item 9 a value (2 failed checks) and by shipping an `EVOC_ITEM` rule (1 failed check, plus two other cases).
  - **Two sentences were overstated and are corrected in place.** `balance.h`'s `xp_table_total() < 65535` was called half of a "stronger pair": it is a floor no plausible curve trips (8,845 shipped, 36,453 for the rejected curve) and the sum is not what `PebbleInstance.xp` holds, so the file now says which half does the work. And "flash does not bind at any size" was true but read as "nothing binds": globals is the tighter budget — 70,348 of 90,000, **19,652 B free** against flash's 503,906 — and P4-C1 spent 0 B of it.
  - **Two findings were checked and needed no change**: `tools/content/_verify_out.txt` is written by every gate run but is gitignored with the reason on the line above it, and the em dashes in `tools/*.sh`, `tests/Makefile` and `game/daylight.cpp` are all pre-existing (`git diff ee08bbc b8b9f1f` touches none of them). The Spanish proper nouns in generated comments stay declared rather than removed: they identify which row is which, every character is Latin-1, and renaming them to ids would make the tables unreadable.

**P4-C2 Battle engine (pure)** — L
- [x] `game/battle.{h,cpp}`: teams of ≤ 3, one active each; round = collect both actions → validate → priority → effective speed (tie by the shared `Rng`) → resolve first/second → faint processing (forced switch) → status ticks → end check (§14 steps 1-9); switching consumes the turn; buffs/protection with durations; `RoundLog` ring; `battle_state_hash()`. **DONE** - the ring shipped as **`BattleLog` + `BattleEvent`** and there is no type called `RoundLog` in the tree (naming only; **narrowed in P4-C6**). The nine steps are nine functions carrying their numbers (`battle_s1_validate_action` … `battle_s9_check_victory`), called once each in order by a driver whose body is exactly those calls, so `grep -oE 'battle_s[1-9]_'` over it reads 1..9 — and `tools/check.sh` now greps that back out. `BattleState` is **212 B, compiled and measured** (`BattleCombatant` 32, `BattleSide` 100, `BattleEvent` 12), padding-free by a sum-of-members `static_assert` on every struct plus `offsetof` guards, pointer-free, heap-free, float-free. The module declares **no instance**, so P4-C2 spends 0 B of globals; the 212 B of state and the caller's log ring are P4-C4's to place and to measure.
- [x] `test_battle.cpp` — every §50 battle case (type advantage, damage, speed order, switching, fainting, buffs, protection, victory, invalid moves incl. unknown move / empty slot / acting for fainted / out of turn → rejected, state untouched) + `test_battle_replay.cpp` (two engines, same seed + actions → identical hash every round; replaying a log yields the same final state). **DONE, plus `test_battle_golden.cpp` over `tests/golden/battle_v1.txt`, which is not in this line and should be** — it is the only artifact that can catch a duration off-by-one both engines share, and the only one that watches the log at all (the round hash is structurally blind to it, because the log lives outside `BattleState`).
- Acceptance: gate. **MET.** `tools/check.sh` → `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1896094 globals=70348 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 26/26` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` → `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 variants at 0 project warnings. 70 new cases over 2,270 checks (`test_battle` 60/60, `test_battle_replay` 8/8, `test_battle_golden` 2/2), and the whole of `test_battle.cpp` also runs clean under `-fsanitize=address,undefined` — which is what backs the claim that no array is indexed before the range checks pass.
- **EVERY CASE WAS MADE TO FAIL. 75 mutations, 74 caught by a NAMED test or a NAMED `static_assert`, 1 provably unobservable and recorded as such.** The sweep is what this step is really for, and it found **eight real holes in the first version of these tests**, every one of them the project's recurring defect: (1) the `moves[i] == 0` guard was redundant with `attack_get()` and **could not fail** — it is deleted, and the case now covers both inputs through the one guard that remains; (2) the incoming half of the switch's position reset was covered for by the outgoing half — `a_pebble_that_walks_in_arrives_with_a_clean_position_of_its_own` was added; (3) the evasion-cap case rolled 35, which hits under BOTH the capped and the uncapped reading, so uncapping the gap changed nothing — it rolls 45 now, which sits between them; (4) step 5's corpse rule was only tested for a dead ACTOR, so deleting the dead-TARGET arm stayed green — `a_recoil_kill_leaves_the_second_action_with_a_corpse_to_skip` was added; (5) the DOT slot's refresh, (6) `EFF_CORRUPT`'s max-of-two, and (7) which side a DEBUFF lands on had no case at all; (8) `set_stage()`'s clamp was hidden by `battle_stat_eff()`'s second clamp, so dropping the stored clamp — a byte P4-C5 compares between peers — left everything green. THE ONE THAT STAYS UNCAUGHT AND WHY: `raw = max(DMG_MIN, …)` is unobservable given the second floor, because every shipped `TYPE_MUL` maps 0 to 0; deleting it changes no result and no test can tell. It is kept because it is `balance.h`'s published formula, and `battle.cpp` says in a comment that nothing claims it is tested.
- **THREE NEW GREP GATES, each proven failable before it landed** (the violation was added, the gate was watched to fail, the violation was removed): (1) **`src/game` may not include `Arduino.h`, `u8g2`, `gfx.h` or `render.h`** — `#include` lines only, so the five game headers that discuss the rule in prose do not trip it. **THIS GATE DID NOT EXIST**, contrary to the P4-C2 brief: `grep -n Arduino tools/check.sh` returned nothing, and purity was enforced only as a confusing compile error inside `make -C tests check`. (2) **`src/game/battle*` may not call `rng_u32|rng_below|rng_chance_permille|rng_seed|rng_seed_all|esp_random`** — the battle draws only from `BattleState.rng`, and this is the exact failure mode "P4-C3's AI reaches for `RNG_BATTLE` and the logs stop reproducing". `test_battle_replay.cpp` carries the runtime half: every named stream must hand back exactly what it would have without the battle. (3) **the nine step calls read 1..9 in order inside `battle_step_round()`** — deliberately narrow, and its comment says so: it catches a REORDERING and proves nothing about what each function does.
- **`balance.h` CORRECTED IN THE SAME COMMIT.** Its `TYPE_MOD_MAX_HITS` paragraph said "per attacker per battle" in one sentence and "P4-C2 owns one counter per side" in the next. `tools/content/sim_engine.py` keeps `tmod_used` on the attacking FIGHTER, so the ownership sentence was the wrong one; three further semantics come from the same code and are implemented rather than guessed (a miss does not spend the edge, a power-0 move does not spend it, and the cap zeroes a DISADVANTAGE as well as an advantage). The rejected reading — cap the bonus, always apply the penalty — is recorded with the note that it is the better game-design argument and is **not what was measured**.
- **FIVE NAMED DIVERGENCES FROM `sim_engine.py`, in `battle.h` where a reader will look**: priority is honoured here and ignored there; buff stages expire here and are permanent there; the timeout cross-multiplies here (per `balance.h`) and sums raw HP there; a tie is an honest `BO_DRAW` here and a coin flip there; a forced replacement costs the turn here (P4-C5's lockstep has exactly one ACTION per side per round and §15 has no replacement message) and is free there. **THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED: the 40–63 % / 57–65 % win-rate numbers are a measured claim about the Python model, not about `battle.cpp`, and nobody may quote them as if they were measured here.** Re-running the simulator with priority, stage durations and the cross-multiplied timeout is the honest fix and it may move the roster.
- **TWO DECISIONS WRITTEN BESIDE THEIR CONSTANTS, because a reasonable reader could pick the opposite and two peers that shipped different answers would desync from round 1.** (a) `BATTLE_SWITCH_PRIORITY` = +6: §14 says "switching costs the turn" and says nothing about when it resolves, and a switch resolving second would make the phrase meaningless. A `constexpr` guard fails the build if a content pack ever ships a move at or above it. (b) Stun is the ONE duration the status tick does not own — it is armed at the end of the user's own turn, so a step-7 decrement in the same round would expire it before it ever skipped anything; it is spent by the action it prevents, exactly as `sim_engine.py` does it.
- **CARRIED, NOT CLOSED.** (1) `CORRUPT_BATTLE_INFECT_PERMILLE` and attack 12's `effect_value 35` have **no consumer**, and `battle.h` names them rather than leaving the absence implicit: the tuned simulator has no passive-infection roll, and adding a draw the tuning never saw is the cheapest possible source of a version-to-version desync. (2) **No shipped learnset can reach `ATK_EFF_DOT`** — attack 13's only species is 46, outside the 36-species prefix — so the golden transcript cannot cover it and `test_battle.cpp` places the move by hand; raising `ROSTER_FAMILIES` fixes it. (3) The engine costs **0 B of the shipped image today, and that is measured rather than assumed**: `arduino-cli` compiles `src/game/battle.cpp` (so its `static_assert`s fire and it must be clean under `--warnings all`), the object has 0 `.data` and 0 `.bss`, and `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` finds **zero `battle_*` symbols in the linked ELF** - nothing calls it, so `--gc-sections` drops the lot and flash is byte-identical to P4-C1's 1,896,094 on all 7 variants. The code size was quoted here as 8,533 B, which was the Berkeley `size` `text` column at this commit and includes `.rodata` for content tables the linker shares with every other user (they are COMDAT groups); **the marginal figure P4-C4 should budget from is the `size -A` `.text` sum, 6,030 B as of the P4-C2/C3 follow-up**, against 503,906 B of flash headroom; the 212 B of state plus a log ring are its RAM decision and must be measured there, not carried forward from this sentence.
- **DELIBERATELY DEFERRED, with the reason**: `battle_log_apply()` — reconstructing the state from log ENTRIES — is a second implementation of the state transition, and the one thing worse than no replay is a replay that agrees with itself. `battle_replay()` re-runs the recorded ACTIONS through the one engine instead, which is what the plan line asks for; entry-level desync localisation belongs to P4-C5 if it earns it.

**P4-C3 Battle AI** — S
- [x] `game/battle_ai.cpp`: greedy expected damage with type mod; switch when HP < 25 % and a better type is benched; never invalid; own `Rng` stream. `test_battle_ai.cpp`. **DONE.** Every clause verified: the score is `2 * battle_damage_pre_roll() + (DMG_RNG_SPAN - 1)` half-points times `battle_accuracy_eff()`, both of them the ENGINE's own functions rather than a second copy; rule 3 fires only below a strict 25 % of `hp_max` **and** with a strictly better-typed bench member; `BattleAi` carries its own 8 B `Rng` and `battle_ai_choose()` takes a **`const BattleState&`**, so a draw from the battle stream does not compile.
- **NEVER INVALID IS STRUCTURAL, NOT CAREFUL.** The AI does not decide legality at all: it enumerates the `PB_MOVE_COUNT + BATTLE_TEAM_MAX` actions that exist, hands each to `battle_validate_action()`, and may return only one that came back `BR_OK`. There is therefore no second copy of any legality rule to drift from the engine's, and a forced replacement is not a special case but the branch where the validator refused every attack.
- **A FOURTH GREP GATE**, proven failable in both the `.cpp` and the `.h`: **no function in `game/battle_ai.*` may take a mutable `BattleState&`**. Narrow on purpose — it matches DECLARATIONS only and proves nothing about what the AI does with the const reference it gets.
- **A NEW TUNABLE IN `data/balance.h`, NOT IN `battle_ai.h`**: `BATTLE_AI_SWITCH_HP_PCT 25`. Unlike the three other numbers `balance.h` carries that the content pack does not, retuning it changes **no hashed state and no wire format** — the AI is a local action PRODUCER whose choices reach a peer only as the two bytes every action travels as — so it may move without a `BATTLE_ENGINE_VER` bump.
- **THREE THINGS P4-C4 INHERITS AND MUST NOT REDISCOVER.** (1) `battle_submit_action(st, ai.side, battle_ai_choose(ai, st))` is the whole call site; there is deliberately no `battle_ai_submit()`, because it would have to take a mutable `BattleState&`. (2) `battle_ai_choose()` returns `{BACT_NONE, 0}` when NOTHING is legal — a value the validator itself refuses by name — so a caller must not forward it blindly; in a real battle the only two states that produce it are "the battle is over" and "this side already submitted". (3) **No win-rate figure anywhere applies to this chooser**: `tools/content/sim_engine.py`, which the 40-63 % roster matrix came from, has no AI at all — `battle_3v3()` scripts both sides — so nothing has ever measured how a greedy chooser fares against the tuned roster. What IS measured is only that both sides win somewhere across 64 fights.
- **RULE 3 CANNOT FIRE FOR A SINGLE-FAMILY TEAM**, and `battle_ai.h` now says so: "strictly better typed" needs a bench member of a different type, and every shipped family is mono-type. Census over 896 roster AI-vs-AI battles: it fired 1,012 times in 25,914 side-rounds (3.9 %), and **all nine mono-type-vs-mono-type cells of that grid are zero**.

**P4-C2/C3 follow-up** — S
- [x] Six blocking findings from the adversarial review, each fixed and each mutation-proven. **DONE.**
- **THE VERSION GUARANTEE `battle.h` SOLD WAS FALSE.** The header promised that a peer on different RULES could never match a hash because `BATTLE_ENGINE_VER` rode in the FNV basis; the basis carried `BATTLE_HASH_VERSION` instead, so bumping the engine version — the one thing the next sentence orders a maintainer to do — moved no hash at all. Not an argument: at commit 9997ed4 the macro appeared in `battle.cpp` exactly three times, and all three were the setup-carried version check, so it reached the hash nowhere. All three version words now go through the FNV step BYTE BY BYTE rather than being XORed in, which also removes a real collision: `(hash 1, content 0x5B4A)` and `(3, 0x5B48)` both folded to `0x5B4B`. `battle_hash_basis()` is parameterised so a host test can prove each word reaches the hash without recompiling the engine three times.
- **`battle_init()` ACCEPTED A MOVE THE SPECIES CANNOT LEARN** (spec §67). It checked only that the four ids resolved, so a peer-supplied team could hand any species any of the 34 attacks. **Decisive, and measured rather than assumed**: a species-1 Paketo that wins 0 of 200 scripted 1v1 seeds against species 17 wins **100** with attack 11 Plaga written into slot 0 — and **103** with the ON-TYPE attack 3 Rafaga, so a type-only rule would have stopped nothing. The rule is the closure of the only two writers of `PebbleInstance.moves[]` in the tree (`box.cpp` and `migration.cpp` both memcpy a learnset whole, and `evolution.cpp` deliberately leaves it alone): the moveset must be the **verbatim learnset of some species in the same family at a stage ≤ this one's**. Not per-species membership, which would refuse every evolved Pebble. New reject code `BR_UNLEARNABLE_MOVE`; `BATTLE_ENGINE_VER` bumped to 2 because init now refuses what it used to accept.
- **THREE TESTS THAT COULD NOT FAIL, the seventh, eighth and ninth instances of this project's named recurring defect.** (1) `protection_halves_after_the_roll_and_never_below_one` had no case for the half of its own NAME: deleting the `DMG_MIN` floor inside the protection branch left the whole suite green. (2) The two `DMG_MIN` floors in `battle_damage_pre_roll` **mutually masked** — every case reached only a NEUTRAL matchup, where `TYPE_MUL` is 1/1 and the two are indistinguishable, so deleting either alone was green; a DISADVANTAGE separates them, and without the second floor a disadvantaged minimum hit deals literally nothing. (3) `a_buff_stage_clamps_at_two_and_a_debuff_can_never_wrap_the_stat` used base ATK 3, where `3 + BUFF_STAGE_MIN` is 1 — which is also `STAT_EFF_MIN`, so the LATER floor answered and the clamp the test names was never reached. Each fix was verified by breaking the guard and watching the named test fail.
- **A FIFTH GREP GATE: the firmware's tuning must match the content pack's.** Thirteen numbers live in both `tools/content/balance.json` (which feeds `CONTENT_VERSION`, and which `sim_engine.py` tuned the roster against) and `src/data/balance.h` (which the firmware compiles, and which is NOT generated). Nothing compared them, so a header-only retune moved hashed battle state with **no `CONTENT_VERSION` and no `BATTLE_ENGINE_VER` change to say so** — re-measured on this tree: with `BUFF_STAGE_MAX` edited from `+2` to `+3` in the header and nowhere else, `make -C tests check` printed `ALL PASS 27/27` and `gen_content.py --check` printed "8 files in sync, CONTENT_VERSION 0x5B4A", unchanged. Every clamp assertion in the tree is written in terms of the constant under test, so no literal pins it. Proven failable three ways (a scalar, an array element, a renamed macro) and restored.
- **FOUR OF THE NINE STEPS INDEXED `team[active]` WITHOUT RANGE-CHECKING IT.** `battle.h` advertises the steps as individually callable and P4-C5 hands in state this engine did not build; with `side[1].active = 3`, `battle_s3_determine_order()` is a **stack-buffer-overflow read** past the end of the 212 B `BattleState` under `-fsanitize=address`. Not reachable through `battle_step_round()` — step 1 refuses it as `BR_EMPTY_ACTIVE` and the driver aborts — so the guards are labelled in the code as untested defence in depth rather than left looking proven. All four battle binaries are clean under `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all`.
- **FOUR SENTENCES WIDER THAN THE TREE, corrected against measurement.** (1) `battle_ai.h` claimed the protected-defender estimate "preserves the ranking"; it does not — 757 of 233,280 plain-roster protected move pairs (0.32 %) invert, worst case 5.9 % of the better move's expected damage. The conservative half of the claim held: the estimate is never more than 2/6 of a point from the engine's exact expectation. (2) The comment defending step 8's second fainting pass said a DOT-killed Pebble "never yields a victory"; `combatant_alive()` requires `hp_cur > 0`, so the victory is found either way — what actually goes missing is the `BCF_FAINTED` flag P4-C5 compares and the transcript's FAINT event. (3) `battle_ai_move_score()`'s "widest reachable" 89,400 is an arithmetic CEILING; the widest the shipped roster reaches is **34,170**. (4) The two object-size figures were quoted side by side from two different measures; both are now `size -A` `.text` sums with the measure named — `battle_ai.cpp.o` 1,148 B, `battle.cpp.o` 6,030 B, with the generated tables in COMDAT groups so adding the `.rodata` would over-count.
- **`BATTLE_AI_VER` HAS NO CONSUMER** and is now labelled as such, the treatment `battle.h` gives `CORRUPT_BATTLE_INFECT_PERMILLE`. A dead `DMG_MIN` floor in `battle_ai.cpp` — provably dead, not merely untested — was **deleted** rather than labelled, because a floor that reads as though it does something is worse than no floor.
- **A LIMIT OF THE HASH, WRITTEN DOWN.** Swapping steps 6 and 7 leaves every round hash byte-identical across a whole battle, because the status tick does not consult `BCF_FAINTED`. `tests/golden/battle_v1.txt` and the third battle grep gate are the only things holding that boundary, and `battle.h` now says so beside the determinism argument.

**P4-C4a Make a species visible (the P3-C3 carried obligation)** — S
- [x] The atlas stops being keyed on the genome. `data/sprites.h`: `sprite_form_of()` takes an ART KEY instead of a `Genome` (which also removes its `GN_GET()` reach-through into the genome layout), `sprite_set_id()` loses its `species` parameter entirely, and `sprite_design_of(art_key, stage)` folds the key into the pool the stage draws from — `% SPRITE_BABY_BODIES` (8) at BABY, `% SPRITE_ADULT_BODIES` (6) at ADULT/SENIOR. New `ui/pet_art.h` holds the ONE resolution, `species_get(species_id)->sprite_id` else `gene_species`, plus `pet_species_name()`. `ui/pet_view.cpp` re-derives `form` in `pet_view_attach()`, which is where `species_id` arrives; `ui/screen_home.cpp` folds the SAME key through the SAME function for the still body. `ui/petfx.cpp` is mechanical — three call sites lose an argument and `s_qry_species` is gone — and still includes neither `sim.h` nor `genome.h`, nor `pet_art.h`. **Why `pet_art.h` is not part of `pet_view.h`:** both animators include `pet_view.h`, and the gate greps for `sim.h`/`genome.h` BY FILENAME, so `species_table.h` could have walked into petfx behind a passing gate.
- [x] A safe fallback for species 0, an unresolvable id and the custom 200..209 range, **bit-identical to the pre-change render**. `% 8` and `% 6` on the genome nibble are literally the old `species & 7` and `% SPRITE_ADULT_BODIES`; `a_pebble_with_no_species_row_draws_what_it_always_did` compares against a written-out copy of the old two functions over 13 no-row ids x gene 0..15 x 6 stages x 7 minor forms x 4 poses.
- [x] The species NAME reaches a screen. `ui_pet_name()` is nickname → `STR_SPC_NAME_*` → dynasty syllables, so HOME, the PEBBLE header and the EVOLVE ceremony all name the creature; `ui/screen_box.cpp`'s nicknameless rows stopped answering `S_SPECIES(gene_species)`, the 16-word pre-roster vocabulary that called ten different creatures SETA.
- [x] **`pet_view_fill()` DELETED, not wired up.** No caller in `Pebblebol/src` for three phases; its stage ladder was not "the same rule `sim_bind()` uses" its own comment claimed (`>= 20 ADULT / >= 10 TEEN / >= 4 CHILD` against `sim.cpp`'s `>= 20 SENIOR / >= 15 ADULT / >= 10 TEEN / >= 5 CHILD`, and it could never return `STAGE_SENIOR`); and its evolution case "proved" a changed body by feeding `evo_state & 3` in as `minor_form`, i.e. by drawing the pet as badly cared for. `PetView.hp_pct` went with it — that function alone wrote it and nothing read it. A stored Pebble needs an art key and a stage, both one expression wide; a second stage ladder that disagrees with the simulation is not worth keeping for a caller that does not exist.
- [x] **THREE TESTS THAT COULD NOT FAIL — the tenth, eleventh and twelfth instances of this project's recurring defect — replaced rather than deleted.** `every_species_row_points_at_a_real_sprite_set` existed BYTE-IDENTICALLY in `test_content.cpp` and `test_evolution.cpp`, and both asserted that `SPR_BABY_BLOB + sprite_id` was arithmetically in range while **nothing in the tree evaluated that sum to draw anything**: species 25..36 would have resolved onto GHOST, TOMB and the ten pose sets, and both copies passed. `test_content.cpp` now resolves every row at every stage through `sprite_set_id()` and asserts a CREATURE body, and states as a number (12, first at species 25) how much of the roster the naive sum would mis-draw; `test_evolution.cpp` takes the question that file owns — does a rule change the body — over all 24 rules. The third was `test_pet_view.cpp`'s care-percentage case, which was cited as proof of the CareId/StatId mapping while driving the fill that has no mapping in it; it drives the live one now, through five DISTINCT `sim_god_set_stat()` values with the distinctness asserted, because five equal stats make any permutation pass.
- [x] Goldens: `home_starter` and `home_maxed` moved (species 1 → `sprite_id` 0 → `SPR_ADULT_BOLOTA`, where the genome nibble 4 used to give `SPR_ADULT_MOHO`; 406 pixels each, all inside the 40x40 body box), `box_list` moved (220 pixels: three slots renamed SETA → Paketo), and `home_species` is NEW — the same genome and a DIFFERENT `species_id`, which before this step was pixel-identical to `home_starter`. Its case asserts the two frames differ AND that every differing pixel is inside the body box, so the pair cannot become two files nobody diffs. No other golden changed; `home_no_pebble` was re-recorded and came back byte-identical.
- Acceptance: gate; matrix. **MET.** `tools/check.sh` → `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1896258 globals=70348 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 27/27` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` → `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 at 0 project warnings. **+164 B of flash, 0 B of globals.** No art was added: `SPRITE_DATA_BYTES` stays 10,623 of its 14,336 budget, and 36 new 24x24x2 sets would have been 15,807 — over it. Art is P10's.
- **NINE MUTATIONS, NINE NAMED FAILURES, AND ONE OF MY OWN CLAIMS REFUTED.** (1) `pet_art_key()` ignoring `species_id` → `the_species_and_not_the_genome_chooses_the_body` (6), `every_evolution_reaches_the_body` (73), `an_evolution_changes_the_creature_the_live_view_draws` (2), plus `snapshot_home`, `snapshot_home_maxed` and `snapshot_home_species`. (2a) `SPRITE_BABY_BODIES` 8→1 → `every_evolution_rule_changes_the_body` (24) and `every_evolution_reaches_the_body` (25). (2b) `SPRITE_ADULT_BODIES` 6→1 → the same two (48 / 49). (3) the BABY fold using the adult pool (`% 6`) → `a_pebble_with_no_species_row_draws_what_it_always_did` (2,730). (4) restoring the naive `SPR_BABY_BLOB + sprite_id` resolution → the **named `static_assert` `species_bodies_are_creatures`** fails the build first; with that assert disarmed, `every_species_row_draws_a_creature_body` (14), `every_evolution_rule_changes_the_body` (40), `every_evolution_reaches_the_body` (41). (5) `pet_species_name()` answering `S(STR_EMPTY)` instead of `nullptr` → `the_species_name_is_the_rosters_own` (4). (6) the BOX rows back on `S_SPECIES(gene_species)` → `snapshot_box_list`. (7) `SPRITE_BODY_LAST` widened to `SPR_EAT_ADULT` → `the_naive_sprite_sum_would_mis_draw_a_third_of_the_roster` (2). (8) `kStatOfCare[]` with HEALTH and ENERGY swapped → `care_percentages_are_care_id_indexed` (2). (9) `apply_species_design()` spending CHILD/TEEN as well → `the_species_and_not_the_genome_chooses_the_body` (2). **THE REFUTED CLAIM WAS MINE AND IT IS CORRECTED IN THE TEST FILES, NOT ONLY HERE**: the first draft of both evolution cases said they would fail at `SPRITE_BABY_BODIES` 8→3 or the adult pool 6→2. Measured, they do not — every shipped rule is a SINGLE step between CONSECUTIVE art keys (`sprite_id == id - 1`, and a family's three stages are three consecutive ids), so `k % P != (k+1) % P` for every pool size except 1. A pool of 3 IS caught, by `a_pebble_with_no_species_row_draws_what_it_always_did` at 3,549 checks; it is just not caught where the comment said. Both comments now name the mutation each case really fails on, and say which case covers the one it does not.

The case asserts the DISTRIBUTION now (at most `ceil(36/8) = 5` species per body, and the unfolded resolution puts 29 on one), which is what the fold is actually for.

- [x] **A BATTLE THAT WAS ALREADY WON PAID NOTHING ON THE WRONG EXIT — the one real defect in P4-C4, and it was an under-award rather than a double-award.** The engine decides the outcome inside `battle_step_round()` while the victory transcript is still playing (the last blow, the faint and the `BATTLE_END` line are four more beats, ~2.4 s), and `battle_leave()` reported a flat `report_once(0u)` for that whole window. `LONG_BOTH` — the global HOME invariant, and `SCR_BATTLE` carries no `SF_LOCK_INPUT` — or a hatch ceremony arriving there turned a WIN into a loss and paid no XP, while B in the identical state ran `end_playback()` and paid. **Measured: ALL 64 of 64 seeded practice battles sit decided-but-unreported in that window, and the 35 of them that were WINS were the ones the old code mis-reported (the other 29 were losses, which a flat `report_once(0u)` happens to get right).** *(Corrected in P4-C6: this said "35 of 64 ... sit decided-but-unreported", which moved the win count onto the population. The test's own line is `64 of 64 battles were decided mid-transcript; 35 won, 29 lost`; the under-award count of 35 was and is right.)* `battle_leave()` reports `won_now()`, the same expression `end_playback()` uses; an abandoned battle is still `BO_UNDECIDED` and still pays nothing, which was the intent the old code did not implement.

What changed is the claim: `every_rule_measured_at_the_level_it_actually_fires_at` binds a Pebble at each rule's own level and reads the stage the SIMULATION derives (rather than restating the ladder, which is the defect that got `pet_view_fill()` deleted), then asserts 12 / 12, the NAME moving for all 24, and the body moving for all 24 once ADULT is reached. Line 507, obligation 6 and the §67 "Evolution works" line all carry the number.

A migrated pet with no typed name arrives unnamed now, `migrate_default_name()` is DELETED with its only call site, and a v1 owner who did type a name still keeps it.

**P4-C5 Local protocol codec, session FSM, lockstep battle protocol, shared validator, fault-injecting loopback (no radio)** — L
- [x] `networking/protocol.{h,cpp}` (§1.4 header, §15 message set incl. `TEAM_SUBMIT` 3 x **48 B** wire instance - the box said 64 and the shipped `PBW_BYTES` is 48, pinned by `static_assert(PBW_BYTES == TR_WIRE_BYTES)`; **narrowed in P4-C6**), `networking/session.{h,cpp}` (HELLO → CAPABILITIES → SESSION_REQUEST/ACCEPT with shared seed → op → GOODBYE; **9 x 1000 ms** timeouts → `SESSION_LOST` (`PROTO_RETX_MS` x `PROTO_RETX_MAX`, `session.h:132-133`; the box said 3 s x 3, **narrowed in P4-C6**); seq window), `networking/battle_link.cpp` (lockstep: both send ACTION, both resolve locally with the shared seed, exchange `ROUND_RESULT{round, hash}`; mismatch → `BATTLE_END(DESYNC)`, no rewards; `TEAM_SUBMIT` → `validate_team` on both sides, `TEAM_VALIDATION` carries the verdict), `game/validate.{h,cpp}` (§15 reject list; reused by the SAVE LOAD path - **the creator is not a consumer yet and this box said it was**: `ui/screen_creator.cpp` makes no validation call, `networking/webui.cpp` registers no upload route, and `data/creator_schema.h` enforces budgets at compile time over the built-in roster only. `validate.h:52-59` already said so; the box did not. **P8-C3 is the chunk that adds the creator call site**; narrowed in P4-C6), `networking/transport.h` + `transport_loopback.cpp` (two in-process endpoints; configurable drop %, duplicate %, reorder window).

The work is real and the label was not; **narrowed in P4-C6**).

- Acceptance: gate; §67 "Invalid peers cannot inject illegal data" **proven on the HOST, which is the half P4-C5 owns**; loopback fault run green. **MET, and narrowed in P4-C6 to agree with the §67 tracker below.** The line used to read as if the acceptance criterion itself were met; the tracker at "Invalid peers cannot inject illegal data" is unticked and names P7-C3, and the tracker is right - `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` finds no `proto_`, `session_`, `pbw_` or `battle_link` symbol in the ELF, so no peer, valid or invalid, has ever reached a device. Three layers are proven here and each is a host proof: `proto_decode()` total over any buffer in eleven pinned steps with thirteen named errors; `pbw_decode()` filling a LOCAL, deriving `evo_state` from its own tables and copying out only on `VR_OK`; and `battle_validate_action()` taking a `const BattleState&`, so a refused action provably cannot write - `Rng` lives inside the state and `rng_next()` will not bind.

**P4-C5a (the first half: the shared validator and the codec) — LANDED.** `game/validate.{h,cpp}` and `networking/protocol.{h,cpp}` with `tests/test_validate.cpp` (24 cases) and `tests/test_protocol.cpp` (24 cases). `session.{h,cpp}`, `battle_link.cpp`, `transport.h`, `transport_loopback.cpp`, `test_session.cpp` and `docs/protocol.md` are the SECOND half and are NOT in that commit; the three boxes above were ticked by P4-C5b below.
- **THE WIRE PEBBLE IS 48 B, NOT THE 64 B THIS PLAN ASKED FOR, AND 64 IS NOT AVAILABLE.** `TR_WIRE_BYTES` is 48 and it lives inside `PendingTrade`, a PERSISTED 64 B blob pinned by four `static_assert`s at `save_schema.h:397-400`, whose own header rule is that a persisted layout is never edited in place. A 64 B record would either not fit the trade journal or would force a `SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump plus a migration for the one blob whose purpose is surviving a power cut. §15's "the same validator" sentence makes the battle record and the trade record one object, so choosing 64 now would mean two wire forms across time. `static_assert(PBW_BYTES == TR_WIRE_BYTES)` is the enforcement; `TEAM_SUBMIT` is therefore 148 B and a frame is 164 B against ESP-NOW's 250.
- **"AN evo_state THAT DISAGREES WITH ITS SPECIES ROW" CANNOT BE SENT AT ALL, so it is answered by INEXPRESSIBILITY rather than by a reject code.** `evo_state` is exactly derivable — the stage bits are `species_get(id)->stage` and the PENDING bit is `evolution_level_ready()`, a pure function of species_id and level — so the byte where it would have travelled is reserved and must be zero, and the receiver computes the field from its OWN tables. `the_evolution_state_is_unrepresentable_on_the_wire_rather_than_checked` pins both halves: a sender whose own stage bits are wrong produces a byte-identical record, and poking that reserved byte is `VR_WIRE_RESERVED`. The reject codes `VR_BAD_EVO_STAGE` / `VR_BAD_EVO_PENDING` stay live on the SAVE path, where the `box.cpp` bug proved they fire on real data.
- **THE CREATOR IS NOT A CONSUMER YET AND THE PLAN'S WORDING WAS WIDER THAN THE TREE.** This step's brief says the creator "validates inline today". MEASURED at `ee75076`: `ui/screen_creator.cpp` is 213 lines of QR/portal screen with zero Pebble validation (`grep -c valid` == 0), `networking/webui.cpp` registers no upload route, and `data/creator_schema.h` enforces its budgets at COMPILE TIME over the built-in roster. There is no inline creator rule to absorb; `validate.cpp` BECOMES the custom-Pebble validator and P8 wires the call. `validate.h` says "the wire, the save path and the constructor" and names the creator as P8's.
- **THE CONSTRUCTOR DOES NOT CALL THE VALIDATOR, AND THE REASON IS A MEASUREMENT.** Making `box_new_pebble()` refuse a slot that fails validation reads well and would break four shipped host tests: it has never required a SEALED genome, and `tests/test_box.cpp:22`, `tests/test_box_sim.cpp:50`, `tests/test_care.cpp:288` and `tests/test_screens.cpp:1300` all hand it a zeroed or hand-built one (`lineage_id` 0 and/or a wrong CRC, i.e. `VR_BAD_GENOME`). What ships instead is the one-line `evo_state` fix the constructor was missing plus `a_constructed_pebble_validates`, which drives it with a real sealed genome over all 36 species.
- **FORTY-TWO MUTATIONS, FORTY-ONE CAUGHT BY A NAMED TEST, ONE PROVABLY UNOBSERVABLE AND RECORDED AS SUCH.** Every `VReject` guard, every `ProtoErr` guard, the CRC span, the endianness, the `evo_state` derivation, the `memset` on reject, the quarantine call and the `box.cpp` line were each broken in turn. Three of them (the learnset walk, `VR_LEVEL_OUT_OF_BAND`, the session check) first failed to COMPILE under `-Werror`, which is a weaker result than a red test, so each was re-run in a form that still compiles and each then failed a named case. **UNOBSERVABLE:** lowering `proto_decode`'s step-1 floor from `PROTO_FRAME_MIN` (16) to 14 changes nothing a test can see — a 14- or 15-byte buffer still reads only `buf[12..13]`, which is in bounds, and then fails step 3 with `PE_LEN`, which the truncation sweep already accepts as a legal answer. Removing the floor ENTIRELY is caught twice: by `every_codec_error_is_reachable_and_has_a_distinct_name` in the ordinary build (`PE_SHORT` becomes unreachable) and, under ASan, as a `heap-buffer-overflow READ of size 1 ... in proto_decode ... protocol.cpp:356` from `the_decoder_reads_nothing_past_the_byte_count_the_transport_gave_it`.
- **SANITIZERS: 30/30 host binaries clean under `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all`**, with `null,nonnull-attribute,returns-nonnull-attribute` disabled for a PRE-EXISTING reason that has nothing to do with this commit: on GCC 13.3 those three make `species_get(...) == nullptr` non-constant inside `data/evolution_table.h`'s `evo_rules_are_well_formed()`, so `game/evolution.cpp` alone fails to COMPILE under plain `-fsanitize=undefined` at `ee75076` too. Clang 18 compiles it but has no ASan runtime installed here. Not added to `tools/check.sh`; run by hand.
- Acceptance for this half: `tools/check.sh` -> `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1915696 globals=72676 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 30/30` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` -> `MATRIX OK`. **+782 B of flash and +24 B of globals**, all of it the SAVE PATH: `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` finds `validate_pebble` (514 B) in the ELF and ZERO `proto_`/`pbw_` symbols, so the codec is gc-sectioned out entirely and costs the device nothing until the second half wires it. The globals are `s_quarantine_why` (10 B) and `s_quarantine_mask` (2 B); the other 12 B is `.bss` placement and is not attributed further.

**P4-C5b (the second half: the session FSM, the lockstep and the fault-injecting loopback) — LANDED.** `networking/session.{h,cpp}` (nine states, a 40 B abort record, an 8 B caller-owned event ring), `networking/battle_link.cpp` (the lockstep, holding NO `BattleState` and NO `BattleSetup` of its own), `networking/transport.h` + `transport_loopback.cpp` (the seam plus two in-process endpoints with configurable drop / duplicate / reorder-window percentages and a scripted "kill the next N frames of this named type" fault, all from ONE seeded `Rng`), `tests/test_session.cpp` (36 cases, 1,053 checks) and `docs/protocol.md`.
- **THE LADDER IS 9 x 1000 ms, NOT THE PLAN'S 3 x 3000 ms, AND THE MEASUREMENT — NOT THE ESTIMATE — IS WHAT CHOSE IT.** Same nine-second deadline. Changing only those two constants over the same 500 seeds per arm: 10 % drop **483/500 vs 500/500**; 10 % drop+dup+reorder **482/500 vs 500/500**; the harsh arm (30 % drop, 10 % duplicate, 20 % reorder) **108/500 vs 474/500**. The design's arithmetic predicted that *most* battles would abort at three rungs (240 obligations x 6.9e-3); the truth is 3.4 %, because the answer-from-state rules mean an obligation rarely spends its own ladder alone — a peer that is behind PULLS what it needs from the peer that is ahead. `session.h` carries the table instead of the estimate.
- **A RETRANSMISSION ALLOCATES A FRESH `seq`, AGAINST THE AGREED DESIGN'S "reused verbatim", because the two rules cannot both hold.** The plan asks for a window that drops duplicates; if a retransmission reused its seq the window could not tell a transport duplicate from a retransmission and would discard exactly the frames the ladder depends on. Message identity is carried by CONTENT instead — which the FSM needs anyway, since a radio can duplicate a frame the sender never repeated. `PF_RETX` stays diagnostic-only. `protocol.h`'s paragraph was corrected rather than left to contradict the code.
- **THREE DEFECTS THE ACCEPTANCE RUN FOUND IN THE AGREED DESIGN ITSELF, each measured before and after.** (1) A retransmitted ACTION stamped with the CURRENT round's open hash instead of its own round's: 263 `SD_OPEN_HASH` desyncs in 500 trials at 10 % drop, **on a link that never corrupted a byte**. (2) A `ROUND_RESULT` that arrived BEFORE we had resolved that round was recorded and never matched, so the agreement barrier only ever looked forward: a link that dropped nothing and only REORDERED ended **472 of 500** trials in `SE_LOST`. (3) A message from an earlier PHASE was dropped as out-of-state, so a CAPABILITIES that overtook its HELLO stranded an honest pair — the design gave the answer-from-state rule to battle rounds only, and it is needed for the handshake too. All three are now zero.
- **TWO HOLES IN THE PROTOCOL AS DESIGNED, both found by a test and both now named rules.** (a) `battle_init()` refuses `BR_DUPLICATE_ID` across ALL SIX members, so a peer that echoed one of OUR Pebble ids reached the engine and came back as `SD_INTERNAL` — this device reporting a bug in `game/validate.cpp` for a lie the peer told. There is now a cross-team id check that answers `VR_DUPLICATE_ID` and blames the peer. (b) The sequence window slid forward to whatever seq it was shown, so **one injected frame carrying seq 0x7000 deafened the endpoint permanently** — every real frame afterwards was stale. `SESSION_SEQ_MAX_JUMP 64` refuses a jump no honest peer can make WITHOUT moving the window.
- **AGREEING IS NOT LEAVING, and the two-generals asymmetry is 88x smaller for it.** Closing as soon as both final hashes matched paid one side and not the other in **88 of 500** trials at 10 % drop and in **18 of 500 on a link that only reordered** — the peer's GOODBYE could overtake its BATTLE_END, or its BATTLE_END could be lost once with nobody left to repeat it. The endpoint now commits rewards on the comparison and STAYS in `SS_ENDING` until the peer's GOODBYE, so the asymmetry needs nine consecutive losses in one direction: **0 in all 3,000 acceptance trials**.
- **THE ACCEPTANCE CENSUS, 500 trials per arm, every trial a pure function of one printable seed.** clean 500/500; 10 % duplicate 500/500; 10 % reorder (window 4) 500/500; 10 % drop 500/500; 10 % of all three together 500/500; **the harsh arm — 30 % drop, 10 % duplicate, 20 % reorder — 474 completed, 25 clean `SE_LOST` and 1 half-paid** (471 / 28 / 1 after the P4-C5 follow-up closed the R4 hole below). `LE_SILENT` (both paid but the two 212 B states differ) is **0 in all 3,000**. The harsh arm exists because the 10 % arms no longer abort at all, and an acceptance run in which every trial completed would leave "or aborts cleanly" untested — so the census asserts BOTH halves, on the arm that actually shows them.
- **ONE BRANCH OF THE AGREED DESIGN IS DELETED BECAUSE NO TEST COULD HOLD ITS CLAIM.** `PROTO_PROBE_AT` sent a BATTLE_STATE alongside the retransmission from rung 3, to "turn a nine-second silence into an early NAMED desync". It cannot: an outstanding ACTION already carries this round's open hash (a mismatch is `SD_OPEN_HASH` before any probe), an outstanding ROUND_RESULT already carries both of that round's hashes, and the third arm of the ladder sends a BATTLE_STATE anyway. Its one MEASURED effect was liveness, not detection — the harsh arm moved from 487 to 474 completions of 500 when it went, and 500/500 either way at the stated fault level — and the 13 trials it cost are recorded rather than rounded away. `SD_PROBE` itself stays live and now has a named case (`an_injected_state_that_disagrees_is_a_named_desync_and_never_a_silent_one`), which also demonstrates the bound on a forged frame: it can burn the battle by name and it cannot move one byte of our state.
- **THIRTY-SIX MUTATIONS, ALL THIRTY-SIX CAUGHT BY A NAMED TEST.** The four the brief named: the sequence window (accepting a duplicate → `a_duplicated_frame_is_dropped_by_the_window_and_changes_nothing`), the timeout (never expiring, and expiring after one rung → `every_state_that_can_lose_its_peer_says_so_by_name_and_pays_nobody`), the hash comparison (`hash_after` → `a_forged_round_hash_ends_the_battle_by_name_and_pays_nobody`, `hash_before` → `a_forged_input_hash_names_the_inputs_and_not_the_rules`), and the team validation (dropping `validate_team` → `a_team_the_peer_could_not_have_raised_is_refused_by_the_right_code`). **Four of the thirty-six were NOT caught on the first pass and each got a new case rather than a note**: the round-1 agreement barrier, `validate_team` itself (every per-member vector was already caught by `pbw_decode`, so it took a team carrying one Pebble twice), the final-hash comparison, and the loopback's own reordering. One mutation first failed to COMPILE under `-Werror` (an unused `band_acceptable`) and was re-run in a still-compiling form.
- **BUDGET: 0 B of flash and 0 B of globals, MEASURED.** Nothing in `app/` or `ui/` references the new modules, so `--gc-sections` drops them: `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` on the ELF finds **zero** `session_*`, `link_*`, `proto_*`, `pbw_*`, `transport_loopback` or `loopback_*` symbols, and all seven `build_matrix.sh` variants are byte-identical to `66993bb` (baseline 1,915,696 / 72,676). When P7 wires it: `Session` is **340 B on the device** (the 360 recorded here was the x86-64 HOST figure and the follow-up below corrects it) — it already contains the 40 B `SessionEnd` and the 144 B of frozen wire records — plus a 16-entry event ring at 128 B, **468 B**, all caller-owned. `LoopbackLink` is 8,020 B and is test-only; nothing on the device declares one.
- **SANITIZERS: 31/31 host binaries clean** under `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all`, with the same three UBSan sub-checks disabled for the same PRE-EXISTING GCC-13.3 `constexpr` reason `P4-C5a` recorded. Run by hand; not added to `tools/check.sh`.
- Acceptance: `tools/check.sh` -> `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1915696 globals=72676 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 31/31` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` -> `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 at 0 project warnings.

**P4-C5 FOLLOW-UP (six hostile verifiers: fuzz, cheat, lossy, validator, honesty x3, gate) — LANDED.** Five defects, all five with a named failing test written before the fix, plus nine sentences narrowed to what the tree actually does. No test was disabled, no `static_assert` weakened, no warning suppressed.
- **`hp_cur == 0` WAS THE LAST THING THREE CHECKERS ACCEPTED AND THE ENGINE REFUSED — AND THE HONEST DEVICE RECORDED IT AS ITS OWN BUG.** `validate_pebble()`, `validate_team()` and `pbw_decode()` all answer `VR_OK` for a fainted member (a legal STORED state); `battle_init()` answers `BR_MEMBER_FAINTED`; `battle_link.cpp` turned that into `SE_PROTOCOL(SD_INTERNAL)` — "a bug in `game/validate.cpp`" — with `bad_index` 0xFF. Measured: it fired for a peer sending `member[1].hp_cur = 0` AND for an honest player whose own team held a fainted Pebble, with nobody lying. Byte for byte the class P4-C5b fixed for cross-team ids and left one instance of. `validate_battle_ready()` now sits beside `validate_level_band()` answering `VR_MEMBER_FAINTED`, runs on the LOCAL team in `session_set_team()` and on the PEER's in `on_team_submit()`, and `validate.h`'s containment claim is now the strong one (`a_battle_ready_team_is_one_the_engine_accepts_outright`, 36 x 5 x 8).
- **A PEER COULD RELABEL A TERMINAL IT HAD ALREADY AGREED.** `on_battle_end()` closed `SE_DESYNC` / `SE_LOST` from the peer's own reason byte without consulting `s.paid`, so a forged `BATTLE_END` after the rewards were committed made `session_rewards_authorised()` true on a non-`SE_DONE` terminal — and so did an rx-budget flood, with no `BATTLE_END` at all. The rule now lives in `session_close()`, the one place a terminal is decided.
- **ONE LEGAL FRAME, REPLAYED, HELD A SESSION OPEN FOREVER.** `on_action_result()` called `session_progress()` for EVERY ACTION_RESULT matching our outstanding ACTION, which is exactly what an honest peer regenerates. Measured: 1,019 injections, seventeen hours of virtual time, `SE_PROTOCOL(SD_RX_BUDGET)` with `tx_retx == 0`. The existing flood case injects `PT_ACTION_RESULT` from `SS_TEAM`, where it never reaches the handler — right type, wrong state.
- **THE FIRST SEQUENCE NUMBER WAS UNBOUNDED**, so P4-C5b's one-packet deafness was still available before the peer had spoken, through a HELLO (session 0, no id needed): `rx_seq_last` 28,672 and both endpoints `SE_LOST` on a link that dropped nothing.
- **`proto_encode()` COULD EMIT A FRAME NO DECODER WOULD ACCEPT**, and `protocol.h` cited a named test for the opposite that **did not exist anywhere in the repository**. A HELLO with a non-zero session encoded PE_OK and step 10 refused it for every `expect_session`: 198 of 2,000,000 random messages in the review's fuzzer, 35 in the case written here when the rule is removed again. **Writing that case found a second thing:** drawing the header's `round` as `(uint8_t)rng_next(rng)` gives ZERO zeroes in 40,000 draws on this xorshift32, so the fuzz arm would never have built the one message it exists for - a HELLO, whose round must be 0 to pass the round rule at all. A low byte is not a uniform small integer; it draws through `rng_next_below()` now.
- **NINE SENTENCES NARROWED, EACH AGAINST A MEASUREMENT.** "Nothing in `src/` writes a nickname today" (`persistence/migration.cpp:221-226` does, and always terminates). `Session` 360 B quoted inside the DEVICE budget (340 B with `riscv32-esp-elf-g++`; the total is 468 B, not 488). LIMIT 1's "what the player sees" and `session.h`'s "the caller pays XP" written in the present tense about P7's screen. LIMIT 4's "0..2 by construction" reading as a reassurance about stakes — measured, a maximum genome beats a minimum one **594 of 600** in a level-10 mirror match, symmetric across sides. The census now records the loopback's own queue-overflow rate per arm and asserts an unfaulted link overflows nothing. `ProtoRoundResult.outcome` is documented as carried-and-never-compared (a comparison could not fail: `hash_after` covers `st.outcome`). `pbw_decode()`'s `validate_pebble()` call is documented as redundant on the battle path and load-bearing for P7's trade path (measured: removing it leaves `test_session` fully green).
- **ONE VERIFIER FINDING REFUTED WITH EVIDENCE RATHER THAN "FIXED".** A review asked for the every-bit-flip sweep to decode out of an exact-sized heap block, reporting that step 8's deletion shows up there as an ASan heap overflow. It does not, and it cannot: a single flipped bit either breaks the trailing CRC (step 4) or the length equality (step 3), so no frame that sweep builds ever reaches the payload read at step 11 — which the case's own assertions state independently. Measured: with step 8 deleted and all 3,680 flips decoded out of exact blocks, ASan reports NOTHING. The sweep that CAN reach step 11 with a forged type is the fuzzer, because six of its eleven mutations reseal the CRC — the exact-sized arena went there instead, and with step 8 deleted it reports `heap-buffer-overflow READ of size 1 ... in proto_decode ... protocol.cpp:433`.
- **ELEVEN MUTATIONS, ALL ELEVEN CAUGHT BY A NAMED TEST, PLUS ONE PROVABLY UNOBSERVABLE.** The encoder's HELLO rule (`every_frame_the_encoder_emits_its_own_decoder_accepts`); `validate_battle_ready()` never refusing (4 named cases) and its `PBS_FAINTED` half alone (2); the call dropped from `session_set_team()` (1) and from `on_team_submit()` (2); the ACTION_RESULT repeat guard (`one_legal_frame_replayed_forever_...`); the paid relabel (`a_peer_cannot_relabel_a_terminal_after_the_rewards_are_committed` **and** the shipped `a_battle_end_that_never_arrives_pays_one_side_and_not_the_other`); the sequence baseline; the loopback's overflow counter firing with behaviour unchanged (the census, by name); step 8 under ASan; and a ninth file under `src/networking` (`GATE FAIL: ... classify it`). **UNOBSERVABLE AND RECORDED RATHER THAN COUNTED:** the census classifier's `pa != pb` narrowing. No trial in 3,000 half-pays on a divergent state, so nothing observes the change; it closes a hole the census would not have reported, and claiming a mutation caught it would be the defect this project keeps finding.
- **SANITIZERS: 31/31 host binaries clean** under `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all` with the same three PRE-EXISTING GCC-13.3 `constexpr` sub-checks disabled, run by hand and not added to `tools/check.sh`. **Two host-test cases allocate on purpose and it is declared:** the truncation sweep and the fuzzer decode out of `new uint8_t[k]` blocks sized to exactly the byte count under test, because that is the only way to put an ASan redzone on the first illegal byte. Host-test-only, never firmware.
- **FIVE NEW NAMED CASES, EACH RED BEFORE ITS FIX AND WITH ITS OWN POSITIVE CONTROL:** `every_frame_the_encoder_emits_its_own_decoder_accepts`, `a_battle_ready_team_is_one_the_engine_accepts_outright`, `a_fainted_member_is_the_peer_s_lie_and_our_own_teams_refusal_never_an_internal_bug`, `one_legal_frame_replayed_forever_cannot_hold_the_session_open` (whose control is a BATTLE_STATE replayed in the same shape, which always obeyed R4), `a_peer_cannot_relabel_a_terminal_after_the_rewards_are_committed` and `the_very_first_sequence_number_is_bounded_too_and_not_only_the_jumps_after_it`; plus a seventh vector (`a fainted member`) in the shipped poison table, the `validate_battle_ready` arm of the reachability sweep and the census's own overflow assertion. **`test_session` 36 -> 40 cases / 1,053 -> 2,099 checks, `test_protocol` 24 -> 25 / 121,302 -> 234,356, `test_validate` 24 -> 25 / 3,526 -> 3,566.** No case was deleted, renamed away or weakened.
- Acceptance: `tools/check.sh` -> `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1915696 globals=72676 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 31/31` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` -> `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 at 0 project warnings. **0 B of flash and 0 B of globals**, and the baseline is measured at INSTRUCTION LEVEL rather than by its size line: building `ab6ecfe` and this commit into two fixed build paths and comparing the ELFs section by section, `.flash.text` (sha256 `d1616c8e...`), `.iram0.text` and `.dram0.data` are **byte-identical**, and `.flash.rodata` differs in **exactly one byte** - the minute digit of the `__TIME__` literal the two builds were started in. The whole `.bin` differs in 66 bytes: that one, the 32 B `app_elf_sha256` in the app descriptor (the ELF gains a `validate_battle_ready` symbol even though nothing calls it on the device), and the 32 B trailing image SHA-256 both of those propagate into. "Byte-identical" is not a checkable sentence about this build system and the P4-C5b report should not have used it; the other six variants report identical flash and globals figures, which is a weaker measurement and is all that is claimed for them.

**P4-C6 Phase-4 exit** — S
- [x] Matrix + tag `v0.4.0-battle`. `tools/build_matrix.sh` → `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 variants at 0 project warnings; the annotated tag exists LOCALLY and is **not pushed** (this environment's remote refuses tag pushes — `send-pack` disconnects — which is why `CHANGELOG.md` links a commit rather than a tag URL, as it does for 0.2.0-core and 0.3.0-pet). **THE EXIT COMMITS ARE NOT ON THE REMOTE EITHER, and that is a separate fact worth stating before the phase is announced.** `git rev-parse origin/claude/repo-exploration-sync-bbonku` is **250f73e**: the exit commit `d21f20f` and the P4-C6 follow-up below exist on one machine. Only *tag* pushes are refused here — the branch push is what put 250f73e on origin and it still works — so the outstanding action is a **branch push**, after which `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[0.4.0-battle]` link should be repointed from 250f73e at the exit commit (the note under the links says so).
- [x] **D2 IS ALREADY CLOSED AND THIS BULLET WAS STALE — the request is not made, it is recorded as satisfied.** The bullet read "D2 decision formally requested from the owner with the §0.2 evidence (needed by P7-C1)". `docs/decisions.md` line 15 records **D2 CLOSED — ESP-NOW (2026-09-02, owner)**, the consequences are written out at line 53, and the evidence P7-C1 starts from — 250 B `ESP_NOW_MAX_DATA_LEN`, a 20-peer table, RSSI on `esp_now_recv_info_t.rx_ctrl`, the send-callback ACK, the `esp_now_init()` ordering, the broadcast peer, `CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_ENABLED` — was verified against the installed core (esp32 3.1.1, ESP32-C3) *before* it was closed. Asking again would have been asking the owner for a decision they made two days before this chunk began. **Two numbers inside that record were written before the code they describe and are corrected at this exit rather than repeated**: the frame is 14 B + ≤ 148 B + a 2 B trailing CRC = **164 B, 86 B to spare** in the datagram (not "a 12 B header + 200 B, 38 B to spare", which was plan §1.4's sketch), and compiling BLE out recovers **712,466 B / 23,504 B** measured here (not the phase-1 audit's 721,632 / 23,688).
- [x] **`docs/decisions.md` carries a Phase-4 exit section** in the format phases 2 and 3 used: the seven-variant table with Δ against `0.3.0-pet`, where the phase's cost actually landed, what the globals trend leaves for phases 5-10, the host-suite totals, the content and sanitiser gates, and a "what phase 4 did NOT measure" list. **`CHANGELOG.md` carries `## [0.4.0-battle]`** above `0.3.0-pet`.
- [x] **EVERY FIGURE IN THIS EXIT WAS RE-DERIVED BY RUNNING A COMMAND, and the phase-3 exit is why.** That exit shipped four false numbers a later audit caught — a ceremony duration that was a different ceremony's, three threshold values that were all wrong with a dependence nobody had checked for monotonicity, "every run a constant length" that was an upper bound for four of six games, and a sentence it claimed to have struck that survived in four other places. A phase-exit document is where a false number lands permanently. **Six stale numbers were found in this tree and corrected rather than carried**: `docs/protocol.md`'s own test counts (24/36/1,053, one commit out of date); the two D2 figures above; `250f73e`'s "three PRE-EXISTING GCC-13.3 `constexpr` sub-checks" (**one line** at this commit, re-derived by building); "zero `link_` symbols in the ELF" (the ELF carries six, from `ui/screen_link.cpp` and the BT stack — the true claim is about `battle_link.cpp`'s five function names); and this file's own "35 of 64 battles sit decided-but-unreported" (**all 64** sit in the window; 35 of them were the wins that paid nothing).
- [x] **NO WIN-RATE PERCENTAGE IS QUOTED ANYWHERE IN THIS EXIT, and that is a finding rather than an omission.** The 40-63 % / 57-65 % band the roster was tuned to came from `tools/content/sim_engine.py`, which diverges from the shipped engine in five ways `game/battle.h` names and **has no AI at all** — `battle_3v3()` scripts both sides. So §12's "type advantage matters without deciding every battle automatically" and §57's balance principles are **unverified for what ships**, and the only measured claim is that both sides win somewhere across 64 seeded fights. **P9-C4** (`tests/tools/balance_matrix.cpp`, 60x60 AI-vs-AI at 1,000 seeded battles a pair) is the chunk that produces real ones.
- [x] **THE DEAD-CODE SWEEP P4-C4a STOPPED ONE ITEM SHORT OF.** Method: `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` over the sketch objects and over the linked ELF, joined — a global in a `.o` and absent from the ELF is one `--gc-sections` dropped, i.e. one nothing references — then cross-checked against a comment-stripped grep of `Pebblebol/src` and `tests`. **The mood badge was a chain of four, dead all the way down**: `PetView.mood`, `PetView.asleep` and `PetView.sick` (written once each in `pet_view_fill_sim()`, read by no line anywhere), `PebbleView.mood_face` (written by `ui.cpp` every frame, drawn by no screen), `sprite_mood_face()` (**zero references in the tree**, last caller gone at P2-C11b) and `spr_mood12`, 144 B of authored art already being dropped from every build. `asleep` and `sick` were a SECOND COPY of `PF_ASLEEP`/`PF_SICK`, which every real consumer reads from `flags`. All deleted; **−42 B of flash on every variant, −16 B of globals on `release`, measured**; the art is recoverable at `250f73e` and the Mood enum, its ladder and its Spanish words stay live. **Two P4-C5 functions had shipped with no caller and no test** — `session_state_name()` and `session_detail_name()`, while their three siblings had both — and are now wired into the acceptance census (a hung trial names the state each endpoint stopped in; every arm prints its terminal `SessionDetail` distribution by name) with `every_session_state_and_detail_has_a_distinct_english_name_and_the_lookup_is_total` behind them. **Four constants with no reader** are each now labelled with the chunk that owns them (`CORRUPT_DURATION_S` had said nothing at all) or with why they are deliberately dead.
- [x] **NINE SENTENCES NARROWED TO WHAT THE TREE DOES**, each against a check: this file's §5 module contract rows for `game/species.h`, `game/battle.h` and `networking/protocol.h` (which between them promised `species_count()`, `species_sprite()`, `validate_custom_species()`, a 12 B header, a 200 B payload, five error codes, five extra message types, a seven-member `Transport`, `session_step`/`session_abort` and an on-device loopback self-test — **none of which exist**); the P4-C5 boxes' "3 x 64 B", "3 s x 3", "reused by load and creator" and "Appendix C vectors"; the P4-C2 box's `RoundLog`; `docs/save_schema.md` §8's field map (three wrong rows, two missing) and §9 (a module and an NVS key P2-C10 deleted, still in the present tense, and five phantom entries in the §5 budget arithmetic); `game/evolution.cpp`'s learnset obligation, which **P4-C2 answered in another file** and which was still filed open here; `persistence/migration.cpp`'s "nothing is lost", contradicted by its own next clause; `ui/pet_art.h`'s two callers for a one-caller function; and `tools/check.sh`'s claim that all thirteen of its balance constants decide hashed battle state when two of them are 0 and decide nothing.
- [x] **§67 line "Invalid peers cannot inject illegal data" is HOST-PROVEN and the two places that said so now agree.** The P4-C5 acceptance line read as if the criterion were met; the tracker below leaves it unticked and names P7-C3. The tracker is right and the acceptance line is narrowed: `riscv32-esp-elf-nm` finds no `proto_`, `session_`, `pbw_` or `battle_link` symbol in the ELF, so no peer — valid or invalid — has ever reached a device.
- **Every per-chunk acceptance figure above is a SNAPSHOT AT THAT CHUNK'S COMMIT and several are now superseded** (P4-C1's "23 host test binaries", P4-C2's "26/26" and 1,896,094/70,348, P4-C4's 1,915,038/72,652 and "28/28"; **and, added by the P4-C6 follow-up because the first version of this list stopped short of them**, P4-C5a's "`test_validate.cpp` (24 cases) and `test_protocol.cpp` (24 cases)" and P4-C5b's "`test_session.cpp` (36 cases, 1,053 checks)" — the P4-C5 follow-up moved them to 25 / 25 / 40 and the exit to 41, and the current figures are **25 cases / 3,566 checks**, **25 / 234,356** and **41 / 2,493**, recorded in the line above and in `docs/protocol.md`'s header). They are left as the record of what was true when each chunk landed; **the current figures are the exit table in `docs/decisions.md`**, and nothing in this exit was copied from a box.
- [x] **P4-C6 FOLLOW-UP — FOUR HOSTILE VERIFIERS READ THE EXIT AND FOUND A SEVENTH WIDE SENTENCE, A SURVIVING TWIN, AND A TRIPWIRE THAT COULD NOT FIRE.** Seven blocking findings, all confirmed against the tree before acting and all corrected by narrowing rather than deleting; none needed refuting. **(1) The cost ledger in `docs/decisions.md` did not add up and its first item was contradicted by the commit it cited.** "P4-C1 and P4-C2 moved the baseline not at all" is true of GLOBALS and false of FLASH, where P4-C1 cost **+3,022 B** (`git log -1 993e3b0`, and line 529 of this file, both in bold). The listed deltas summed to 19,560 B against a stated phase total of +22,582 B, and the missing 3,022 B — 13.4 % of the phase's flash bill — was attributed to nothing after being explicitly denied. It was also measuring P4-C5a across the skipped `ee75076` follow-up (+658 instead of +782 and −124). **P5-C1 Scan-only Wi-Fi** — M
- [x] `net.cpp`: `NPH_SCANNING`; `net_scan_start()` = `net_request(RADIO_WIFI)` without `WiFi.begin()` (scan forces `enableSTA` only, audit §7.2), `WiFi.scanNetworks(async=true, show_hidden=true, passive=true, 300 ms/ch)`; `net_scan_poll()` hashes `BSSID‖SSID‖device_id` with FNV-1a into `net_hash` and classifies `category` (OPEN/HIDDEN/HOME/BUSINESS/PUBLIC/UNKNOWN from `encryptionType()`, hidden flag, RSSI bucket, SSID token hash lists in `balance.h`, §20); `scanDelete()`; `net_request(RADIO_OFF)`; never `WiFi.begin()` (§68 r5) — grep gate. **DONE, with one deliberate departure and it is named here rather than in a commit message.** `NPH_SCANNING` exists; `net_scan_driver()` is the entry point rather than a bare `net_scan_start()`, because the job that owns the timeout lives in `wifi_scanner.{h,cpp}` and reaches the radio through a four-function seam - which is what lets a host test drive every exit path with no radio. `WiFi.scanNetworks(async=true, show_hidden=true, passive=true, WIFI_SCAN_DWELL_MS=300)`; `scanDelete()` moved into `wifi_down()` as well as the read, because the core heap-allocates one `wifi_ap_record_t` per access point and ANY route to `RADIO_OFF` out from under a finished scan leaked it. **THE HASH IS OVER THE HARDWARE ADDRESS AND THE SALT, NOT `BSSID+SSID+device_id`**: a hidden network reports an empty name on one scan and may reveal it on the next, and a renamed router keeps its address, so folding the name in would give ONE access point TWO identities and silently reset its cooldown; and the salt is `fnv1a32("pbl-scan" + device_id)` rather than the raw id, which goes on the wire in `ProtoHello`. The cost of that choice - an access point that rotates its address reads as new each scan - is written down in `net_classify.h`.
- [x] `networking/wifi_scanner.{h,cpp}` job wrapper with a 12 s software timeout (§47) and cancel (B). **DONE.** Caller-owned `WifiScanJob` (8 + 8x16 = 136 B, so it costs zero globals until a screen declares one), states IDLE/RUNNING/DONE/FAILED/TIMEOUT/CANCELLED, `WIFI_SCAN_TIMEOUT_MS` 12,000. The timeout is checked AFTER the poll so an answer arriving on the deadline tick is an answer; the radio is released exactly once per run on every exit path through one `release()`; and `net_service()` carries a strictly-later backstop (`WIFI_SCAN_TIMEOUT_MS + RADIO_SETTLE_MS + 1 s`) for a job nobody is pumping, which cannot pre-empt or disagree with the job's own clock.

**P5-C2 Cooldowns (persisted, epoch-based; RAM fallback when uncalibrated)** — S
- [x] `game/cooldowns.{h,cpp}` on `CooldownTable` (`cd` pair): `cd_ready(hash, now)`, `cd_arm(hash, now + 2 h)`, LRU eviction of 32; when `gt_cal_state() == CAL_UNSET` a per-boot RAM table is used instead so an uncalibrated device cannot farm (T9); a clock rollback never shortens a cooldown. **DONE.** `cd_ready(t, hash, CdClock)` / `cd_arm(...)` / `cd_take_dirty()`; period `ENCOUNTER_COOLDOWN_S` 7,200. THE CLOCK IS INJECTED as a `CdClock {now_epoch, now_ms, cal}` - the same shape `save_manager.h` injects its clocks with - so `game/` stays pure and the uncalibrated path is drivable on the host. Eviction is ONE rule: reuse the row with the smallest deadline, which with a fixed period is both least-recently-armed and expired-first. The `CAL_UNSET` fallback is a separate per-boot table IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, keyed the same way but with MONOTONIC-MILLISECOND deadlines, and a calibration landing mid-session PROMOTES its live rows into the persisted table (`now_epoch + remaining`) from inside both entry points, so neither can forget it. What the fallback does NOT close - a reboot clears it - is a comment in `cooldowns.h` and an assertion in the test, not an implication.
- [x] `test_cooldowns.cpp` (reboot survival via `kv_mem`; expiry by epoch; eviction order; rollback; uncalibrated path). **DONE, 17 cases / 329 checks**, including the reboot round trip through `save_cooldowns()` + `save_load_all()` over `kv_mem`. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THE EVICTION CASE WAS A TEST THAT COULD NOT FAIL AND THIS IS THE MEASUREMENT**: it armed 32 networks in slot order, so the row that ended soonest WAS slot 0, and a mutant that simply always evicted slot 0 passed it. The deadlines are now skewed by `(i + 7) mod 32` so "evict slot 0", "evict the last slot" and "evict the least-recently-armed" are three different answers.
- Acceptance (this box had none): gate + matrix, as above. **P5-C3 MUST CALL `cd_begin()` FROM `setup()` AND POLL `cd_take_dirty()`**, and it inherits two facts written down in `cooldowns.h` rather than left to be discovered: an uncalibrated device can still farm by REBOOTING (spec §21's "avoid resetting when battery dies" is unachievable with no trustworthy timestamp — the design trades a catastrophic farm for a linear one), and the unmetered `XP_SRC_CAPTURE`/`XP_SRC_ITEM` sources in `game/xp.h` are farm-proof only because a scan sits behind this cooldown, so P5-C3's SPECIAL XP burst is either metered or withheld on an uncalibrated device. That choice belongs in P5-C3, in writing.

**P5-C3 Encounters** — M
- [x] `game/encounters.{h,cpp}`: `encounter_roll(ScanResult, day_bucket(6 h), progress, RNG_ENCOUNTER) → {WILD, ITEM, SPECIAL, NOTHING}` from `data/encounter_table.h` weights (singular — that is the filename P4-C1 generated; §22; NOTHING ≥ 15 %); wild species by rarity ∩ `category_mask`; wild level = clamp(active ± 2, 1..30); SPECIAL outcomes: XP burst and **corruption event** (sets `CORRUPTED` status on the active with a 24 h timer — hook for P9-C5); deterministic under seed (§20). **DONE.** `encounter_roll(EncounterInput, EncounterResult)` is a PURE FUNCTION of section 20's own input list - net_hash, the six-hour bucket, the device seed, the category, the signal and the Box count - and draws from NO named stream. **THAT IS A DEVIATION FROM THIS BULLET'S `RNG_ENCOUNTER` AND IT IS DELIBERATE**: drawing the outcome from a shared stream would make two scans of the same access point in the same bucket answer differently, which is the opposite of what §20 asks for, and would make the stream POSITION part of the answer so no host test could pin a seed. `RNG_ENCOUNTER` stays what the plan names it for - the CAPTURE roll, where a re-roll must be real. The wild level is `clamp(active ± 2, 1..30)` with `ENCOUNTER_LEVEL_SPREAD` named once. **A REAL DEFECT CAME OUT OF THE DISTRIBUTION TESTS AND IS FIXED**: the first version took successive steps of one xorshift32, and because the SPECIAL branch is only reached when the FIRST draw lands in a 4-10 wide band mod 100, the second draw was badly structured - measured, HIDDEN's four events weighted 20/30/20/30 came out 53/32/**0**/15 %, i.e. one event was UNREACHABLE in two categories. Every stage now gets its own seed (the encounter seed folded with a one-byte tag through the same FNV-1a, then one xorshift step).
