[CAPTURAR] [DEJAR]", §23); nothing found → toast. `test_encounters.cpp` (same seed → same outcome; weights over 10k rolls within tolerance; NOTHING occurs; category mask honoured; level clamp). **DONE, 22 cases / 868,651 checks.** `ui/screen_network.cpp` owns the one `WifiScanJob` (136 B), holds the radio only while it scans and releases it on EVERY route out - its leave hook cancels, so LONG_BOTH and the auto-return cannot leave Wi-Fi up. `ui/screen_encounter.cpp` carries BOTH the ENCOUNTER transient and the CAPTURE attempt in one translation unit, because the capture screen is about the creature the encounter screen is showing. **THE WEIGHT CASES ARE ANCHORED TO THE TABLE READ AT RUN TIME, not to literals**, so shuffling a category's weights moves the expectation with the reality - and `the_measured_shares_track_the_table_and_not_each_other` additionally asserts the six categories differ, which is what a picker that ignored the category cannot survive.
- [x] **DISCHARGED — SPECIAL HAS A PAYLOAD TABLE.** `tools/content/specials.json` → `SPECIAL_EVENTS[]` (a roster: id, kind, value, name) and `SPECIAL_DROP_TABLE[]` (per-category weights summing to 100), emitted into `data/encounter_table.h` beside `ITEM_DROP_TABLE` and picked by the same two-stage walk, with three generated guards: `special_drop_rows_are_well_formed()`, `encounter_special_rows_have_an_event()` and `special_kinds_are_all_populated()`. Exactly the two kinds this bullet promises and no third invented - `SPEV_XP_BURST` pays `SPECIAL_XP_SCALE * value` through the new `XP_SRC_SPECIAL`, `SPEV_CORRUPTION` sets `PBS_CORRUPTED` for 24 h. **THE 24 h TIMER HAD NOWHERE TO LIVE**: `PebbleInstance` had the status bit and no deadline field, so the status could only be set and never expire. Four of the twelve reserved bytes are now `corrupt_until_epoch` - no migration (nothing in the tree set the bit before this commit, so no save carries a stale one), no wire change (`reserved[12]` is not in the 48 B record and `PBW_STATUS_MASK` refuses the bit outright), and no new VReject (`validate.h` already states that every epoch in the struct is checked against nothing, because no rule exists to check one against). `game/corruption.{h,cpp}` owns the status and its clock; P9-C5 grows it with the effects. **AND THE XP BURST IS WITHHELD ON AN UNCALIBRATED DEVICE**, which is the choice P5-C2's acceptance box demanded in writing: `xp.h`'s unmetered sources are farm-proof because a capture consumes an encounter and an item consumes the item, a SPECIAL burst consumes neither, and `cooldowns.h` says the two-hour cooldown is a per-boot table while `CAL_UNSET`. Metering it is unavailable (`Inventory.xp_ledger` is four persisted bytes pinned by a static_assert), so the honest answer is to pay nothing. *The original bullet:* P4-C1's completeness pass found it and recorded it in three places a ticked box does not get re-read from: line 541 above, the banner of `data/encounter_table.h`, and `tests/test_content.cpp::the_special_encounter_outcome_still_has_no_payload_table`. Spec §22 names four outcomes; SPECIAL is 4–10 % of **every** category's scan and the pack carries **no event roster, no ids, no per-category weights** behind it. The bullet above already promises two SPECIAL outcomes (XP burst, corruption event) — **that is the payload table, and it belongs in `tools/content/*.json` with a generated header and generator guards**, not as two `if`s in `encounters.cpp`, or the roster and the encounter logic stop being one source of truth. The test above now fails on any pack edit (it pins `CONTENT_VERSION 0x5B4A`), so filling this hole trips it and the person who fills it has to come back and rewrite the case.
- [x] **DISCHARGED — `encounter_item_rows_have_a_drop()` IS EMITTED NEXT TO ITS WILD TWIN**, and it caught the mutation the bullet asks for: HIDDEN's ITEM band 1..2 → 3..3 now fails the firmware build with the guard's own sentence, where before it compiled clean, passed every host case, and was caught only by `verify.py` - which `check.sh` skips with a word when python3 is missing. **THE BULLET'S CLAIM WAS HALF WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS RECORDED**: `tools/content/verify.py:290` HAS checked this since P4-C1 and is in the gate, so what was missing was the firmware half, not the check. **AND THE HOST CASE THAT NAMED THE PROPERTY COULD NOT FAIL ON IT — INSTANCE EIGHTEEN.** `every_item_drop_row_resolves_and_the_two_stage_pick_respects_rarity` checks per-category sums, row shape, a COMMON-only band and an empty band, and NEVER pairs an ITEM row's own band with its own category's drop list; measured, it stayed green under the mutation. `every_item_encounter_row_can_actually_produce_an_item` is the case that does, and it was proven falsifiable by disabling the new static_assert and running it against the same mutated pack. *The original bullet:* `encounter_table.h` asserts `encounter_wild_rows_have_a_pool()`, so a WILD row can always answer. Its ITEM twin, `item_drop_rows_are_well_formed()`, checks only category/id/weight/reserved and a per-category sum of 100 — **nothing asserts that a category's ITEM row has a drop whose `rarity` lies inside that row's `rarity_min..rarity_max`**, which is the step the banner says the picker takes ("then rejected if the item's rarity falls outside"). Measured from the generated tables at this commit: it holds, **100 of 100 eligible drop weight in all six categories** (UNKNOWN/HOME/PUBLIC 0..1, BUSINESS/HIDDEN 1..2, OPEN 0..2), so the rejection step **never fires today** — which is exactly why nobody would notice one item edit turning ITEM into an outcome `encounter_roll()` cannot answer. The banner tells P5-C4 what **not** to do ("must not read it as a care item") and nothing about what to do. ### Phase 6 — Activity — size M — goal: sensor abstraction, activity score, growth rewards, deep-sleep integration

**P6-C1 Sensor abstraction** — S
- [x] **DONE, and host-testable, which the bullet does not ask for and is the only reason any of the rest is checkable.** All seven §19 words ship as `{Hz, ms}` step lists in flash (23 steps, 92 B), a four-deep queue that refuses the NEWEST play when full, one step per `audio_service()` call and never a delay. `tests/test_audio.cpp` is **21 cases / 1,633 checks** over a RECORDING sink: the engine reaches the piezo through an `AudioSink` of two function pointers the way `wifi_scanner.cpp` reaches the radio, so the emitted sequence is asserted note by note with no board. **`CF_MUTE` IS TWO RULES, NOT ONE**, and both are named cases: a play made while muted is REFUSED rather than deferred (un-muting must not fire a burst of what the player silenced), and mute arriving mid-note stops the pin on the next service call rather than at the end of the note. The mute question is a bound hook that re-reads the live `Config`, so there is no second copy of the flag. **`noTone()` ON EVERY EFFECT END IS STRUCTURAL**: `end_effect()` emits the sink's `tone_off` on the natural end, on `audio_stop()` and on mute, and `every_effect_ends_with_one_tone_off_after_a_real_note` refuses an effect whose last step is a silence. **LEDC, NOT THE CORE'S `tone()`, AND THE REASON IS A NUMBER**: Arduino-ESP32 3.1.1's `Tone.cpp` lazily creates a FreeRTOS task with a 3,500-word (14,000 B) stack plus a 128 x 16 B queue on first use, and its `noTone()` `xQueueReset()`s a start that has not run yet; it also reaches the pin through the same `ledcAttach`/`ledcWriteTone` so it would have been strictly more expensive. Read from the core source, **not measured on a board**. The device sink detaches the channel between effects and drives the pin low (§18 "GPIO inactive except during sound playback", "avoid continuous PWM"). **§18's DURATION BANDS ARE MAPPED AND CHECKED WITHOUT COPYING THE NUMBERS TWICE**: `every_effect_is_inside_the_spec_18_band_it_was_written_for` holds the band per word; no single effect reaches the 500-1500 ms evolution band on purpose, because `ui/ceremony.cpp` composes that from four cues across the 4,480 ms show. **COST, ATTRIBUTED** (`docs/decisions.md`, "D8 - the tone engine landed against an open decision"): +9,028 B flash / +136 B globals on the baseline, +9,036 / +152 on the release build; 22 B of that is the engine's own state and ~114 B is the ESP-IDF LEDC driver arriving with the first PWM output in the tree. That is most of phase 6's flash line for one chunk of three, and it is paid once. **D8 IS STILL OPEN**: `PIN_PIEZO 3` is committed as the PROPOSAL, `audio.cpp` names the macro and never a number, `PB_PINS_CONFIRMED` is still undefined, and `tools/check.sh` gained a gate that fails any `#define PIN_...` outside `core/config.h` (driven with a planted second definition). Two dormant D8 `static_assert`s were added and mutation-checked under `-DPB_PINS_CONFIRMED`, which **found and fixed a latent defect**: `PIN_VBAT_ADC` was declared after the guard block, so an armed build did not fail an assertion, it failed to compile.
- [x] **DONE.** Both signatures exactly as the bullet writes them, plus a 12 B `MotionSample` (three int16 milli-g axes, a wrapping `seq`, a `t_ms`) whose layout is pinned by `offsetof` so a later board revision is a driver and not also a redesign of what the game may read. `tests/test_motion.cpp` is three cases: the capability answers false, the layout holds, and **a refused poll leaves every byte of the caller's sample alone**. **THAT LAST ONE IS THE CASE WORTH HAVING AND IT IS NOT PEDANTRY**: zeroing `out` would be worse than refusing, because {0,0,0} milli-g is a legal reading meaning free fall, and P6-C2's activity score would be averaging in a measurement nobody made. Driven from two different fill patterns so a call that happened to write the test's own byte cannot slip through. No pin is invented and no I2C address is probed (§68 r3). **IT COSTS 0 B IN THE IMAGE TODAY**, because nothing calls it and the linker drops it - which is a fact about the linker and not a claim that it is tested by the firmware; the host binary is what executes it.
- [x] **THE WIRING, WHICH IS DELIVERABLE 3 AND IS COVERED BY EXACTLY ONE HOST TEST - SAID PLAINLY RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** `ui/ceremony.cpp`'s phase-edge function was marked by P2-C11c as where P6's audio calls belong; it now arms four cues, each exactly once per show (three `SFX_BUZZ` with the three crack jolts, `SFX_CHIRP` on the flash, `SFX_RISE` on the reveal, `SFX_DOUBLE_BEEP` on the name), with the SHARDS edge left silent because it is 80 ms after the flash - closer together than the shortest effect. The EVOLVE arm enters at `CP_FLASH` without crossing the edge that arms it, so its chirp is armed in `ceremony_begin()`. `ui/ui.cpp` adds `SFX_RISE` beside the level-up's `rd_flash()` and ONE `SFX_BUZZ` for a refused care action - hoisted above the two refusal branches so a third refusal reason cannot arrive silent. `ui/screen_battle.cpp` adds `SFX_BUZZ` on `RLE_HIT` and `SFX_FALL` on `RLE_FAINT`, beside the shake and the flash they belong to. **ONLY THE LAST OF THE FOUR IS REACHABLE FROM A HOST BINARY**: `a_hit_and_a_faint_each_arm_their_own_cue_exactly_once` drives a real battle with the recorder bound and counts NOTES (a buzz is one, a fall is five), so wiring both edges to the same cue fails on the note total while the per-effect `tone_off` total still matches. `ceremony.cpp` and `ui.cpp` are device translation units no host binary compiles: those three cues are checked by the compiler and by reading, and by nothing else. **Nothing has been heard.**
- Acceptance: gate + matrix. **MET on this tree.** `tools/check.sh` -> `BUILD OK variant=baseline flash=1936964 globals=73316 warnings=0` / `ALL PASS 39/39` / `GATE OK`; `tools/build_matrix.sh` -> `MATRIX OK`, 7/7 at 0 project warnings, `release caps: flash 1212844/1600000  globals 49660/65000`. §67's audio rows are NOT claimed: no piezo has been soldered, D8 is open, and nothing in this repository has ever run on a board.

**P6-C2 Activity score + growth rewards** — M
- [x] `game/activity.{h,cpp}`: daily score = carried minutes awake x1 + interactions x2 + distinct new `net_hash` x10 + peers met x15, each capped (§25, §57); feeds `xp_add(XP_ACTIVITY)`, a happiness bonus and a rare-encounter permille bonus for the next roll; day bucket persisted in `inv` ledger fields; zero when `CAL_UNSET`. **DONE, with three deviations from this line and all three are the carried-forward boxes below: the day bucket is in `cd`, not `inv` (there is no byte in `inv`); the XP source is `XP_SRC_CARRY`, not a new `XP_ACTIVITY`; and the caps are `ACT_CAP_CARRY_MIN` 240 / `ACT_CAP_INTERACT` 20 / `ACT_CAP_NETS` 10 / `ACT_CAP_PEERS` 4 for a full day of 440 points, which are P6-C2's numbers - §25 gives the four terms and their multipliers and no caps at all.** The three rewards: XP is one point per 10 points of the day's CUMULATIVE score (44 a day against `XP_CAP_CARRY` 48, so the meter binds on a heavy day); happiness is 250 care milli-points per 20 points (5.5 % of the bar a day, clamped like a care item's restore); the rare bonus is a permille scaled 0..`ENC_RARE_BONUS_MAX_PM` and handed to `EncounterInput.rare_bonus_pm`.
- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD FROM THE PHASE-5 EXIT — `Inventory` HAS NO FREE BYTE, so "day bucket persisted in `inv` ledger fields" (the bullet above) has nowhere to land.** `grep -n -A12 "struct Inventory" Pebblebol/src/persistence/save_schema.h`: 32 B exactly, `static_assert`ed, and every byte spoken for — magic 2, version 1, slots 1, `seq` 4, `ledger_epoch` 4, `xp_ledger[4]`, `items[7]` × 2 B, `crc16` 2 — with `offsetof` asserts on `seq`, `items` and `crc16` and a fixture pinning them. Three ways out and none is free: **(a)** derive the activity day from `ledger_epoch`, which already exists and is already a day anchor — cheapest, and it couples the activity day to the XP ledger's snapshot day; **(b)** grow `Inventory`, which is a `SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump plus a migration plus a new fixture (the P2-C9 machinery is all there); **(c)** a new persisted pair with its own CRC and quarantine — the only option that does not touch a struct two other systems read. **Decide in writing before coding**, because (b) is the one that silently breaks a v1 save if it is done by hand.
  **DECIDED: none of (a), (b) or (c) — the fourth option, which is that `CooldownTable` already had four reserved bytes and is the right owner.** `act_day` (u16, the UTC day index) and `act_score` (u16, that day's total) replace `reserved_a[4]` at offset 8, pinned by two new `offsetof` asserts, with the blob still 272 B and every byte after them where it was. No bump, no migration, no fixture.
  **(a) IS WRONG AND THE REASON IS NOT COST.** `Inventory.ledger_epoch` is re-stamped every time XP is *spent* (`app.cpp`'s XP funnel calls `gs_save_xp_ledger(after, sim_now())`), so `ledger_epoch / 86400` is "the day of the last XP spend", not "the day these counters belong to". Two different facts, and deriving one from the other gives one fact two owners — the same objection `encounters.h` already makes to folding the cooldown state into `EncounterInput`. A day that never rolls because nobody spent XP is not the cheap version of a day bucket, it is a different quantity.
  **(b) IS MUCH MORE EXPENSIVE THAN "a migration plus a fixture", MEASURED.** A `SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump is *not* "add a row to `migrate_run()`'s step table": that table is reachable only from the legacy-`save`-key branch, and `pair_load()` sorts any non-equal version into `st.bad` so `load_all_inner()` returns `LOAD_CORRUPT` **before** the migration branch is ever reached. Driven directly by the phase-6 survey, with throwaway probes linked against this tree's own objects: a healthy v2 save with the version byte aged to 1 and correct CRCs loads as `LOAD_CORRUPT` with the pet unrecovered, and the `ck_*` checkpoint behind the SAVE ERROR screen's "Recuperar" dies with it (`save_restore_checkpoint()` returns 0). A real bump therefore needs a version-tolerant pair reader and a new branch in `load_all_inner()` written first, and it invalidates `box`, `cfg`, `cd`, `cs` and `tr` as collateral — five blob types that did not change. Keep it for a real widening (`INVENTORY_SLOTS` 7→12, which `save_schema.h` already flags as "a reserved-free schema bump").
  **(c) WORKS AND WAS NOT NEEDED.** A new `ac0`/`ac1` pair is invisible to an old firmware and absent-means-defaults to a new one, at the cost of a `BlobOps` row, four call sites, 6 NVS entries and ~2x `sizeof` in globals (`GameState` is instantiated twice). Reach for it when the activity state outgrows four bytes.
  **WHAT IS AND IS NOT PERSISTED, because the answer is smaller than the question and that is the finding.** Only the day and the day's TOTAL are on flash. The four per-term counters and the distinct sets are per-boot RAM, so a power cycle *does* clear the per-term caps — and it buys nothing, because `act_score` is capped at `ACT_SCORE_MAX`, which is exactly the sum of the four capped terms, i.e. exactly what an honest day reaches. A rebooting player gets there sooner and never higher. `tests/test_activity.cpp`'s `power_cycling_thirty_times_cannot_beat_one_honest_day` is that claim, and moving the total into `.bss` makes it fail at 420 XP against the honest 44.
- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD — `xp_add(XP_ACTIVITY)` WOULD BE A FIFTH METERED SOURCE AND THERE ARE FOUR SLOTS.** `XP_LEDGER_SLOTS` is 4 and `game/xp.h` asserts `(int)XP_SRC_BATTLE + 1 == XP_LEDGER_SLOTS`: the metered sources are exactly the first four enumerators and each owns one byte of `Inventory.xp_ledger`. Phase 5 hit this wall and took the only answer that cost nothing — `XP_SRC_SPECIAL` is **unmetered**, and its award is **withheld entirely while `gt_cal_state() == CAL_UNSET`**, because the only thing rate-limiting a SPECIAL is the 2 h cooldown and while uncalibrated that table is per-boot. P6-C2 must make the same choice **explicitly** for activity: meter it (which is the schema change above), or cap/withhold it another way and write the anti-farm argument down. An unmetered daily score on an uncalibrated clock is a farm, and it will not be visible in a test that only checks the cap.
  **DECIDED: no fifth source. Activity XP is paid through `XP_SRC_CARRY`, and the choice is written into `game/xp.h` next to the enumerator rather than left in a commit message.** `XP_SRC_CARRY` is already the daily-windowed bucket (`XP_CAP_CARRY` 48 / `XP_WIN_CARRY_S` 86400) and its declared meaning is "time carried awake", which is the activity score's own largest term. **THE COST, stated because it is real: activity XP and the passive carry drip now compete for one budget, so a heavy-walking day crowds out the drip.** They measure the same thing, which is the argument for it; a day that earns 48 XP from carrying earns none from networks, which is the price.
  **WHY NOT (metered):** `XP_LEDGER_SLOTS` 4→5 makes `Inventory.xp_ledger` 5 B, which moves `offsetof(items)` from 16 to 17 and fails that struct's own assert — or keeps 32 B by taking `INVENTORY_SLOTS` 7→6, which silently deletes the seventh item stack out of every save in existence. Either way it is an in-place edit of a persisted layout and drags the whole bill of the box above. (The enum renumbering itself would have been free: `XpSource` reaches no wire.)
  **WHY NOT (unmetered, like `XP_SRC_SPECIAL`):** an unmetered source is bounded only by the daily cap, and the daily cap is only real if the day survives a restart — and P6-C3 makes the device restart itself every ten idle minutes. That option reduces to "persist the counters", which is the box above, not an independent answer.
  **THE FIVE LAYERS, in the order they bite** (`game/activity.h` carries this list): `CAL_UNSET` scores zero (the `encounter_special_xp()` withholding, reused verbatim, plus a refusal below `NT_EPOCH_SANE_MIN`) → the day comes from the wall clock and only ever moves FORWARD, so a rollback finds the budget where it left it → the day's TOTAL is on flash → per-term caps with an exact distinct set behind the repeatable terms → and `XP_SRC_CARRY`'s meter underneath everything.
  **CORRECTED AT THE PHASE-6 EXIT (P6-C4): THE LAST CLAUSE OF THAT SENTENCE WAS FALSE, AND IT WAS THE CLAUSE THE WHOLE LIST LEANED ON.** It read "…which a reboot cannot refill and which `xp_ledger_restore()` re-seeds to ZERO on an untrusted clock". The restore aged the saved budget forward by `(now_epoch − saved_epoch) / refill_step`, both epochs are wall clock, and the wall clock is TYPED on the time screen — so a reboot refilled it exactly as often as the player was willing to type a date. **Measured: 100 rounds of (clock +1 day, reboot, one Wi-Fi scan of the SAME ten access points) spent 990 metered XP and 125,000 care milli-points in ZERO real seconds, against an honest 37.8 XP per real day and a happiness bar that holds 100,000.** Neither half does it alone — 100 reboots with the clock still spend 0, 100 clock jumps without a reboot spend 0 metered XP — and **neither module's own test binary contained both modules**, which is why `test_activity.cpp` could hold four named cheat cases and miss this one. P6-C4 removes the aging term outright (`game/xp.cpp`), scales the happiness by the XP the ledger actually granted (`act_happy_for_granted_xp`), links `activity.o` into `test_xp` and adds three named cross-module cases. The layer is now what the sentence said: **the only thing that refills the bucket is `xp_ledger_tick()` over seconds the device watched pass.**

- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD — THERE IS NO DAILY DISTINCT-NETWORK SET, AND THE COOLDOWN TABLE IS NOT ONE.** "distinct new `net_hash` ×10" needs a per-day set of hashes; what phase 5 left is a `CooldownTable` of 32 rows that is a **two-hour LRU** (the 33rd network evicts the row that ends soonest, and the same access point re-arms every two hours), plus `network_screen_seen()` / `network_screen_fresh()` in `ui/screen_network.cpp`, which are counts for **the last scan only**, not persisted and reset by the next one. The cheap trap to avoid by name: counting `cd_arm()` calls counts one access point twelve times a day, which is a farm rather than a diversity score. Note also that the NETWORK screen currently **discards every result but the first off-cooldown one** — the rest of `s_job.res[]` is dropped when the screen leaves, so if activity wants the whole scan it must be handed the array while the job still holds it.
  **DECIDED: an EXACT set of ten `uint32_t`, in `game/activity.cpp`. 44 B of globals, no false positives and no false negatives — and the cap is what makes exactness affordable rather than a luxury.** A set never needs more entries than its term's cap, because past `ACT_CAP_NETS` no further network can score; an eleventh entry could not change an answer. Measured by the phase-6 survey against the alternatives, over 2,000 simulated days x 6 scans per access point driven with real `net_hash_from_bssid()` values: `ExactU32<10>` 41 B / 0 errors; `FoldU16<10>` 22 B / 3 under-counts; a 128-bit k=2 bitset 16 B / 43; a 256-bit one 32 B / 16. **Every approximate form errs in ONE direction — a new network reading as already-seen, i.e. UNDER-counting — which is structural (a set that only adds can say "seen" too often and never "new" too often) and is the safe direction. It is still an error, and 25 B was not worth buying one.** The one inexactness that remains is upstream and is named in the module: `net_hash` is a 32-bit salted digest, so two access points can in principle collide and be credited once between them, costing exactly one point in the player's disfavour. A full set answers "seen" to everything, which is not a degradation: the term is at its cap.
  **BOTH TRAPS THIS BOX NAMES ARE CLOSED AND ONE OF THEM WAS THE REAL WORK.** Counting `cd_arm()` calls is refused by construction — the score never sees the cooldown table. And `ui/screen_network.cpp`'s `hand_off()` really did drop the array: it returns on the first off-cooldown network and on three further empty paths, so anything asked for inside that loop sees one network out of a dozen or none at all. `act_note_networks()` is called with the WHOLE `s_job.res[]` **before** the walk, because diversity is about what was SEEN and not about what was explorable — a street entirely on cooldown is the same street, and asking inside the loop would zero the score exactly where the player has been walking most.

- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD — THE ENCOUNTER ROLL'S INPUT LIST IS CLOSED AND TESTED; ADD TO IT, DO NOT REACH AROUND IT.** `EncounterInput` (`game/encounters.h`) has seven declared fields and `encounter_roll()` is total, stateless and clock-free **by contract**, with `every_declared_input_reaches_the_answer` perturbing each field one at a time until the answer moves. The "rare-encounter permille bonus for the next roll" must therefore be a **new declared field**, with the totality sweep and the per-category distribution cases extended over it — not a file-scope variable the roll reads, which would destroy the property P5-C3's deviation was taken to protect (two scans of the same network in the same six-hour bucket answer the same). Note the distinction the tests already make and a new input must respect: `progress` is a **seed** input, documented as tuning nothing; a bonus that moves WEIGHTS is a different kind of input and needs a case that can see a distribution, not just a membership.
  **DECIDED: one new declared field, `uint16_t rare_bonus_pm`, with its own stage — and a `static_assert(sizeof(EncounterInput) == 20)` beside it, because THE TEST THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO GUARD THIS DOES NOT.** `every_declared_input_reaches_the_answer` enumerates the seven fields BY HAND and reads no `sizeof`; an eighth field added and left unwired passed `test_encounters` **22/22**. The assert is what stops the build and puts the next person in front of the case list; the perturbation is what proves the field is wired once they are there.
  **THE BONUS IS DELIBERATELY NOT IN `encounter_seed()`.** Folding it in would make a bigger bonus RESHUFFLE rather than IMPROVE, so no test could assert monotonicity — and monotonicity is the only property that makes it a bonus rather than a stirring stick — and it would break `encounters.h`'s promise that rescanning one network inside its bucket shows the same encounter. It gets `ENC_STAGE_RARE`, its own independent draw, consumed after `pick_row()` and only for a WILD row: one step up the rarity ladder, same category, `rarer_wild_row()` total by construction (bounded walk, same-category WILD rows only, identity at the top band). **The outcome never changes, so the WILD/ITEM/SPECIAL/NOTHING split is invariant at every permille and §22's 15 % NOTHING floor is structurally safe rather than merely measured.**
  **THE PERTURBATION HAD TO BE PAIRED, AND THAT IS A FINDING ABOUT THIS KIND OF FIELD.** Every other field is folded into the seed, so sweeping it against one fixed base moves the whole draw. This one is a THRESHOLD on one independent draw, so for any single fixed input the draw either clears it or never does — measured: written as a sweep, `CHECK(moved_bonus > 0)` FAILED on a correctly wired field. The case compares the same input with the bonus off and on across the same 64 networks instead.
  **AND THE DISTRIBUTION CASES ARE THE POINT, because every phase-5 case runs at bonus 0** and would have left the entire weight-moving half untested by construction. Four new cases: a zero bonus reproduces the table's own per-band wild weights (the checkable form of "zero is the identity"); the outcome tally is IDENTICAL — exact equality, not a tolerance — at 0, ¼, ½ and max; the rare share rises monotonically and the common band keeps at least half its weight at the maximum; and any permille at or above the cap produces the cap's distribution roll for roll, because a pure function may not trust its input. `ENC_RARE_BONUS_MAX_PM` is **250** and lives in `data/balance.h` because two modules read it: at 1000 every common-band roll is promoted and the common band empties completely. The "common band is non-empty" form of that check is worthless — at 999 permille about one roll in a thousand survives and it still passed — so the case asserts the SHARE instead, which fails for any cap above 500.

- [x] `test_activity.cpp` (caps, day rollover, reward monotonicity, uncalibrated → 0). 19 cases at P6-C2, **21 at the phase-6 exit**, linking `activity.o` and nothing else. **Four of them are cheat cases and each was written by BREAKING the guard it covers and watching a NAMED case go red** — the observed output is pasted above each one: dropping the `CAL_UNSET` arm of `clock_ok()` (`an_uncalibrated_clock_scores_absolutely_nothing`), relaxing the day roll to `day != t.act_day` (`a_clock_rolled_backwards_cannot_reopen_a_spent_day`, and 20453 != 20454 is the rollback landing), moving the day's total into `.bss` (`power_cycling_thirty_times_cannot_beat_one_honest_day`, **420 XP against the honest 44**), and deleting `seen_or_add()`'s membership walk (`the_same_network_seen_thirty_times_scores_exactly_once`, 10 credits where 1 is right).
  **AND THE DIRECTION THEY ALL MISSED WAS FORWARD (found at the phase-6 exit, P6-C4).** Four cheat cases, and the clock is exercised uncalibrated, insane and rolled BACKWARDS — never moved forward, which is the only direction that resets `act_score` and therefore the only one worth typing. Worse, `a_new_day_resets_the_score_and_every_counter` blessed the reset mechanism without bounding how often a day can be created, so the suite certified the farm's engine as correct. `a_forward_clock_set_opens_one_day_at_a_time_and_never_more_than_one_budget` covers it now, and it says in its own body that **this module cannot stop a manufactured day and is not the place that bounds one** — a typed day and a day the device spent switched off are the same evidence.

**P6-C3 Power states + sleep-correct time** — L (deep sleep depends on D1)
- [x] `hardware/power.{h,cpp}` + `SLEEP` state: ACTIVE → DIM (contrast 40 via `rd_contrast_ramp`, 30 s idle) → IDLE (`rd_power(false)` render.cpp:399-411, FPS 1, radio OFF, 2 min) → DEEP_SLEEP (10 min idle, no radio job; `esp_deep_sleep_enable_gpio_wakeup` on both buttons **only if D1 gives buttons on GPIO0-5**, otherwise light sleep with `gpio_wakeup_enable`); forced save + `save_touch_lastseen` before any sleep; Wi-Fi/BLE forced OFF first (§45).
  **THE CONDITION IN THIS BULLET RESOLVES *NO*, AND THE SURVEY ESTABLISHED IT FROM THE INSTALLED CORE RATHER THAN FROM MEMORY.** `SOC_GPIO_DEEP_SLEEP_WAKE_VALID_GPIO_MASK` is `BIT0|…|BIT5` and `SOC_RTCIO_PIN_COUNT` is 0, so the ESP32-C3's deep-sleep wake domain is GPIO0..GPIO5 and nothing else; ext0/ext1 are not compiled for this chip. `PIN_BTN_R 2` is inside it, `PIN_BTN_L 10` is not, and D1 is deferred, so **the deepest rung is a LIGHT sleep with `gpio_wakeup_enable()` on both buttons** — which from light sleep is legal on all 22 GPIOs, because the digital pad domain stays powered. Said plainly, because it is a product fact: **a deep sleep on this pin map would leave the left button physically dead**, and would make every wake a reset taken with a finger on GPIO2, a strapping pin whose behaviour at a deep-sleep wake could not be established from any installed header. `docs/decisions.md` records all of that under D1's consequences, with the battery arithmetic: light instead of deep costs ~2.7 days of ~26 on D11's own model, while D11's unmeasured 100× spread swings the same answer from 26 days to 9.
  **NOT COMPILED OUT BEHIND `PB_PINS_CONFIRMED`, AND THE CORRECTION IS DELIBERATE.** D1's consequences used to promise the deep-sleep path would sit behind that macro "so it turns on by itself the day the map is confirmed". `PB_PINS_CONFIRMED` is not the condition — a confirmed map with `PIN_BTN_L 10` still cannot wake on two buttons — and a branch nobody has ever compiled switching itself on unattended is worse than no branch. `hardware/power.h` computes the real condition, `PWR_DEEP_WAKE_BOTH_BUTTONS`, and carries a `static_assert(!PWR_DEEP_WAKE_BOTH_BUTTONS)` that FAILS THE BUILD with a message naming the choice. Measured: editing `PIN_BTN_L` to 4 stops the build with that message.
  **THE LADDER IS PURE AND ITS FOUR EFFECTS ARE HOOKS**, so `tests/test_power.cpp` drives ten idle minutes with no board. Two details that are not decoration. (a) The idle clock is app.cpp's own gesture stamp and **not `sm_idle_ms()`** — measured, not argued: IDLE releases the radio by NAVIGATING, `sm_goto()` resets the navigation idle clock, and the ladder therefore reset its own timer at the instant it arrived and oscillated for ever. (b) DIM goes through `ui_note_power_dim()` and `bright_service()` rather than calling `rd_contrast_ramp()` itself, because ui.cpp is already the one owner of the contrast base (user brightness, asleep-pet dim); a second writer would fight it on whichever moved last. When both dims are on the panel takes the lower.
  **IDLE AND SLEEP DIFFER IN THE SLICE, NOT IN THE MECHANISM.** Both stop the CPU with `esp_light_sleep_start()`; IDLE's slice is one second so the pet keeps ticking at 1 Hz for a player who is probably still in the room, SLEEP's is `PWR_SLEEP_SLICE_MS` (8 s) and app.cpp charges the whole slice in one `logic_tick(owed)` on the far side. **That last part needed a change to the tick scheduler and it is the sharp edge of this chunk:** `app_loop()` advanced its cursor by exactly 1000 ms per pass and resynchronised past anything longer than `NT_TICK_RESYNC_MS` (4 s), so an 8 s slice would have run ONE second of simulation and thrown the other seven away. `NT_TICK_MAX_OWED_S` (16) replaces it, whole seconds owed are CHARGED rather than dropped, and app.cpp `static_assert`s the pair so a widened slice cannot silently outgrow the bound (measured: `PWR_SLEEP_SLICE_MS 20000` fails the build **and** a named host case).
- [x] `gametime`: monotonic source across sleep = RTC-backed `time()` (sdkconfig `CONFIG_ESP_TIME_FUNCS_USE_RTC_TIMER=y`) instead of `esp_timer_get_time()` uptime (gametime.cpp:129, :297-298); `BOOT_DEEPSLEEP` charges elapsed time as normal absence (never as a crash); care/box/cooldown catch-up runs on wake exactly like boot.
  **THE SOURCE IS `esp_rtc_get_time_us()`, THE RTC *COUNTER*, AND NOT `gettimeofday()`, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS LOAD-BEARING.** Both are RTC-backed and both survive a sleep, but `gt_set_epoch()` MOVES the wall clock with `settimeofday()` — the first calibration is a forward jump of about 1.7e9 s. The uncalibrated cooldown table's deadlines are monotonic MILLISECONDS measured against this same clock, so a source that a calibration can jump would expire all thirty-two rows at the instant the owner finishes typing the date: **exactly the catastrophic farm `game/cooldowns.h` refuses.** The counter is not the clock; `settimeofday()` moves an offset applied on top of it. Cost, written down and NOT measured on hardware: the read is documented as taking up to one RTC_SLOW_CLK cycle (~7 µs), and `CONFIG_RTC_CLK_SRC_INT_RC` means the slow clock is the internal ~136 kHz RC oscillator, so time across a long sleep carries its drift — fine for charging care, not fine for agreeing with a phone to the second.
  **`gt_mono32()` IS THE NEW PUBLIC NAME AND IT HAS EXACTLY TWO NEW CALLERS**, both chosen by one rule: anything whose answer decides a GAME outcome. `ui_explore_clock()`'s `now_ms` (the cooldown table) and `app.cpp`'s save-wear filter. Presentation timing — animation phase, modal lifetimes, the frame scheduler, the navigation idle clock — keeps `millis()`, because it wants "how long has this CPU been awake" and costs nothing to be wrong about. `tools/check.sh` gates `ui_explore_clock()` for the first of those.
  **`BOOT_DEEPSLEEP` ALREADY CHARGED CORRECTLY AND THE FIX WAS TO MAKE THAT VISIBLE.** `app.cpp`'s exclusion list was four literals inside one `if`, where nothing could see it — adding `BOOT_DEEPSLEEP` to it would have silently stopped every sleep from charging with no test able to fail. It is `nt_boot_charges_absence()` in `core/nt_types.h` now, pure and host-tested; measured, adding `BOOT_DEEPSLEEP` back to the refusing set fails a named case.
- [x] `app.cpp`: the busy loop yields (`delay(1)` when no frame is due and no job is pending; light-sleep tick in IDLE) — audit risk 16.
  Both rungs, and the second is the one that meets the acceptance number. ACTIVE and DIM take `delay(1)`; IDLE and SLEEP stop the CPU for `pwr_slice_ms()`, so the loop rate in IDLE is about 1/s against the plan's ≤ 10/s. **The light sleep is better than a long `delay()` and not merely cheaper:** the wake is a hardware level interrupt on both pins, so a press cannot be missed however long the slice — which is what made polling-with-a-long-delay unusable (a 100 ms yield can drop an 80 ms tap, and in IDLE the panel is dark so that press is the one that matters most).
- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD FROM THE PHASE-5 EXIT — DEEP SLEEP EMPTIES THE UNCALIBRATED COOLDOWN TABLE, WHICH TURNS A DISCLOSED LIMIT INTO A CHEAP EXPLOIT.** While `gt_cal_state() == CAL_UNSET`, cooldowns live in `game/cooldowns.cpp`'s `static CdRamRow s_ram[COOLDOWN_SLOTS]` — plain `.bss`, per boot, on the monotonic `millis()` clock — because there is no trustworthy timestamp to persist. `cooldowns.h` says an uncalibrated device can farm **by rebooting** and calls that a deliberate trade of a catastrophic farm for a linear one. **P6-C3 makes the device reboot by itself:** a deep-sleep wake re-runs `setup()`, `cd_begin()` starts from an empty table and `millis()` restarts at zero, so ten idle minutes free all 32 networks with no keystroke at all. Three ways out: keep the fallback in RTC-retained memory (`RTC_NOINIT_ATTR`, which this tree already uses for `hardware/boot.cpp`'s `RtcKeep` — survives deep sleep and a soft reset, lost on power loss), refuse encounters entirely while `CAL_UNSET`, or accept it and say so in §67's own terms. The bullet above already promises "cooldown catch-up runs on wake exactly like boot": for the PERSISTED table that is true and needs only the epoch; for the RAM table there is nothing to catch up, and that difference is the finding.
  **DISCHARGED BY NOT BUILDING THE THING THAT BREAKS IT, AND THAT IS THE HONEST FORM RATHER THAN THE LAZY ONE.** The exploit needs a REBOOT: `cd_begin()` running again and `millis()` restarting. A light sleep is not a reset, so neither happens — the 256 B RAM table is simply still there on the far side, and its deadlines are measured in `gt_mono32()`, the RTC counter, which carried the gap. Both halves are pinned by named host cases in `tests/test_cooldowns.cpp`, and the mutation is the alternative design itself: inserting `cd_begin()` at each of 450 eight-second wakes — which is what a deep-sleep rung would do — **fails `a_full_uncalibrated_table_survives_a_night_of_eight_second_sleep_slices` with 483 failed checks**, every network free at every wake. That is the exploit, measured rather than described.
  **NONE OF THE THREE LISTED WAYS OUT WAS TAKEN, AND EACH IS REFUSED FOR ITS OWN REASON.** `RTC_NOINIT_ATTR` would cost 256 B of RTC RAM to defend a farm that no longer exists, and would still be lost on the power cut the RAM table is already honest about. Refusing encounters while `CAL_UNSET` would punish the only player who cannot fix it — a device that has never been told the date — for a hole the sleep design does not open. Accepting it and disclosing it in §67's terms would be disclosing something untrue: with light sleep the pre-existing §21 limit (one reboot frees everything, at the cost of a boot) is exactly what it was at the phase-5 exit and no worse. **What the sleep design owes the debt is a red line for the day it changes**, and that is `power.h`'s `static_assert`, whose message names `game/cooldowns.h` by file so the next person reads this paragraph before writing the deep-sleep rung.
- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD — A POWER-DRIVEN RADIO SHUTDOWN MUST GO THROUGH `wifi_scan_cancel()`.** IDLE turns the radio off after two minutes and the NETWORK screen is the one consumer that can be mid-job: it owns a `WifiScanJob` and pumps it. A bare `net_request(RADIO_OFF)` behind the job's back leaves it `WSCAN_RUNNING` until its own 12 s clock and then reports FAILED or TIMEOUT — an error the player did not cause — while `wifi_down()`'s `scanDelete()` is what frees the driver's per-access-point array. `wifi_scan_cancel()` is the single path that stops the driver and releases the radio **exactly once**, is idempotent, and has a named host case. Cheapest correct rule: a screen holding a radio job counts as activity for the idle timer, and any state change that drops the radio cancels through the job first.
  **BOTH HALVES BUILT, AND THE RELEASE IS A NAVIGATION.** `s_job` is a file-static inside `ui/screen_network.cpp` and is unreachable from the power module by design, so the ladder's `release()` hook calls `ui_home()`: `sm_goto()` runs the leaving screen's `leave()` hook, and `network_leave()` is already `wifi_scan_cancel()` + `wifi_scan_reset()`. That is not a workaround for the encapsulation, it IS the interface — and it keeps navigation in `app/state_machine.cpp` where the hard rules require it, and releases CREATOR's AP by the same route. The other half is `PowerInput.held`, fed by a new `network_screen_busy()` query (a query, never a handle): while a scan is in flight the ladder is clamped at DIM and cannot reach the rung that drops the radio.
  **ONE CORRECTION TO THIS BOX AS WRITTEN: the heap leak it describes was already closed at the phase-5 exit.** `net.cpp`'s `wifi_down()` calls `WiFi.scanDelete()` on every route to `RADIO_OFF`, so a bare `net_request(RADIO_OFF)` would not leak the array. What it really breaks is the JOB: it stays `WSCAN_RUNNING` with `stopped == 0`, `wifi_scan_is_busy()` goes on answering true **to the very ladder that is trying to sleep**, and the next poll reports FAILED to a player who caused nothing. `tests/test_power.cpp` holds a REAL `WifiScanJob` against a fake driver and asserts both outcomes — the correct one and, as a named counter-case, the bare shutdown's — and `tools/check.sh` now fails any mention of the radio API inside `hardware/power.*` (measured: one added `net_request(RADIO_OFF)` line trips it).
- [x] `test_clock.cpp` += elapsed across a simulated sleep gap; `test_care.cpp` += recovery accrues across sleep; DIAG shows loop iterations/s per power state.
  41 host binaries now, `test_power.cpp` being the new one (15 cases, 7,326 checks, linking `power.o` + `wifi_scanner.o` and nothing else). **WHAT THE SLEEP-GAP CASES GUARD AND WHAT THEY DO NOT, because this is exactly where this project's recurring defect lives.** They guard the arithmetic: a gap nobody observed is charged in full and in one piece, including one that crosses the 2^32 ms boundary with no sample inside it (mutation: dropping Defence 1's wrap increment fails it, plus three older cases). They do NOT guard the choice of SOURCE — that `gt_mono_ms()` reads the RTC counter on the target rather than esp_timer's uptime — because a host build has neither clock. **That half is a firmware-build fact and it has never run on hardware.** `test_care.cpp`'s three cases pin the other end: thirty minutes slept in `PWR_SLEEP_SLICE_MS` slices lands on the same bytes as thirty minutes awake and as one 1800 s call (mutation: dropping `sim.cpp`'s sub-hour remainder carry fails it and two older cases), a stored pebble's `box_recover()` is slice-invariant the same way, and the same gap handed over with `clock_known = 0` charges nothing at all — which is what a sleep misclassified as a crash looks like, and it is silent.
  DIAG's ENERGIA page (GD_SYS_POWER) shows the rung in force, loops/s for all four rungs, total seconds slept, and the two wake pins with the word `light`. **Writing its host case found a real bug**: `s_window_ms == 0` was both "no window yet" and "millisecond zero", so the first window restarted on every call and billed 101 passes to a window that held 100. It is a separate `s_window_armed` flag now.
- Acceptance: gate; §67 "Device sleeps correctly", "Timed systems work after sleep", "No obvious battery-draining loops" (bench: a 30 min deep sleep charges 30 min of care and box recovery; loop rate in IDLE ≤ 10/s).
  **MET ON THE HOST, NOT ON A BENCH, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE CAVEAT.** Gate + matrix green (baseline 1,948,602/73,580; release 1,224,010/49,924 = 76.5 % / 76.8 % of the caps). The 30-minute charge is a host case in `test_care.cpp` and the ≤ 10/s figure is what `pwr_slice_ms()` structurally produces (a 1 s slice in IDLE is ~1 loop/s) and what DIAG's ENERGIA page will read out. **Nothing here has run on hardware**, so the three things a bench must still settle are: that `esp_light_sleep_start()` really returns on a `GPIO_INTR_LOW_LEVEL` from GPIO10 and GPIO2 with the internal pull-ups the buttons use; that the loop rate in IDLE really reads ~1/s on the ENERGIA page; and the current draw, which decides whether the whole ladder was worth its 9.6 K of flash and is dominated by D11's unmeasured boost module rather than by anything in this chunk.

- [x] **THREE DEFECTS WERE FIXED BEFORE THE TAG WAS CUT, AND ONE OF THEM STOPPED THE SHIPPING BUILD FROM RUNNING AT ALL.** They are listed worst first, and each is here because a NAMED test or a gate now fails without the fix.

  **1. THE RELEASE ARTEFACT NEVER ADVANCED GAME TIME.** `app.cpp`'s `logic_tick()` moves the world by `sim_step_seconds() * owed`. `sim_step_seconds()` returns `CareCtx::scale`, a zero-initialised static whose ONLY writer was `sim_set_time_scale()`, whose only two callers are `dev/godmode.cpp:322` and `:595` — **both inside `#if GOD_MODE_ENABLED`**, and the `#else` stub's `god_begin()` does not call it. `release` is `GOD_MODE_ENABLED=0`, the build `config.h` itself calls "the artefact that actually gets flashed", so on that build the scale stayed 0, every tick was `sim_tick(0)`, and care decay, ageing, poop/sickness, the XP carry drip and the activity carried minute were all dead for as long as the device was switched on. `gs_touch_lastseen()` still ran, so the next boot's absence was ~0 too: the pet decayed **only for time the device spent powered OFF**. Pre-existing (the stub dates to phase 2), but P6-C3 rewrote that exact line into `sim_step_seconds() * owed` and rested the whole sleep ladder on it.
  **NOTHING IN THE SUITE COULD SEE IT, and that is the lesson:** every case in `test_care.cpp` calls `sim_tick(n)` with its own `n`, so the number the firmware multiplies by was never on the line. Measured: planting `uint32_t sim_step_seconds(void){ return 0; }` left the whole suite at **ALL PASS 41/41**. **Fix:** `sim_bind()` sets the default (`if (g.scale == 0u) g.scale = 1u;`) — a default that lives inside a dev feature is not a default — and god mode is now an override, documented as one in `sim.h`. **Guard:** `care_a_freshly_bound_sim_advances_one_real_second_per_tick`, whose first assertion is the release boot's value and whose second drives `app.cpp`'s own expression over a half-hour sleep. **Mutation:** removing the default fails it with 6 checks (`sim_step_seconds() 0 != 1`, then five care bars that never moved), 27/28.

  **2. A TYPED CLOCK PLUS A REBOOT WAS AN UNBOUNDED ACTIVITY FARM.** Written up in full in the P6-C2 box above, because that is where the sentence it falsified lives. Summary: `xp_ledger_restore()` aged the saved budget forward by a wall-clock gap the player types on the time screen. **990 metered XP and 125,000 care milli-points in zero real seconds, against 37.8 XP per honest day.** Fix in three parts — the aging term removed (`game/xp.cpp`), the happiness scaled by the XP the ledger actually granted (`act_happy_for_granted_xp`, because the happiness had **no meter at all** and needed no reboot to farm), and the false comments in `game/activity.h`, `game/xp.h` and `app/app.cpp` corrected to what the code does. **Guards:** `activity.o` is linked into `test_xp` (the farm is cross-module and neither module's own binary could see it) plus `a_typed_day_plus_a_reboot_cannot_refill_a_spent_activity_budget`, `a_typed_day_without_a_reboot_cannot_pay_a_second_time_either` and `an_honest_day_still_pays_what_it_always_paid`. **Measured after:** the same 100 rounds, given a FULL bucket to start with, spend **exactly `XP_CAP_CARRY` (48) and then nothing for ever**; the honest day is unchanged at 37.8/day.
  **WHAT THE HONEST PLAYER PAYS FOR IT, said plainly:** time the device spends switched OFF no longer refills the XP budget. The meter now means "XP per day of device-**ON** time" rather than per day of wall time. It is smaller, it is one-directional (never in the player's favour), and it is the only direction that cannot be typed. Two existing cases were NARROWED rather than deleted and both narrowings are recorded in their own bodies: `a_round_trip_under_reports_the_budget_and_never_over_reports_it`'s `CHECK_NEAR(live, back, 1)` becomes `CHECK_EQ(back, at_save)`, and `the_battle_bucket_bounds_an_afternoon_of_fighting` drove its "a reboot cannot shortcut it" claim **through the very call that was the shortcut** and now drives it through `xp_ledger_tick()`.

  **MEASURED AT THE TAG.** Gate green, all seven matrix variants at 0 project warnings. Baseline **1,948,802 / 73,580** (81.2 % / 81.8 % of `GATE_FLASH_MAX` / `GATE_GLOBALS_MAX`); release **1,224,210 / 49,924** = **76.5 % / 76.8 %** of the caps `build_matrix.sh` enforces, and 38.9 % of the 3,145,728 B `app0` slot. Phase 6 is **+20,866 flash / +400 globals** on `0.5.0-explore` — **174 % of the top of its own 8-12 K flash line** and half the low end of its globals line, with ~17 K of the overrun being LEDC and `esp_sleep` linking into the tree for the first time and paid once. Host suite 37 → **41 binaries, 708 → 787 tests, 1,802,703 → 3,658,841 checks**. `CONTENT_VERSION` unchanged at 0x02B5, screen goldens unchanged at 60. Sanitizers by hand over the three changed binaries (ASan+UBSan, on a copy with the two known GCC 13.3 constexpr-fold lines neutralised exactly as the phase-5 exit recorded): `test_xp` 19/19, `test_activity` 21/21, `test_care` 28/28, **zero reports**. `docs/decisions.md` carries the variant table, the per-commit table and the three defects; `docs/budget.md`'s P6 row is closed and its section-3 projection re-scored; `CHANGELOG.md` has `[0.6.0-activity]`.

P6-C3's ONE LOAD-BEARING LINE HAD NO GUARD.** `gt_mono_ms()`'s `ARDUINO` branch (`esp_rtc_get_time_us() / 1000ULL`) is the whole of "a monotonic source that survives sleep" and the whole of the uncalibrated-cooldown debt's discharge, and reverting it to `millis()` — the exact regression the chunk was written to prevent — is invisible to the host suite, because no host binary compiles that branch. `tools/check.sh` gated `ui_explore_clock()`, the CALLER, and nothing gated the line. **Fix:** a second gate in the same shape, on `gt_mono_ms()`'s device branch: it must name `esp_rtc_get_time_us()` and must not name `millis()`. **Mutations, both halves:** swapping the source to `millis()` → `GATE FAIL: gt_mono_ms() does not read esp_rtc_get_time_us() on the device`; adding a `millis()` call beside the correct source → `GATE FAIL: gt_mono_ms()'s device branch reads millis() (1)`. A host test that compiles that branch against fake Arduino headers would be stronger and is carried into phase 7 rather than claimed here.
