  1. **The modem-sleep check, and it is the one to run first.** Fresh boot → LINK → count beacons received in 10 s. Then boot → NETWORK (a full scan) → back → LINK → count again. `WiFiGenericClass::_sleepEnabled` defaults to `WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM` on the C3, it is applied at STA start, the prebuilt libs have `CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE=y` (so it bites an UNASSOCIATED station), and `net.cpp`'s `scan_begin()` sets the cached value to `true` — a static that survives a mode change and `net_request(RADIO_OFF)`. `link_begin()` calls `WiFi.setSleep(false)` BEFORE `WiFi.mode(WIFI_STA)` for exactly that reason. A materially lower second count means the call is missing or ordered wrong.
  2. **Two boards see each other's beacon in LINK** (the acceptance above).
  3. **Unicast and the send-callback ACK after HELLO**: `espnow_stats().tx_ok` rising and `espnow_ever_acked()` true after the first unicast to a bound peer.
  4. **`rx_ring_overflow` after a real battle.** `LINK_RX_SLOTS` is 32 because a lossy link was measured to burst 19 frames in one direction; on air that counter is the only thing that can say the depth is wrong, because a frame lost to ring overflow is indistinguishable to `session.cpp` from one the radio dropped.
  5. **Channel and residency**: that a device entering LINK after a §40 scan still hears beacons (the scan sweeps 1..13 and leaves the radio wherever the last dwell ended; `link_begin()` re-applies `PB_LINK_CHANNEL` on every bring-up because the channel is not stored in NVS).

- [x] **WHAT P7-C1 BUILT, AND THE THREE THINGS IT FOUND.** Files: `networking/rxring.{h,cpp}` (the SPSC ring, pure and caller-owned), `networking/discovery.{h,cpp}` (the beacon codec and the peer table, pure and caller-owned, reaching the radio through a four-call `LinkRadioDriver`), `networking/transport_espnow.{h,cpp}` (the only file in the tree that includes `esp_now.h`), `NPH_LINK` and `net_link_driver()` in `net.{h,cpp}`, and two host binaries — 41 → **43**, 787 → **827 tests**, 3,658,841 → **3,672,108 checks**.

  **1. THE SEAM HELD, AND THAT IS THE HEADLINE.** `networking/session.cpp` and `networking/battle_link.cpp` are BYTE-IDENTICAL across this chunk: the radio arrived behind `transport.h` with no widening, no new field and no `#ifdef`. `ctx` carries the endpoint, `mtu` is `PROTO_FRAME_MAX` (164, not `ESP_NOW_MAX_DATA_LEN`'s 250 — the tighter ceiling is the correct one), `send` returns true for `ESP_OK` only, and the send-callback ACK is deliberately NOT plumbed into that bool, because `transport.h:36-40` requires a refused send and a dropped frame to be indistinguishable and wiring the ACK back would create a path the loopback cannot exercise. `tests/test_link_transport.cpp` runs **a whole battle between two real sessions over a Transport whose `recv()` IS `rxring_pop()`** — the same ring code the Wi-Fi task fills — so "the session runs over the radio unchanged" is demonstrated and not asserted. **A new gate makes it stay true:** `session.cpp` and `battle_link.cpp` may contain zero preprocessor conditionals.

  **2. THE RING DEPTH THE SURVEY PROPOSED WAS WRONG BY A FACTOR OF FOUR, AND IT WOULD HAVE FAILED SILENTLY.** The survey argued from the protocol's shape that "a lockstep round has at most a handful of frames in flight per direction … Eight is generous, not tuned". **Measured over 300 varied battles: one `session_poll()` puts up to TEN frames in the peer's ring on a clean link and NINETEEN at a 10 % per-frame drop**, where the nine-rung ladder re-sends while the peer is still catching up. Eight slots hold seven and refused three frames per clean battle; sixteen hold fifteen and would still have overflowed. `LINK_RX_SLOTS` is **32**, and the failure mode is why the number is measured rather than reasoned about: **a frame lost to ring overflow is indistinguishable to `session.cpp` from a frame the radio dropped**, so the symptom on a bench would have been a link quietly slower than it should be, with no error anywhere. `a_lossy_link_bursts_harder_than_a_clean_one_and_the_ring_still_absorbs_it` fails if the constant goes back down, and asserts the burst really did exceed fifteen so it cannot pass vacuously.

  **3. THE HARDWARE ADDRESS HAS ONE HOME AND A GATE THAT SAYS SO.** The sender's six bytes live in `s_slot_addr[LINK_PEER_CAP][6]` inside `transport_espnow.cpp`; what crosses into `discovery.h` — and therefore into the game, the UI and anything persisted — is an opaque `slot` byte. `tools/check.sh` fails the build if `discovery.{h,cpp}` so much as NAMES that identifier, in a field, a parameter or a comment (the prose cost is paid in `transport_espnow.h`, which is the file that legitimately handles it). **The shape not to copy is in this tree right now:** `core/nt_types.h`'s `BlePeerInfo` has the raw address as its first member and `ble_social.cpp:243` copies it out of the scan callback into the table the game reads. **And the gate's limit is stated rather than glossed: it catches a RENAME and it cannot see a VALUE** — six bytes through two `uint32_t` fields would leave it green, exactly as `check.sh:490-509` measured for `ScanResult`'s padding — so `a_disc_peer_has_no_room_to_hide_a_hardware_address` pins `sizeof(DiscPeer)` and every offset, and the struct has one declared spare byte and no room for six.

  **A NARROWING OF D2, RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE DISCOVERED.** `docs/decisions.md`'s D2 consequences say "the discovery beacon announces the channel and the session pins it to `PB_LINK_CHANNEL`; P7-C1 must handle a peer found on another channel by re-tuning before HELLO, not by failing." **The beacon carries no channel field and there is no re-tune path.** Both devices set `PB_LINK_CHANNEL` on every LINK bring-up, so a peer on another channel is not discoverable at all rather than discoverable-and-unreachable, and there is nothing to re-tune to. That is a smaller design than D2 described; it is written into `docs/decisions.md` under P7-C1 with the reason, and the cost is stated there too — a device left on another channel by something outside this firmware is invisible, and only a bench can say whether that ever happens.

  **MUTATION-TESTED.** Nineteen source mutations and seven gate mutations were planted and each one made a NAMED case or a NAMED gate fail; the table is in `docs/decisions.md`. Four are worth naming here because they are the ones a review would not catch: overwriting at `tail` when the ring is full (fails on the PAYLOAD comparison, not merely on a counter), clamping an oversize record to `stride` instead of refusing it (fails on "the ring is still empty"), returning 0 from `rxring_pop()` for a record that does not fit the caller's buffer — the "0 is a lie" path `transport_loopback.cpp:107-118` wrote down for P7 to inherit — and moving the `act_note_peer()` call out of its "just qualified" branch so it runs per beacon.

  **MEASURED AT THE COMMIT.** All seven matrix variants green at 0 project warnings, `MATRIX OK`. Baseline 1,948,802 / 73,580 -> **1,958,560 / 79,412**; release 1,224,210 / 49,924 -> **1,233,762 / 55,740** = **77.1 % / 85.8 %** of `GATE_RELEASE_FLASH_MAX` / `GATE_RELEASE_GLOBALS_MAX`, and 39.2 % of the 3,145,728 B `app0` slot. **+9,758 flash / +5,832 globals on the baseline, and 5,312 of those globals are one buffer** (`s_rx_bytes`, the session ring) whose size was measured rather than guessed; `docs/budget.md` §4 attributes all 5,832 symbol by symbol and records that `rxring.cpp` and `discovery.cpp` contribute ZERO, which is what the purity gate buys. Host suite 41 -> **43 binaries, 787 -> 827 tests, 3,658,841 -> 3,672,108 checks**. `CONTENT_VERSION` unchanged; screen goldens unchanged (no screen was touched).

  **NOT MEASURED, NOT TESTED, NOT CLAIMED.** No radio ran. The channel, the broadcast peer entry, the receive callback's threading and preemption, ESP-NOW's own duplicate suppression and MTU enforcement, the modem-sleep default and whether two boards can hear each other are all bench facts. The SPSC claim rests on the discipline in `rxring.h` and on the two precedents it names (`hardware/input.cpp`, `networking/ble_social.cpp`); a host binary is single-threaded and is not evidence about the Wi-Fi task.

**P7-C2 LINK screen, consent, BOX → LINK entry points** — M
- [x] Fold or drop `STR_SO_LINK_SOON` ("LINK - Fase 7"), the P2-C7b placeholder: it shows an internal plan phase number to the player. **DONE, AND ITS TWIN WENT WITH IT.** `STR_LINK_PHASE` ("Enlace - Fase 7") was the string the LINK screen actually drew and it had the same defect; both are deleted from `core/strings_es.h` (enum row and table row), and `tests/golden/screens/link_phase7.pbm` is deleted with the frame that drew them. The placeholders that remain — `STR_SOON_TRADE`, `STR_SOON_BREED` and `STR_PHASE_7` on `SCR_TRADE` / `SCR_BREED` — are kept ON PURPOSE and are named here rather than swept: those two screens really are placeholders (P7-C4 and P7-C5), so a line saying so is honest where LINK's was not.
- [x] `LINK` state: peer list → "¡PEBBLEBOL ENCONTRADO! [COMBATE][INTERCAMBIO][CRIAR][CANCELAR]" (§42) → `SESSION_REQUEST/ACCEPT` requires A on **both** devices; both show peer name + operation + state; every wait has a timeout → "CONEXIÓN PERDIDA / A: Reintentar / B: Salir" (§47).
  **HOW "A ON BOTH" IS BUILT, because the shape matters more than the tick.** It is not a dialog in front of an automatic handshake: it is the ONLY thing that starts one. Until the local player has opened the card and pressed A there is **no Session on this device at all** — `session_init()` has not been called, so nothing drains the transport and a peer's HELLO is not even looked at — and no unicast peer is bound, so on the device `transport_espnow.cpp`'s receive callback counts that HELLO as `rx_wrong_peer` and drops it inside the Wi-Fi task. Two independent gates, and both have to go for a device to be dragged into a session it never agreed to. The handshake is SYMMETRIC (`session.h`: "both endpoints may call `session_start()` — the ROLES are decided by device id, not by who spoke first"), so each device speaks when ITS player presses A and the one that pressed alone climbs its nine-rung ladder into §47's "CONEXIÓN PERDIDA / A: Reintentar / B: Salir". `tools/check.sh` now fails the build if `session_init()` or `session_start()` is called anywhere but `ui/screen_link.cpp`.
  **THE OPERATION IS NOT ON THE AIR.** §43/§44 close the beacon's field list at a device id, a name, a version and capabilities; the operation the player picked is not one of them, so two devices that pick different operations discover it in the session and never in a broadcast.
- [x] BOX inspect menu gains "INTERCAMBIAR" and "CRIAR" (§9 "initiate breeding; initiate trade"): pre-selects the Pebble and enters LINK with that intent. `link_arm_intent(op, slot)` + `ui_push(SCR_LINK)`, consuming the arming in the next `link_enter()` exactly as `battle_arm()` is consumed by `battle_enter()`. It consents to nothing and touches no radio; the armed slot LEADS the team the session then offers. **The two OPERATIONS are still P7-C4 and P7-C5 and the card says so** — which is a better answer than the toast this used to be, because the entry point is what P7-C2 owes and a player who takes it now sees the peer list and the real menu.
- [x] `test_screens` snapshots for LINK states. Four: `link_searching`, `link_peers`, `link_card`, `link_lost`. The three modes that need a LIVE session (the wait, the hand-off and the ended page) have no golden and are covered by `every_link_mode_draws_inside_the_panel` in `tests/test_link_screen.cpp` instead — a frame whose content depends on a running handshake is not a frame a golden can hold still, and pretending otherwise is how a golden becomes a picture of a bug.
- [x] **CARRIED FORWARD FROM P7-C1 — `act_note_peer()` HAS ITS FIRST DRIVER.** `ui/screen_link.cpp`'s browse calls `link_service(...)` once per frame, which is the call that pays `act_note_peer()` once per peer that crosses `LINK_PEER_HITS_MIN`. `a_peer_met_over_the_link_is_scored_once_and_the_day_is_saved` drives it through the SCREEN (thirty further beacons from the same device score nothing and dirty nothing), and `ui_explore_commit()` puts the changed day on flash. **The bullet at P7-C1 that this discharges is ticked there.** What has NOT changed: `tests/test_xp.cpp`'s `an_honest_day_still_pays_what_it_always_paid` still measures the 380 / 38 / 4,750 world, because that case constructs its own day and never opens a LINK screen; the term is now reachable in the product, which is what the bullet asked for.
- [x] **`link_is_busy()` IS FOLDED INTO `ui_radio_job_busy()`** — the one line P7-C1 said P7-C2 owed. `ui.cpp` now answers `network_screen_busy() || link_screen_busy()`. **AND `link_screen_busy()` IS THE JOB'S OWN FLAG AND NOTHING ELSE, which took a mutation to establish:** it read `link_is_busy(job) || (a session is live)` for one afternoon, and deleting the second half failed nothing — because a session only ever exists over a job `link_hold()` left `LS_RUNNING`, so the right-hand side could not answer true where the left answered false. That is a sentence wider than the tree, so the SENTENCE was narrowed rather than the test stretched around an unreachable branch, and the dependency on `link_hold()` is written beside it.
- [x] **A NEW CALL IN `networking/discovery.h`: `link_hold()`.** Found rather than planned, and it is the difference between a link that works and one that dies the instant it is agreed. `link_cancel()` reaches `net_request(RADIO_OFF)`, which passes `wifi_down()`, which calls `espnow_end()` — so ending the browse the obvious way would have taken the stack down one frame after the two players consented. `link_hold()` stops the browse (no beacon, no drain, no TTL sweep, no §47 ceiling on the BROWSE) and leaves the job `LS_RUNNING`, so `link_is_busy()` and the power ladder still know the radio is out and `link_cancel()` is still the ONE release path. **The ceiling is not lost, it changes owner:** from there the bound is the session's own ladder, nine rungs of `PROTO_RETX_MS`, an order of magnitude tighter than `LINK_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS`.

**P7-C3 Battle over link (lockstep)** — M
- [x] `BATTLE` state driven by `battle_link.cpp` over the real transport; rewards only on `BATTLE_END(OK)`; `DESYNC` shows a neutral message, no rewards, both Boxes untouched.
  **THE REWARD GATE IS ONE EXPRESSION IN ONE PLACE.** `ui/screen_link.cpp`'s `link_battle_status()` answers `UI_LKB_WON` only where `session_rewards_authorised()` is true — which `networking/battle_link.cpp`'s `compare_end()` sets only when the peer's `BATTLE_END` carried the SAME outcome AND the SAME final hash as ours — and `ui/screen_battle.cpp`'s `won_now()` reads that and nothing else for a linked entry. So `SE_DESYNC` and `SE_LOST` arrive at `ui_battle_result()` as `won == 0` and fall out on its own `if (!won) return;` with no ledger touched and no Box written. `UI_LKB_BROKEN` is deliberately a FOURTH answer and not `UI_LKB_LOST`: a desync is not a defeat, and printing one as the other is the same class of mistake as calling `BO_ABORT` a draw. `tools/check.sh` fails the build if `session_rewards_authorised()` is read anywhere but `ui/screen_link.cpp`.
  **ONE REPORT PATH, REUSED AND NOT DUPLICATED.** P4-C4's `report_once()` is untouched; what changed is `won_now()`, which the link entry answers from the session. That is deliberate — the plan's own note says this bug has been fixed twice in this project — and `two_devices_that_both_consent_fight_one_battle_reported_exactly_once` holds it across the whole battle plus the walk out of the result page.
- [x] **EVERY "SIDE 0 IS THE PLAYER" IN THE BATTLE SCREEN BECAME A BYTE, AND IT HAD TO.** `networking/session.cpp` fixes the two sides from the two device ids (lower id first), so on one board of every pair the player is side 1. Thirty-three lines of `ui/screen_battle.cpp` — the legality predicates, the submit, the two `fill_art()` calls, the chrome's HP pair, the switch list, the outcome word, the armed battle modifier and the four test queries — now read `s_me` / `s_foe()`. Left as literals they would have drawn the PEER's team as the player's on one of the two devices and paid the wrong half of the fight. `the_higher_device_id_plays_side_one_and_still_sees_its_own_team` is the case.
- [x] **`BTM_WAIT`, a sixth mode.** The lockstep has our move and is waiting for the peer's. A mode and not a spinner over `BTM_MENU`, because the menu must not accept a second action for a round the engine already has one for. `tests/test_battle_screen.cpp`'s allocation sweep now ASSERTS that a single-device battle never enters it (that binary links no session at all), and `tests/test_link_screen.cpp` carries the frames-and-allocations half for it.
- [x] **`battle_leave()` HANDS THE LINK BACK, AND THAT IS A HOLE THIS CHUNK FOUND.** `app/state_machine.cpp`'s `sm_home()` (LONG_BOTH) discards the whole back stack and runs ONLY the current screen's `leave()`, so the LINK screen underneath a linked battle would never have been asked — leaving the radio up, the session unpumped and the power ladder clamped at DIM for ever. `battle_leave()` runs on every route off `SCR_BATTLE` (that is the same property its one-report path already depends on), so it is the hook that closes the session and releases the radio. `going_home_from_a_linked_battle_pays_nothing_and_gives_the_radio_back` is the case, and deleting the one line fails it.
- Acceptance: **[ ] §67 "Local multiplayer works" (bench, two boards) — NOT RUN, NO HARDWARE IN THIS ENVIRONMENT.** See the bench list below.
- **MEASURED AND NOT PAPERED OVER — A LINKED ROUND GIVES EACH PLAYER NINE SECONDS TO CHOOSE.** `networking/session.h`'s rule R4 says only PROGRESS resets the retransmission ladder, and `battle_link.cpp`'s `on_battle_state()` counts a waiting peer's probe as STALE — so a round that neither device advances is closed `SE_LOST` after `PROTO_RETX_MAX * PROTO_RETX_MS` = 9,000 ms. **Measured through the real screen and a real peer: 9,950 ms from the round opening to the link dying, with the screen's own countdown reading 8,950 ms at the start of it** (`a_player_who_thinks_longer_than_the_ladder_loses_the_link_and_is_paid_nothing`). The clock resets on every frame that makes progress, so in practice the FIRST mover has nine seconds from the round opening and the SECOND has nine from the first mover's action — but nine seconds is nine seconds, and a player who thinks longer loses the link and is paid nothing.
  **WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT AND WHY NOT MORE.** The screen SHOWS the clock (`RONDA 3 7s` in the linked battle's header) so the deadline arrives as a number rather than as a broken link. Substituting a move on a timeout is forbidden in principle — `battle_link.cpp` says why, and the reason is that the two engines would disagree about the substitute's legality — and stretching the ladder is a change to `session.h`'s own MEASURED tuning (the 3×3000 vs 9×1000 table) which a UI chunk has no business making on the side. **The remedy, if play says nine seconds is too short, is `PROTO_RETX_MAX` / `PROTO_RETX_MS` in `networking/session.h`, and the cost is that every OTHER wait in the protocol gets longer with it.** Decide it in writing; do not do it by hand next to something else.
- [x] **TWENTY SOURCE MUTATIONS AND THREE GATE MUTATIONS, AND FOUR OF THEM WERE NOT CAUGHT ON THE FIRST RUN.** The table is in `docs/decisions.md`. The four are the useful half: a linked result reported the moment the ENGINE finishes (the session normally agrees while the transcript is still playing, so the guard needed a case that DELAYS the peer's confirmation by blocking one `BATTLE_END` by name); the local side hard-coded back to 0 (every assertion still passed, because the session routed the action correctly and only the PANEL had the wrong team on it - `battle_screen_side()` exists because of this); the round transcript never re-opened (the 48-entry ring only overflows in a long fight, so the case now watches the transcript's own ROUND LABEL); and the peer list offering an UNQUALIFIED peer (counting qualified peers is not the same as mapping a list row to one, and the count came from a different function than the row). **AND ONE MUTATION FAILED NOTHING BECAUSE THE CODE WAS WRONG:** `link_screen_busy()`'s second clause was unreachable, so the SENTENCE was narrowed rather than the test stretched to cover a branch that cannot happen.
- **THE BENCH LIST P7-C2/C3 OWES, in the order to run it.** None of it is host-observable and none of it is claimed. It comes AFTER P7-C1's five, and item 1 of that list (the modem-sleep check) is still the first thing to run on a board:
  1. **Two boards list each other in LINK.** This is P7-C1's item 2 and it is also this chunk's gate: `link_qualified_count()` reaching 1 needs three beacons at 500 ms above `LINK_RSSI_MIN`.
  2. **The consent gate, on the air.** Press A on ONE board only and confirm the other stays on its peer list, shows nothing and starts nothing — and that the first board reaches "CONEXIÓN PERDIDA" after about nine seconds. This is the half a host binary CANNOT see: that an un-bound device's receive callback really does drop the initiator's unicast (`espnow_stats().rx_wrong_peer` rising on the silent board).
  3. **§67 "Local multiplayer works".** Press A on both, fight a whole battle, and confirm BOTH panels show the same round numbers, the same HP pair and complementary outcomes — one "¡GANASTE!" and one "Has perdido" — and that exactly one of the two devices toasts the XP line.
  4. **The nine-second move clock, felt rather than measured.** Have one player wait through a round. Both boards must reach the neutral "Enlace interrumpido" and NEITHER may pay.
  5. **The radio after a linked battle.** Leave the battle with LONG_BOTH and confirm on the DIAG screen that the radio is off and the power ladder is no longer clamped at DIM — this is the `sm_home()` hole above, and it is the one thing in this chunk whose failure mode is a battery draining in a pocket.

**P7-C4 Trade (atomic)** — M
- [x] `game/trade.{h,cpp}` + `networking/trade_link.cpp`: OFFER (wire instance) → both `validate_pebble` → READY → CONFIRM → COMMIT (§16); `PendingTrade` (`tr`) written **before** COMMIT with both ids and a phase byte; on boot a pending record in phase < COMMIT rolls back, in COMMIT completes; incoming instance gets a fresh `id` + `TRADED` flag + `origin TRADED`; `save_checkpoint_all()` after; `tests/test_trade.cpp`: never duplicated, never lost.
- [x] **THE FAULT INJECTION IS A SWEEP AND NOT A LIST OF FIVE, AND THE KNOB FOR IT DID NOT EXIST.** `tests/fakes/kv_mem.h` offered `kv_mem_fail_next_put()`, which is ONE-SHOT — a test built on it can only cut where it already knows the index, i.e. at the moments whoever wrote it was already thinking about. `kv_mem_fail_after_n_puts(n)` (six lines) plus `kv_mem_power_restore()` turn a cut into a parameter, and `test_trade.cpp` sweeps **every flash write the sequence performs** (measured: 10 per committed trade — 5 through the store seam and 5 inside `save_checkpoint_all()`), plus the four writes that open the journal, plus nine cut points **inside the resolver itself**, each followed by a reboot, a resolve, the pair invariant and a SECOND reboot that must change nothing.
- [x] **THE ARITHMETIC MISMATCH IN THIS BULLET, NAMED RATHER THAN QUIETLY RESOLVED.** "Each of the 5 phases" counts WIRE steps (OFFER / validate / READY / CONFIRM / COMMIT); `PendingTrade.phase` encodes **three** non-idle phases. Both are right: the journal deliberately collapses the five, because only three of them change what BOOT has to do. `docs/protocol.md` §23 has the table.
- [x] **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 1 → 2, AND THE REASON THIS PLAN AND `docs/protocol.md` BOTH GAVE WAS FALSE.** Both said P7's trade messages would exceed `PROTO_PAYLOAD_MAX 148`. A `TRADE_OFFER` is 4 + 48 = **52** bytes and the cap did not move. The bump was mandatory for a different reason — the **type space was closed** at 1..12 and `proto_decode()` refuses anything else with `PE_TYPE` — so the conclusion survived and the reason did not. Four types appended (13..16), two reserved bytes became fields (`SESSION_REQUEST.rules` = the operation, `SESSION_ACCEPT.op_echo` = the answer), `PROTO_PAYLOAD_MAX` unchanged at 148, the golden ACTION frame re-recorded with ONLY byte 0 and its two CRC bytes moved.
- [x] **TWO DEFECTS THE LOSSY ARM FOUND AND A CLEAN LINK COULD NOT.** Both are "decide from the arrival that happened to be last" rather than from state, the same shape as the R4 hole P4-C5 found. (1) A READY that arrived before its own OFFER left an endpoint parked in `TLP_REVIEW` with both halves in hand — **74 of 200 trials at 10 % drop, 183 of 200 at 30 %** — because `maybe_ask_player()` was called from one handler only. (2) The ladder REGENERATES a frame from state rather than replaying bytes, so the new operation byte had to be written in TWO places, and `resend()`'s `SESSION_REQUEST` did not carry it: the retransmitted request said "battle" while its sender was trading, the responder refused `SD_OP`, and the initiator read the refusal's `SESSION_ACCEPT` as an agreement — **34 of 200 at 10 % drop, 0 of 200 clean.** Both are now pinned by named cases that kill exactly one frame.
- [x] **TWO PRE-EXISTING DEFECTS IN THE PERSISTENCE LAYER, FIXED HERE BECAUSE THE JOURNAL RESTS ON THEM.** (a) `commit_all()` rewrote five of the six blobs and **not** the trade journal, so after the SAVE ERROR screen's "Recuperar" RAM said IDLE and flash still held a live record, which the next boot then resolved against a Box restored from a checkpoint that predates it. One line, and `the_checkpoint_recovery_path_no_longer_leaves_a_stale_journal_behind` fails without it. (b) `save_checkpoint_all()` does **not** checkpoint the journal and must not — a mid-trade checkpoint is a second, stale source of truth for one transaction — which is now written into `save_manager.h` rather than left as an omission.
- [x] **`gs.trade` HAD NO READER AND THE JOURNAL WAS BYTES NOBODY USED.** `save_load_all()` loaded it and `grep -rn 'gs.trade' app/ ui/ game/` returned nothing. `app_setup()` now calls `boot_trade()` between `gs_load()` and `boot_box()` — before the pet is bound, because the resolver can change which Pebble the Box holds — and the player is told with `STR_TR_RESOLVED` / `STR_TR_ROLLED_BACK` rather than watching a Pebble silently appear or silently go.
- [x] **THE LINK SCREEN DRIVES IT, SO THE RELEASE ARTEFACT CAN ACTUALLY TRADE.** P7-C2 left INTERCAMBIO as a row that answered "Aún no está listo"; it opens a real trade session now, and `LK_SELF_CAPS` claims `DISC_CAP_TRADE` so a peer running this firmware offers exactly the operations that exist (CRIAR is still not claimed, and the comment at that line says why). **THERE IS NO SIXTH SCREEN MODE**: the review happens inside `LKM_WAIT`, which already shows the peer, the operation and the state, and which swaps the state word for "what goes → what comes" and offers `A` the moment both devices have validated both records. A mode whose only difference is two lines of text is a mode nobody can reason about, and it would have cost a golden that photographs a frame whose content depends on a live handshake.
- [x] **THE SECOND CONSENT IS A DIFFERENT QUESTION FROM THE FIRST AND THE SCREEN ASKS BOTH.** The A on the card agrees to TALK TO THIS PEER; the A on the review agrees to THESE TWO PEBBLES, which neither player could see until both records were on the table. `networking/trade_link.cpp` applies only where it holds BOTH confirms, and `tests/test_link_screen.cpp` drives the whole exchange through two gestures a player could make with no back door: before the second A the Box is byte-identical and the journal is at `TRADE_RECEIVED`; after it the Box has swapped and the journal is `TRADE_IDLE`.
- [x] **THE PLAYER HAS ABOUT NINE SECONDS TO ANSWER THE REVIEW, AND IT IS THE SAME LADDER P7-C3 ALREADY RECORDED.** Once both READYs are in, this endpoint owes a CONFIRM; only PROGRESS resets the ladder and a peer that has already confirmed sends nothing new, so a review nobody answers closes `SE_LOST` after `PROTO_RETX_MAX * PROTO_RETX_MS`. **Nothing is lost when it expires** - the journal rolls back and both players keep what they had - so it is a shorter deadline than a battle round's on a question that needs less thought. The remedy and its cost are P7-C3's, unchanged: the two constants in `networking/session.h`, which lengthen every other wait in the protocol with them. Written into `ui/screen_link.h` rather than discovered on a bench.
- [x] **WALKING AWAY CLEARS THE JOURNAL RATHER THAN LEAVING IT FOR THE NEXT BOOT.** `release_all()` runs the abort hook for a trade that never reached its commit, so a player who backs out does not get an "Intercambio cancelado" toast a whole power cycle later. The boot resolver stays the backstop for the case a screen cannot reach, which is the power cut.
- [x] **`BOXF_TRADE_PENDING` STILL HAS NO WRITER, DELIBERATELY.** Within one device the journal alone answers every question at boot, so a flag would be a second source of truth for a fact already held; the half it was reserved for is the CROSS-PAIR rendezvous (compare two journals at the next LINK with the same `peer_id` and let the side that did not apply apply then), which is NOT built here. `game/trade.h` says so in those words.
- [x] **A `TDR_LAST_PEBBLE` CODE WAS WRITTEN, MEASURED TO BE UNREACHABLE, AND DELETED.** "The last Pebble on the device cannot be given away" is a real rule and `TDR_ACTIVE` plus Box invariant B3 already hold it: `box_active()` runs `mask_sync()`, which repairs an `active_slot` pointing nowhere to the lowest occupied slot, so a Box with one Pebble always reports that Pebble as active. A named reject nothing can return is a name, not a rule.
- Acceptance: §67 "Trade is atomic" — **within one device, PROVED and swept; across the pair, BOUNDED and MEASURED, not proved.** Two parties over a lossy link with no third party cannot make an exchange atomic (the last message of any handshake is unacknowledged), so `game/trade.h` states the impossibility, `COMMIT` subsumes `CONFIRM` to narrow the window from one lost frame to a CONFIRM and a COMMIT lost together for a whole ladder, and `test_trade.cpp` reports the residual as a table: **800 trials over four fault arms, 0 SPLIT** (200/200 clean, 200/200 at 10 % drop, 200/200 at 10 % drop + dup + reorder, 195/200 at 30 % drop + 20 % reorder). That is a measurement over this loopback and this fault model and it is not evidence about a radio.
- [ ] **BENCH, AND IT IS THE ONLY WAY TO CLOSE THIS: TRADE A PEBBLE BETWEEN TWO REAL BOARDS.** It comes AFTER P7-C1's five and P7-C2/C3's five. Both boards list each other, both players pick INTERCAMBIO, both press A, and afterwards each board's BOX holds the other's Pebble exactly once with `origen: INTERCAMBIO`. Then repeat it and **pull the battery out of one board between the two A presses**, power it back on, and confirm it shows "Intercambio completado" or "Intercambio cancelado" and that the two Boxes across the pair hold exactly two Pebbles between them. No host binary can see any of it: the loopback delivers regardless of binding, and a `kv_mem` power cut is not a flash erase.

**P7-C5 Breeding** — M
- [x] **The god-taint gate is re-implemented in the game layer**, as `game/taint.h` — two inline functions, no `.cpp`, shared verbatim by `game/trade.cpp` and `game/breeding.cpp`. It is NOT in `validate_pebble()` (a tainted Pebble is a legal OBJECT and `PBF_GOD_TAINTED` is inside `VLD_FLAGS_MASK` on purpose; a rule there would quarantine a god-mode tester's own device) and NOT in the transport (that is what P2-C7b correctly removed). `pb_is_tainted()` reads **both** markers — the genome bit AND `PBF_GOD_TAINTED` — because they have independent writers: `persistence/migration.cpp` sets the flag from a v1 save without touching the genome. Reading one is a hole with a name, and the test drives each marker on its own. The rule is the one P2-C7b deleted: a clean unit refuses a tainted one, a tainted unit accepts anything, evaluated by each side on its own incoming record. **It is honour-based and `game/taint.h` says so**: a peer running modified firmware clears the bit and the gate cannot tell.
- [x] `game/breeding.{h,cpp}` over `genome_breed()`: compatibility = same non-zero `compat_group`, both stage ≥ 1, distinct ids (§17) — `SpeciesDef.compat_group` has existed since P4-C1 and was **read by nothing in `src/`** until now; offspring species = base stage of the family of the parent chosen by the shared seed; genome variation clamped to the genesis envelope; `generation = max+1` saturating; both devices compute the same child from the shared seed, through a `genome_set_rng()` source and **never** a `genome_seed()` reseed (`game/capture.h` names that trap and this is the first caller to walk around it); the child goes to the Box only on an A-confirm with a free slot (audit risk 5: no auto-accepted egg), through `box_new_pebble()` and then `validate_pebble()` before the slot is kept.
- [x] **TWO BULLETS ABOVE WERE REWRITTEN RATHER THAN IMPLEMENTED, AND HERE IS WHY.**
  **(a) "One inherited move from each parent if legal" CANNOT BE BUILT AGAINST THE SHIPPED VALIDATOR.** `validate.cpp`'s `moveset_is_learnable()` accepts a moveset only when it is the verbatim, in-order `moves[4]` of some species in the same family at this stage **or below**; a stage-0 offspring's only candidate is its own base row, so ANY substituted move is `VR_UNLEARNABLE_MOVESET` — the child would be quarantined on the next load and refused by `pbw_decode()` on the wire. Widening the validator to set membership reverses a rule with a MEASUREMENT behind it (injecting attack 11 onto a species-1 Paketo took a scripted 1v1 from 0/200 wins to 100) and would have to be widened identically in `battle.cpp`'s second checker, which is pinned to the first by `the_two_learnset_checkers_agree_on_every_roster_row`. The offspring gets its base species' learnset verbatim and `breeding.h` says why.
  **(b) "`lineage_id = hash(A,B)`" IS A TRAP.** `genome_breed()` already sets `lineage_id` and `Genome.crc16` covers bytes 0..13 INCLUDING it, so overwriting without `genome_seal()` produces a genome that fails `genome_valid()` → `VR_BAD_GENOME` on the next load. `genome_breed()`'s own rule is kept (higher `cq_hi` parent, 6 % founder mutation), and since the BLE beacon that used to supply `cq_hi` is going away it is handed the parent's LEVEL instead — a number both devices already have, on the 48 B wire record, deterministic.
- [x] **THE "HARD BALANCE CEILING" IS TWO HALVES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM COULD EVER BE BROKEN, WHICH THE TESTS SAY OUT LOUD.** The BATTLE half is structural: `gvar(v) = v*3/16` folds 0..15 to 0..2 whatever any number of generations does, so a case asserting it CANNOT FAIL and is labelled as such. What is not free is the CARE envelope: `genome_breed()`'s mutation reflects inside the gene's own 0..15, and `gene_appetite_mult()` / `gene_metabolism_mult()` / `gene_hardiness_mult()` run the full 600..1500 per-mille. `breed_compute()` clamps the six numeric genes back into the genesis band and `GENESIS_LUCK_MIN/MAX` moved to `core/nt_types.h` so the band has ONE definition. **MEASURED, both arms in one case: 200 dynasties × 40 generations of UNCLAMPED breeding left the band 8,655 times and reached the full 0..15; the clamped arm left it 0 times** — and the case asserts the control arm escapes, or the clamp would be a guard nobody can fail.
- [x] `tests/test_breeding.cpp` — 16 cases, 95,381 checks. The compat matrix is **all 36 × 36 roster pairs with the exact `BreedReject` for each** (1,296 pairs: 120 OK, 720 `BRD_STAGE`, 456 `BRD_COMPAT_GROUP`) plus symmetry, because a handful of hand-picked pairs passes with the stage rule deleted; the offspring passes `validate_pebble()` over 400 seeds and takes both parents' families; the budget ceiling over 10,000 random pairs; determinism (256 seeds byte-identical, and 256 of 256 seeds produce a DIFFERENT child, so a "deterministic" child that ignored the seed would fail); `genome_valid()` on every child; and that breeding neither reseeds nor consumes `RNG_BREEDING`.
- [ ] **`breed_link.cpp` IS NOT BUILT, THE RELEASE IMAGE THEREFORE CONTAINS NO BREEDING AT ALL, AND THIS IS THE ONE PLACE THAT SAYS SO.** MEASURED, not assumed: `riscv32-esp-elf-nm -S` over the release `.elf` finds no `breed_check`, no `breed_compute` and no `genome_breed` — nothing reachable from `setup()` calls them, so `--gc-sections` dropped the module and this chunk's 8,872 B of flash is the TRADE's end to end. **That check is here because of what the phase-6 exit found** (the shipping build never advanced game time, for four phases, because a default lived inside a dev-only function): ask what the RELEASE artefact does, not what the build in front of you compiles. `game/breeding.cpp` is complete, tested and *callable*, and the *shared seed* it needs already exists (`session_derive_seed()`), but no wire driver carries a breeding and the LINK card's CRIAR row still answers `STR_UI_SOON`. The reason is scope, not difficulty: P7-C4 spent its budget on the trade's atomicity, and a second wire operation means a second set of protocol types, a second `SessionOp`, a second screen mode and a second lossy-arm table. **What exists is the half a host binary can prove; what is missing is the half only a bench can.** Carried to P7-C6.
- Acceptance: §67 "Breeding compatibility works" (host-proved: the 36 × 36 matrix), "Generated Pebbles remain balanced" (host-proved: the envelope clamp with its unclamped control arm). **The two-device half of "breeding works" is NOT claimed** — see the bullet above.

**P7-C6 Phase-7 exit** — S
- [x] Matrix + tag `v0.7.0-social`; D2 outcome recorded; if BLE was kept, the measured leak x 32 sessions must fit the heap budget or `FEATURE_BLE` goes to 0.
  **DONE, AND THE LAST CLAUSE DOES NOT APPLY — SAID EXPLICITLY RATHER THAN LEFT LOOKING FORGOTTEN.** BLE WAS kept, but not because the leak was found acceptable: `FEATURE_BLE` is already **0 in the `release` variant**, which is the artefact that ships, so nothing in the shipping image opens a BLE session at all and 32 x ~672 B is 32 x a session no shipping build starts. What is kept is the SOURCE, in the baseline and dev builds, as the only fallback transport until ESP-NOW has run on a board. The leak was never measured (D2's own consequence list says it "no longer needs measuring", correctly), and measuring it now would be measuring a subsystem this firmware is about to delete. **`docs/decisions.md`'s "Phase-7 exit" carries the D2 outcome table and the exact bench test that unblocks the deletion.**
  **THE MATRIX: all seven variants green at 0 project warnings.** baseline 1,994,460 / 80,492; no-ble 1,281,842 / 56,996; no-web 1,936,472 / 78,444; no-god 1,981,944 / 80,332; sh1106 1,994,460 / 80,492; all-off 578,868 / 26,388; **release 1,269,468 / 56,820** = 79.3 % / 87.4 % of the caps. Phase 7 total: **+45,658 flash / +6,912 globals** on the baseline, **over `docs/budget.md`'s 20-35 KB / 2.0-4.0 KB allowance on both axes**, and §7 there says where every byte of the overrun went.
- [ ] **BENCH, NOT RUN, NO HARDWARE IN THIS ENVIRONMENT — THE TWO ITEMS THAT UNBLOCK EVERYTHING ELSE.** P7-C1's "two boards see each other's beacon in LINK" and §67's "Local multiplayer works". They are left unticked on purpose and they are not a formality: **the BLE deletion (712,618 B of flash, 23,496 B of globals, re-measured at this tag), `breed_link.cpp` and the cross-pair trade rendezvous are all downstream of the first of them.** What the owner must run, in order, is the eleven-item bench list P7-C1 and P7-C2/C3 wrote out above; its **first** item is the modem-sleep check and its **second** is this one: flash two boards, open LINK on both, confirm each lists the other — `link_qualified_count()` reaching 1 needs three beacons at 500 ms above `LINK_RSSI_MIN` (-70 dBm), inside `LINK_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` (90 s). **If it passes:** `ble_social.{h,cpp}` may be deleted, `FEATURE_BLE` removed, `no-ble` promoted to the baseline and `BlePeerInfo` taken out of `core/nt_types.h`. **If it fails:** run bench item 1 first — `WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM` is the C3 default, it bites an UNASSOCIATED station, and `net.cpp`'s `scan_begin()` caches `true` in a static that survives a mode change and `net_request(RADIO_OFF)`.
- [x] **THE EXIT'S BLOCKING FINDING: A SUCCESSFUL TRADE DESTROYED A PEBBLE ON THE SHIPPING BUILD. FIXED, GATED AND MUTATION-TESTED.** `game/trade.cpp` writes B1 (clear the outgoing slot) then B2 (file the incoming one), and `box_add()` fills `first_free()` — **the slot B1 just released** — so the sequence writes ONE KEY TWICE microseconds apart. `save_manager.cpp:350` defers a second write of one key inside `SAVE_MIN_GAP_MS` (1,000 ms), `force` does NOT bypass that branch, and it **returns true**. Both P7-C4 shims (`ui/ui.cpp`, `app/app.cpp`) returned that `true` as `TradeStore.write_slot`, whose contract in `game/trade.h` is literally "every function returns whether the bytes LANDED" — so B2 never reached flash, B3 wrote the Box header and W4 cleared the journal, leaving nothing to repair it. **The boot resolver carried the identical defect through the identical shim, so the last line of defence destroyed what it exists to save.**
  **REPRODUCED INDEPENDENTLY BEFORE IT WAS FIXED AND IT IS WORSE THAN THE REPORT SAID:** with `app/app.cpp`'s own clock wiring bound in the fixture, the CLEAN trade case fails with **no fault injection at all** — Box count 2 instead of 3, `traded == 0`, and **9 flash writes instead of 10**.
  **THE FIXTURE IS WHY IT SURVIVED 27,819 CHECKS.** `tests/test_trade.cpp:106` passed `save_set_clock(nullptr, ...)`, which switches OFF the entire wear-filter branch (`if (s_now_ms && s_have_written[slot])`); `app/app.cpp:804` binds a real one. The 15-point kill sweep, the 9-point resolver sweep and the 800-trial lossy table all ran against a `save_manager` **the release artefact does not execute**. This is the phase-6 finding with the polarity inverted: there a default lived inside a dev-only path, here the DEFAULT is the safe one and the SHIPPING wiring is the dangerous one.
  **THE FIX IS A SECOND ENTRY POINT AND NOT A WIDENED FLAG.** `save_pebble_now()` writes with no filter and no deferral and returns whether the bytes landed. `force=true` was deliberately NOT widened: `persistence/game_state.cpp` calls it on every care action and RELIES on the deferral for flash wear. **Two gates, both proven to bite:** `save_pebble()` may not be called from `app/` or `ui/`, and no host test may bind `save_set_clock(nullptr, ...)`. **Three named cases:** `a_second_write_of_one_slot_inside_the_floor_is_deferred_and_says_so`, `save_pebble_now_reaches_flash_twice_inside_the_floor_and_never_says_it_did_not`, `a_slot_out_of_range_is_refused_by_both_write_paths` — plus the whole trade sweep, which now runs against the shipping clock.
- [ ] **CARRIED FORWARD TO PHASE 8 — `breed_link.cpp` IS NOT BUILT AND `DISC_CAP_BREED` IS NOT CLAIMED.** `game/breeding.{h,cpp}` is complete, tested against the whole 36 x 36 roster matrix and reachable, and the shared seed it needs already exists (`session_derive_seed()`); what is missing is the wire driver. **RE-CONFIRMED AT THIS EXIT WITH `nm` OVER THE RELEASE `.elf`:** no `breed_check`, no `breed_compute`, no `genome_breed` — the shipping artefact cannot breed, and §67's "Breeding compatibility works" and "Generated Pebbles remain balanced" stay host-proved-only for that reason. The shape is known because P7-C4 built its twin: a `SessionOp`, four protocol types behind a version bump, a caller-owned link struct, a review step inside `LKM_WAIT` and a lossy-arm table. **It is not a small job and it must not be done next to something else** — both devices have to compute a BYTE-IDENTICAL child from one seed, and `game/breeding.h` states the two consequences that makes real: the draw ORDER inside `genome_breed()` becomes a cross-device contract, and the argument order (A is the INITIATOR's parent on both devices) is what `the_two_ends_must_agree_on_which_parent_is_a` measures at 200 differing children out of 200 seeds. **Do it after the bench, not before**: a second wire operation over a transport that has never linked two boards is a second thing to debug at once.
- [ ] **CARRIED FORWARD TO PHASE 8 — THE CROSS-PAIR TRADE RENDEZVOUS.** `PendingTrade.peer_id` is written on every record and `BOXF_TRADE_PENDING` still has no writer, both on purpose: the half they were reserved for is comparing two journals at the next LINK with the same peer and letting the side that did not apply apply then. Without it the residual is the one `docs/protocol.md` §24 measures — **0 split trials in 800 over four fault arms, and zero is a measurement over this loopback and this fault model, not a proof.** The honest sentence today is "within a device, never duplicated and never lost at any power-loss point; across the pair, never duplicated and never lost once reconciled, with the un-reconciled window bounded by the ladder", and the second clause has no reconciler behind it yet. **P7-C6 strengthened the FIRST clause and did not touch the second:** the within-device sweep now runs against the shipping millisecond clock, and `tests/fakes/kv_mem.cpp`'s `kv_erase()` refuses once the store is dead (it did not, so a swept "power cut" could still erase checkpoint slots after the device was supposed to be gone — a fault model that refuses writes and permits deletes is not a power cut).
- [x] **DISCHARGED — `sim_gain_restore()`'s TYPED-CLOCK HOLE.** The `elapsed * cap / 3600` term is deleted. Both epochs are wall clocks a player types on the time screen; measured before the fix, 100 rounds of (clock +1 h, reboot) from a fully SPENT ledger manufactured **4,000 whole happiness gain points** against a cap of 40 an hour, and the same restore with no gap manufactured 0. Closed exactly the way `xp_ledger_restore()` was at P6-C4: the restore hands back the snapshot and every point past it is refilled by `gain_refill()` out of seconds the device watched pass. **THE COST IS WRITTEN DOWN IN `game/sim.h` RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** off-time no longer refills the hourly budget, so a player back after an hour away may have no full meal for up to 29 minutes — which is the exact complaint `app/app.cpp` says this restore was added to fix. Decided in writing, as the bullet asked: an unkind hour is recoverable, a stat budget a power cycle refills is not. Three named cases in `tests/test_care.cpp`; the mutation that restores the term fails the first one with **202 checks**.
- [x] **DISCHARGED — A PEBBLE AT `XP_LEVEL_MAX` NOW EARNS ITS ACTIVITY HAPPINESS.** `meter_take()` moved above the `XP_LEVEL_MAX` early return in `xp_add()`. Measured before the move: a level-29 Pebble living a full day was paid **5,500 milli** of happiness and a level-30 Pebble was paid **0** of the same 5,500 owed, because `app_pay_activity()` scales the reward by what the meter spent and the meter was never touched. **The one line changes what the device-wide ledger MEANS** — "XP handed out" rather than "XP that found a home", coherent with why it is device-wide — and the bullet's own condition ("no test pins the current behaviour either way") is met by **two** new cases, not one: `a_pebble_at_the_top_of_the_curve_still_earns_its_activity_happiness` and `the_meter_is_spent_at_the_top_of_the_curve_for_every_metered_source`, the second of which owns the cost source by source (a maxed Pebble now drains a daily budget it used to leave for a Box-mate). Reverting the line fails both by name.
- [x] **DISCHARGED — `gt_mono_ms()` HAS THE STRONGER GUARD, AND THE MUTATION THAT JUSTIFIES IT IS THE HEADLINE.** `tests/fakes/arduino/Arduino.h` and `tests/fakes/arduino/soc/esp32c3/rtc.h` (one symbol each), `tests/fakes/arduino_clocks.cpp`, a `gametime_arduino.o` rule compiled with `-DARDUINO`, and `tests/test_clock_device.cpp` — 4 cases, a new host binary — driving the DEVICE branch across a wake that zeroes uptime while the RTC keeps counting, plus an uncalibrated cooldown that must still expire on its own schedule. **THE MUTATION: divide the RTC by 1,000,000 instead of 1,000. `tools/check.sh` prints `GATE OK`; all four cases fail.** A grep can say the right identifier appears in the right function and can never say the clock behaves — which is exactly what the bullet claimed and is now demonstrated rather than argued. A new gate keeps the fake narrow: exactly one Makefile rule may pass `-Ifakes/arduino`, and the directory holds exactly two files, because a wider fake is a second Arduino core nobody diffs against the real one.
- [x] **DISCHARGED — A DROPPED TICK IS COUNTED, AND BOTH HALVES ARE ASSERTED.** The `owed` arithmetic moved out of `app_loop()` into `hardware/power.cpp`'s `pwr_tick_budget()`, **for a reason and not for tidiness: `app/app.cpp` includes `Arduino.h` and cannot be compiled on the host**, so a counter left there would have been a guard nobody could fail — which is the exact thing the bullet warned about. Two saturating counters (`pwr_tick_stalls()`, `pwr_tick_lost_s()`) on the ENERGIA page beside `pwr_loops_per_s()` and `pwr_slept_ms()`: **how often AND how much**, because "how often" alone cannot tell a hiccup from a device that stops for minutes. `a_gap_wider_than_the_bound_charges_one_second_and_says_it_lost_the_rest` asserts that the counter moved *and* that exactly one second was charged *and* that a whole `PWR_SLEEP_SLICE_MS` is still charged in full (the case the bound was raised for at P6-C3 must not become a stall); `the_tick_budget_survives_the_millis_wrap` is the second case. Three mutations, each failing it by name. **6 B of globals, attributed symbol by symbol in `docs/budget.md` §7.**
- [x] **DISCHARGED — `CooldownTable.act_day` IS CLAMPED AT BOOT, AND (b) IS RECORDED AS DOCUMENTATION.** (a) `act_adopt()` is the one look at the field that came off flash, called from `app_setup()` between `act_begin()` and `god_begin()`, with the repair persisted rather than re-made every boot. `ACT_DAY_MAX_INDEX` is `0xFFFFFFFF / ACT_DAY_S` = 49,710 and the field is a u16 that holds 65,535, so the range between them is a set of days `open_day()`'s `day > t.act_day` can never beat. **MEASURED, both arms in one case:** ten simulated years score **146,000** points from an honest table and **440** from one carrying `act_day = 0xFFFF`, and the poisoned arm asserts the day never rolled once — a control arm, so the clamp is guarding something that can be broken. Deliberately NOT in `validate.cpp`: that validator's subject is a `PebbleInstance` and widening it is the scope creep `game/validate.h` argues against. (b) The environment is sampled once per `logic_tick()` and the slice is charged against that one sample — worst measured care difference over half an hour at `EPOCH0`: **0 milli-points**. No code change; `app/app.cpp` now says so beside `sim_tick(step)`, because both it and `hardware/power.h` described the charge as if it were environment-exact.
- [x] **DISCHARGED AS DOCUMENTATION — THE DEEP-SLEEP TRIPWIRE NOW NAMES ITS SECOND FILE.** The paragraph sits directly under `hardware/power.h`'s `static_assert(!PWR_DEEP_WAKE_BOTH_BUTTONS)`, so whoever trips it opens both files: `app_loop()`'s `const uint32_t ms = millis()` is `esp_timer`, i.e. UPTIME — the clock `gametime.cpp` deliberately moved away from — and it is correct today only because `esp_light_sleep_start()` resynchronises `esp_timer` from the RTC on the way out. A DEEP sleep does not, so the tick scheduler would see a backwards jump, `pwr_tick_budget()` would charge nothing and the idle ladder would restart at ACTIVE for ever. The fix is `gt_mono32()`, which `tests/test_clock_device.cpp` now drives across exactly that wake — **and it is a SECOND edit, not a consequence of the first.**
- [x] **AN UNTESTED LOAD-BEARING GUARD FOUND IN REVIEW AND GIVEN A CASE.** `networking/session.cpp`'s `SS_SESSION` arm reads `t == PT_SESSION_ACCEPT && s.role == SR_INITIATOR`, and deleting the role clause left **all 46 host binaries green**. It is not redundant: one forged, well-formed `SESSION_ACCEPT` drives a RESPONDER out of `SS_SESSION` and makes it adopt the attacker's nonce as `nonce_peer`, which is a `session_derive_seed()` input. The consequence is bounded to DENIAL — `session.h` already states that an off-path device that reads one frame can inject well-formed ones — so it is a coverage gap and not a defect, and it now has `a_responder_refuses_a_session_accept_and_never_adopts_its_nonce`.
- [x] **TWO SMALLER REVIEW FINDINGS RECORDED RATHER THAN SWEPT.** (1) `SessionEnd.detail` has **four** readings, not the three `session.h` claimed: `networking/trade_link.cpp` closes `SE_REJECTED` with the `SessionDetail` `SD_TRADE_REFUSED` at three of its five sites, and the two enums collide (`SD_TRADE_REFUSED` is 22, `VReject` 22 is `VR_BAD_CARE`), so a reader following the old sentence printed "a care value outside range" for a god-taint refusal. Nothing in the product reads `end.detail` today, so it is a trap for a log reader rather than a live defect; the header now names all four. (2) **`networking/session.cpp` DID change in phase 7** — P7-C1's "byte-identical" headline is true of P7-C1 and holds through P7-C3, and **P7-C4 changed it** (+86/-3 lines) to carry the trade as a second `SessionOp`. The load-bearing claim survives and is gate-enforced (every added line greps clean for `esp_now|esp_wifi|WiFi|arduino|#if|espnow|radio|rxring|driver`); the accurate sentence is *the radio chunks did not touch it, the second OPERATION did*, and it is in `docs/decisions.md` because a reader skimming the transport summary would otherwise carry forward something false.
- Commit message: `phase-7: social — ESP-NOW (or BLE per D2) transport behind the §59 seam, consent-gated LINK, lockstep P2P battle, journaled atomic trade, balanced breeding`

### Phase 8 — Creator — size L — goal: PIN, Wi-Fi AP/server, QR, mobile UI, sprite editor, validator, upload

**WHAT PHASE 7 HANDS PHASE 8, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** Written at the P7-C6 exit so none of it
is rediscovered. Five items; the first two are the ones that can stop phase 8 dead.

1. **GLOBALS ARE THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, AND THE PARAGRAPH THAT USED TO BE HERE WAS WRONG
   ABOUT WHY.** It read: *"The lever is BLE - 712,618 B of flash and 23,496 B of globals,
   three times the remaining headroom - and it is blocked on one bench test."* **There was no
   lever.** The `release` variant was `GOD_MODE_ENABLED=0 FEATURE_BLE=0`, so the image the
   release caps police never contained BLE; that 23,496 B is `baseline` minus `no-ble`, a DEV
   build figure quoted against a SHIPPING build's headroom. P8-C0 deleted BLE anyway (the owner
   authorised it without the bench test - `docs/decisions.md`, "D2's LAST CLAUSE, CLOSED ON A
   BET") and **release globals moved by sixteen bytes.**

   **The number was right; the explanation was not.** `release` is at **87.4 %** of
   `GATE_RELEASE_GLOBALS_MAX` with **8,196 B free**, and phase 8 brings up an HTTP server, a
   2 KB raw-body buffer (`CS_BODY_MAX`), a 48 KB page blob in `.rodata` and a sprite staging
   area. `docs/budget.md` §3 forecast **1.5-3.0 KB of globals** for phase 8: it fits, leaving
   5.2-6.7 KB for phases 9 and 10. Tight, not critical. **The 48 KB page blob is `.rodata` and
   costs FLASH** - release is at 79.3 % of a 1.6 MB cap with 331 KB free - so the row to watch
   while writing the creator is globals, and the one thing in phase 8 large enough to matter on
   its own is `CS_BODY_MAX`.

   **WHAT P8-C0 IS ACTUALLY WORTH** is that `baseline` is now **1,281,500 / 56,980**, within
   12,374 B of flash and 176 B of globals of the artefact, where it was 725 KB and 23.7 KB away.
   `tools/check.sh` polices the baseline, so the gate now polices a build shaped like the one
   that ships - which is phases 6 and 7's standing lesson applied to the gate itself.
2. **NEITHER BENCH ITEM WAS RUN AND THE ELEVEN-ITEM LIST IS THE FIRST TASK ON A BOARD.**
   **STILL TRUE AFTER P8-C0, AND MORE SO:** the BLE deletion went ahead on the owner's explicit
   bet that ESP-NOW works, not on evidence that it does. Item 2 (two boards list each other) is
   now the test that decides whether a `git revert` of the P8-C0 commit is needed, and that
   revert restores BLE, `BlePeerInfo`, `RADIO_BLE`, the settle window and the `no-ble` variant
   whole.
   P7-C1 wrote five items, P7-C2/C3 wrote five more, P7-C4/C5 wrote one; they are above, in the
   order to run them, and item 1 is the modem-sleep check. **Everything downstream — the BLE
   deletion, `breed_link.cpp`, the cross-pair trade rendezvous — waits on item 2 (two boards
   list each other).** Phase 8 needs no radio link of its own (its Wi-Fi is an AP, not a peer),
   so it CAN proceed in parallel; what it must not do is spend the headroom BLE is holding on
   the assumption that BLE is about to go.
3. **`breed_link.cpp` AND THE CROSS-PAIR RENDEZVOUS ARE REAL, SCOPED WORK, NOT LOOSE ENDS.**
   Both are written out in the P7-C6 block with their shapes. Neither belongs inside a phase-8
   chunk: the first is a second wire operation with its own protocol types and lossy-arm table,
   the second is a two-journal comparison at the next LINK with the same `peer_id`. If phase 8
   has room, they are a phase 7.5; if not, they go at the head of phase 10's list. **What they
   must not become is something done next to a creator endpoint.**
4. **TWO MATRIX VARIANTS CHANGED MEANING IN PHASE 7 AND A PHASE-8 READER WILL BE MISLED BY
   BOTH.** `no-web` grew **+616,358 flash / +25,824 globals** across the phase, and **none of
   it is a phase-7 feature**: `net.cpp`'s `NT_NET_WANT_WIFI` became
   `(FEATURE_WEB || FEATURE_ESPNOW)`, so the variant now links the whole Wi-Fi driver it used
   to compile out. It has stopped being the "no Wi-Fi at all" build, and **`all-off` is the
   only variant left with no radio in it**. Phase 8 turns `FEATURE_WEB` back on by definition,
   so the row it should watch is `all-off` for the floor and `release` for the cap.
5. **THE FIXTURE LESSON, BECAUSE PHASE 8 WRITES A NEW ONE.** The phase-7 exit's blocking
   defect survived 27,819 checks because `tests/test_trade.cpp` bound
   `save_set_clock(nullptr, ...)`, which switched off a whole branch of `save_manager.cpp` that
   the release artefact runs. A gate now forbids that specific line, and the general rule is
   the one to carry: **a host fixture that differs from `app_setup()`'s own wiring is testing a
   firmware nobody flashes.** `tools/creator_smoke.sh` and `test_creator_api.cpp` should be
   written against the same clocks, the same store and the same rate limiter `app/app.cpp`
   binds — and where they cannot be, the difference belongs in a comment at the top of the
   fixture, not in a diff nobody reads.

On `check.sh`'s `PURE_NET` list; both networking purity gates apply (verified by mutation: a `static uint32_t` added to it fails the gate by name).
- [x] **The persisted half is `gs_creator_load()` / `gs_creator_store()`** in `persistence/game_state.cpp`, driven by `tests/test_game_state.cpp` against the real `save_manager` and the `kv_mem` fake. `gs_load()`'s three lines that zeroed `creator_pin` / `pin_fail_count` / `pin_lock_until` on every boot **are deleted** — they were the P2-era guard for a feature that did not exist, and with them in place a PIN could not survive a reboot at all. The concern they named is answered in the code: a restored fail count leaves the gate armed but **not locked**, so nobody is locked out of a device that prints the PIN on its own screen.
- [x] **THE CLOCK QUESTION, ANSWERED.** The lockout deadline is `gt_mono32()` and lives in **RAM only**; the idle timer is `gt_mono32()` too. `gt_now()` is refused for both, and the reason is the phase-6 cousin: it is uncalibrated by default (an estimate that restarts from `save_last_seen()` after a power cut) **and from P8-C3 the phone sets it** through `POST /api/time` — the unauthenticated party on the far side of this very gate. An uncalibrated clock therefore does **nothing** to the lockout, by construction. A power cut loses the RAM deadline; `pin_fail_count` survives and brings the gate back **armed**, so the next wrong PIN re-locks at once, and the residual concession (one skipped 60 s wait per reboot) is worth nothing to someone who must stand in front of the screen to reboot it. `ConfigV2.pin_lock_until` is written as a **DIAG mirror on the same edge and never read back**; `save_schema.h` says so at the field.
- [x] Only the **armed edge** (0 or `CREATOR_PIN_FAIL_MAX`) reaches flash — an entire attack episode is two writes. Persisting every failure would have handed an unauthenticated client one flash write per guess, which is the one thing a rate limiter cannot stop.

The header is where the HOSTILE-INPUT POSTURE is written down: what is trusted (the compiled tables, and a flash record ONLY after `validate_custom_species()` has seen it), what is not (method, path, `Content-Length`, `Content-Type`, the body bytes, `X-Pin`, the body `pin`, the `Host` header, the epoch), and that **the page's own checks are a courtesy to the user and never a control** - the device cannot tell our page from curl, so it assumes curl.
- [x] **`GET /api/schema` is served from a GENERATED document, `data/creator_schema_json.h`,** emitted by `tools/gen_content.py` from the SAME content object as `creator_schema.h` in the same run - 744 B of `.rodata`, **0 globals**, 4-arg `send_P`. Three things hold T4 and each catches what the others cannot: `gen_content.py --check` fails on a hand edit; `static_assert`s in the emitted header pin its numbers to the compiled constants (so two emitters drifting is a BUILD failure); and `tests/test_creator_api.cpp` reads the document's TEXT and asserts every number in it including all 34 attack rows, which is the half a generator check cannot see.
- [x] **THE BODY CAP, and it is what closes audit §12's "Body limits: none".** Every POST route uses the 4-arg `on()` so `canRaw()` is true and the body arrives in the core's fixed `HTTP_RAW_BUFLEN` (1436 B, heap, freed per request) instead of `readBytesWithTimeout()` growing a `malloc` to the attacker's own `Content-Length`. The rules are a PURE module, `networking/creator_body.{h,cpp}`, so a host binary drives them byte by byte: at `RAW_START` the declared length decides the whole body - `<= CS_BODY_MAX (2048)` collect, `<= CS_BODY_DRAIN_MAX (8192)` **drain then 413**, above that or NEGATIVE **close the socket from inside the hook**. `Content-Length: 0` is **411**, never an empty upload.
- [x] **THREE HOLES THE PLAN DID NOT NAME, ALL CLOSED.** (1) `POST /anything-else` still walked the unbounded reader, because `onNotFound()` is not a handler and `Parsing.cpp:182` needs `_currentHandler` non-null - a catch-all `Uri` is registered LAST with the same hook and `onNotFound()` is gone. (2) A **multipart** `Content-Type` takes the body down `_parseForm()`, which calls the SAME `_ufn` through `upload()` where `_currentRaw` is null and `WebServer::raw()` dereferences it with no check - a null dereference any client could ask for; the hook reads the collected `Content-Type` first, which is why `Content-Type` joined `X-Pin` in `collectHeaders()`. (3) `note_request()` ran before the rate limiter, so a client answered **429** still dropped the renderer to `FPS_LOW` - P8-C2 flagged it and the order is now rate limit, then hint.
- [x] **`networking/creator_parse.{h,cpp}` - a fixed-schema reader, no JSON library, PURE.** Two documents and nothing else. **Length-bounded, never NUL-bounded** (`CP_NUL`, not a terminator - a NUL-bounded reader validates a PREFIX of what arrived); duplicate keys are `CP_DUP_KEY`, never last-wins; **no recursion and no depth counter**, because no value reader can read an object or an array that was not expected there - ten thousand open braces is one `CP_BAD_TYPE` and a constant amount of stack. Seventeen named codes. The name is converted UTF-8 -> Latin-1 here and its character set is asked of `game/validate.h`'s `creator_name_char_ok()`, so the parser and the flash validator share ONE allowed set. `slot` and `budget_used` are NOT in the document: a page-chosen slot is an attacker-chosen slot, and a page that prices its own Pebble prices it at zero.
- [x] **`validate_custom_species()` is the third call site `game/validate.h` has been saying does not exist yet.** Fourteen new `VR_CS_*` codes, appended (never inserted - the values above are named in logs). It is a SECOND ENTRY POINT and not a policy flag because it judges a different OBJECT: a `CustomSpeciesRec` is a species DEFINITION, `validate_pebble()` takes a creature. **§15 is satisfied literally**: the CREATURE the upload becomes goes through `validate_pebble()`, the same one call the wire and the save path make, with no creator branch in it. It is also the FLASH guard - `save_manager.cpp` runs it over every `cs*` record at boot, because "it survived a CRC" is not evidence about a stat total.

**WHAT SHIPPED THAT THE BOX DID NOT ASK FOR, AND WHY.**

1. **THE ATTACK AND TYPE NAMES ARE SERVED FROM `/api/schema`, NOT COPIED INTO THE PAGE
   (+427 B of `.rodata`).** The ATTACKS screen draws 34 attacks; a screen listing "ataque 17" is
   not usable, so the names had to come from somewhere, and 34 Spanish strings typed into
   `app.js` would be exactly the second source of truth `creator_schema_json.h` exists to
   prevent (T4). `gen_content.py` gained `json_ascii_string()` and the document gained `"tn"`
   and `"an"`, emitted as `\uXXXX` escapes so it stays printable ASCII.
   **`CONTENT_VERSION` is unchanged at `0x02B5`** — it hashes the JSON and this is an emitter
   change — so no golden moved. `tests/test_creator_api.cpp` compares every served name against
   `ES[ATTACKS_TABLE[i].name_idx]` **by codepoint**, because one side is UTF-8 and the other is
   JSON escapes and a byte comparison would fail on every accented name while both are right.

2. **THE GENERATOR TIES `CREATOR_API_VERSION` INTO THE PAGE.** `app.js` carries
   `0 /*@CREATOR_API_VERSION@*/`, the generator substitutes `core/version.h`'s value and emits
   `static_assert(INDEX_HTML_API_VERSION == CREATOR_API_VERSION)`. Bumping the version without
   regenerating stops the firmware compiling instead of shipping a page that tells the phone the
   wrong number; deleting the placeholder fails the generator by name.

3. **`tools/page_test.mjs` + `tests/tools/creator_decode.cpp`, AND THEY ARE THE POINT OF THE
   CHUNK'S TESTING.** Nothing in JavaScript and nothing in a host test can see whether the page's
   XBM bit order is the device's. The harness drives THE SHIPPED BLOB (extracted from
   `index_html.h`) in a headless Chromium with real pointer events, answers it with THE SHIPPED
   SCHEMA (extracted from `creator_schema_json.h`), and pipes the body the page POSTs into a
   binary linking THE REAL `creator_parse.cpp` AND `validate.cpp`. 51 assertions, ~3 s, wired
   into `tools/check.sh` with the three-way exit the content gate's convention asks for
   (0 pass / 1 hard fail / 2 skip with a printed word).

4. **TWO NEW GREP GATES.** Every `/api/...` the page calls must be registered under
   `src/networking` — the half a byte-diff structurally cannot see, and a page fetching
   `/api/pebbles` reads to the user as "the creator is broken" while `--check` stays green — and
   the page may not name an off-device host (§33 "works offline after connection").

5. **THE SIGUIENTE BUTTON GATES ON "AT LEAST ONE DAMAGING MOVE" TOO.** The browser test found the
   page would let a user walk to VALIDAR with four support moves and be refused
   `VR_CS_NO_DAMAGING_MOVE`. The gate is named in the code as an affordance — "you have not
   filled this in yet" — never a permission, and every rule it names is also in
   `localProblems()` and on the device.

6. **`BODY / COSMETICS` IS THE STATS SCREEN.** `CustomSpeciesRec` has no cosmetic field other
   than the sprite, so the build is the four base stats and the cosmetics are the next screen.
   Recorded so it is not read as a missing screen.

7. **THE PAGE IS STRICTER THAN THE DEVICE IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE:** it refuses `"` and `\` in a
   name, which `creator_name_char_ok()` accepts, because the device's string reader has no
   escapes and either would come back as `CP_SYNTAX` with no field named. Narrower is the safe
   direction.

**THE MUTATION THAT SURVIVED, STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN.** Replacing the budget bar's integer
arithmetic with `balance.json`'s FLOAT sentence left the first sweep GREEN. The two forms agree
on all 14,837 (S, A) pairs in range — it is an **equivalent mutant in JavaScript**. The check was
replaced with an exhaustive sweep comparing `window.PB.pct` against `game/validate.cpp`'s
`creator_power_pct()` over every pair, which kills a wrong constant, a lost rounding term and a
missing saturation. The integer form stays because a reader diffing the two files should see one
expression, and because the equivalence is a property of T=22, B=185 and IEEE-754 doubles.

**ACCEPTANCE — §67 "Mobile editor works" AND "Sprite editor works" ARE NOT TICKED, AND A GREEN
BROWSER RUN IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR EITHER.** Both are BENCH items. A desktop Chromium at a 390x844
viewport has a mouse with one exact pixel of contact, no palm, no glove, no sunlight, no
one-handed reach and no on-screen keyboard eating half the viewport.
- [ ] §67 **"Mobile editor works"** — NOT OBSERVED. **OWNER:** flash a board, open CREATOR, join
  `PEBBLEBOL-XXXX` from a phone, open `http://192.168.4.1/`, type the PIN shown on the device,
  and walk all nine screens one-handed. Confirm the bottom bar is reachable with a thumb, the
  on-screen keyboard does not hide the name field, and the page still works after the phone
  locks and unlocks.
- [ ] §67 **"Sprite editor works"** — NOT OBSERVED. **OWNER:** on the same phone, draw a
  recognisable shape on both frames, use RELLENO, ESPEJO H and DESHACER, and check that a cell
  can be hit reliably with a thumb (the cells are ~16 CSS px at 390 px wide; every BUTTON is
  44 px, the cells cannot be). Then upload and confirm the Pebble appears in the Box.
- [ ] **THE PAGE ON A REAL SOCKET** — NOT OBSERVED. No HTTP request has been made in this
  environment; the harness's server is node, not `WebServer`. **OWNER:**
  `curl http://192.168.4.1/ | wc -c` (expect 42,245), `curl -H 'X-Pin: NNNN'
  http://192.168.4.1/api/state`, and confirm the page loads over the AP in under two seconds.

**P8-C5 QR screen, smoke script, exit** — S — **LANDED.** Gate green (49/49 host binaries +
PAGE TEST OK 51/51), MATRIX OK on six variants, **+26 flash / +0 globals on every one of them**.
`ui/qr.cpp` untouched, as the box asked.

- [x] **CREATOR screen "ESCANÉAME / [QR] / PIN: NNNN" (§34).** Four lines down the 62 px column
  beside the 62 px symbol: `ESCANÉAME` (`GF_BODY`), the SSID or the address by variant
  (`GF_TINY`, ASCII-only and legally so — both are ASCII by construction), `PIN` and the four
  digits at 9×19 (`GF_BIG`), and `Con el móvil`. §34's "do not clutter this screen with unrelated
  UI" is why **three things were removed**: `STR_CREATOR_PHASE` ("Creador - Fase 8", deleted from
  `strings_es.h` rather than repurposed — the page it said did not exist now does, and it was
  also the tree's one Spanish prose string drawn in the ASCII-only 4x6 face), the "Conéctate a la
  red:" hint, and the always-on IP line that contradicted the symbol every five seconds. The
  five row baselines carry **four `static_asserts`** so the panel is checked by the compiler, not
  only by `tests/golden/screens/creator_portal.pbm`. `web_pin() == 0` ("none issued") draws
  `----` in the body font: `logisoso16_tn` is digits only and a dash in it is a missing glyph.
- [x] **The QR payload alternates and carries NO PIN (§39).** The existing five-second alternation
  is unchanged and `qr.cpp` is untouched; the payload is `WIFI:S:<ssid>;;` or `http://<ip>/` and
  nothing else. The `WPA` half of the box's own sentence is **not** implemented and P8-C2 records
  why in `screen_creator.cpp` at length: the shortest legal WPA2 payload is 40 B → QR version 3 →
  29 modules → 70 px, on a 64-row panel. Reopening it needs a bench call, not a code change.
- [x] **`creator_payload()` and the test the §39 gate could not be.** `tools/check.sh`'s grep for
  a formatted query parameter looks at `src/networking`, which is where `net_url()`'s old `?k=`
  lived and is not where `ui/screen_creator.cpp` is — **a PIN appended in `build()` would have
  shipped with the gate green.** Three host cases now read the encoded bytes back: the two
  payloads by name; **byte-identity at all 9,999 PINs, both symbols** (a substring search would
  false-positive, because `PEBBLEBOL-1234` is an ordinary real SSID); and no `?`, `=` or `&`,
  which is a query parameter of any name. Mutation: appending the PIN with no `?` at all fails
  `creator_payload_carries_no_pin_for_any_pin` at pin 2 and survives the query-parameter check,
  which is exactly why the sweep exists.
- [x] **A test in the suite that could not fail, fixed rather than left.** `creator_payload_carries_no_pin`
  asserted three properties of `CreatorInfo.url` — things the FIXTURE types into that field. It is
  replaced, and the half that was never about the fixture is renamed to
  `creator_join_string_fits_the_version_2_byte_budget` and now measures `creator_payload()`.
- [x] **`tools/creator_smoke.sh`: all 7 routes, 413, 403, the 6th wrong PIN locked, the idle
  timeout** — plus the catch-all, the `CS_BODY_DRAIN_MAX` band (socket closed, no answer), the
  411 an empty or chunked body gets, and the 415 a multipart POST gets **followed by a request
  that must still be answered**, because that path is the one where `WebServer::raw()`
  dereferences a null pointer. The last four are there so this script really does supersede
  P8-C3's owner curl list rather than covering most of it. Written as a script for a BOARD. **It binds nothing** — it speaks HTTP
  to a device that ran `app_setup()`, so the wiring is not reproduced, it is the wiring — and the
  top of the file lists the six things it therefore cannot control and what it does about each.
  `--variant` is REQUIRED and has no default (`docs/budget.md` §8); every constant is read out of
  the header that owns it and a missing macro is exit 2 naming it; phase 1 refuses to proceed
  unless the board's API version, `FW_VERSION`, `CONTENT_VERSION`, advertised `CS_BODY_MAX` and
  served page `Content-Length` all match this tree. There is no `--fake`, no mock and no loopback
  default.
- [x] **Three gates for it in `tools/check.sh`**, all mutation-tested: `--dry-run` must still parse
  the tree; the route set it probes must EQUAL the set `src/networking` registers, **in both
  directions** (a route nobody smoke-tests is as much a finding as a route nothing serves); and it
  may not name a loopback host.

**WHAT THIS CHUNK DID NOT OBSERVE, AND IT IS MOST OF WHAT THE SCRIPT IS FOR.** There is no board,
no access point and no phone in this environment. The script's **bash** was driven end to end
against a throwaway stub in a scratch directory — all four phases, both branches of the idle
assertion, the 413 path, the socket-close path and the full 63 s lockout — which found three real
defects in it (two whitespace-brittle JSON readers, the failure arithmetic below, and a `die()`
inside `$(...)` that exited the subshell and let a missing macro print an error and then exit 0).
**The stub is not committed, is not in the gate, and none of that is evidence about a device.**

- [ ] §67 **"QR connection works"** — NOT OBSERVED. A symbol is only "working" when a phone reads
  it, and nothing here has a camera. **What IS held:** the encoder is validated module-by-module
  against the Python `qrcode` reference by `tests/test_qr.cpp`; the payload is exactly the two
  strings and carries no PIN at any PIN; and the drawn symbol is pinned at the real 128x64 by
  `creator_portal.pbm` at `QR_BOX_SIZE / (25 + 6) = 2` px per module. **OWNER: flash a board, open
  CREATOR, and with a phone (a) scan the alternating "join" symbol and confirm the phone offers to
  join `PEBBLEBOL-XXXX`, (b) scan the other symbol and confirm the browser opens
  `http://192.168.4.1/`, and (c) confirm the PIN on the device screen is readable at arm's length
  and is NOT anywhere in the scanned URL.** This is also where the still-open P8-C2 question —
  whether a 62 px version-2 symbol scans at all at arm's length — is settled.
- [ ] §67 **"PIN required"**, **"Device-side validation works"**, **"Wi-Fi activates only when
  necessary"** and **"Inactivity timeout works"** — NOT OBSERVED, and this chunk is what turns
  their four separate owner curl lists into one command. **OWNER: join the board's AP and run
  `tools/creator_smoke.sh --variant release --pin NNNN`.** Expect `CREATOR SMOKE OK`; it takes
  about eight minutes, most of it the 60 s lockout wait and the 5 min idle wait. **Run it against
  the RELEASE build** — a baseline result is evidence about baseline and nothing else. It creates
  one Pebble called SMOKE and sets the device clock; release the Pebble afterwards.
- Acceptance: §67 "QR connection works" — **left unticked above.** Matrix green on six variants.

  still open on the bench, so cutting it would date a claim nobody has checked.
